zero, two and six. Radial derivative cross terms vanish by angular
orthogonality. Finally,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \partial_\theta(B_{\rm aff}c+Cc^2)&=-\sin\theta(B_{\rm aff}+2C\cos\theta),\\
 \partial_\theta[\sin\theta(b+c\,d)]
            &=c\,b+(2c^2-1)d,\\
 \partial_\theta[\sin^2\theta\,T]&=2\sin\theta\,c\,T .
 \end{aligned}
\]
Substitution into (PG4), using (PG8), proves (PG7), including its
off-diagonal entries.

\subsection*{3. Explicit cap lower bounds of orders six, four and two}

Set \(\alpha_0=23/24\). For \(0\le\theta\le R\le1/2\),
\[
 \alpha_0\theta\le\sin\theta\le\theta,\qquad
 J_0\le R^3/3,\qquad I_0\le R^5/5.
 \tag{PG9}
\]
The lower sine bound follows from
\(\sin\theta\ge\theta-\theta^3/6\).
The scalar block can be completed exactly:
\[
 E_0=\frac{I_0}{4J_0}|B_{\rm aff}+2C I_1/I_0|^2
       +\frac{|C|^2}{J_0}(I_2-I_1^2/I_0).
 \tag{PG10}
\]
In particular the estimate below holds for the actual affine
coefficient \(B_{\rm aff}\); optimization in this identity does not replace it.
The covariance determinant is
\[
 I_0I_2-I_1^2=
 \frac12\int_0^R\int_0^R
 \sin^4\theta\sin^4\phi(\cos\theta-\cos\phi)^2\,d\theta\,d\phi
 \ \ge\ \frac{\alpha_0^{10}R^{14}}{2205}.
 \tag{PG11}
\]
For the inequality, integrate (PG9) between \(\theta\) and \(\phi\)
to obtain
\(|\cos\theta-\cos\phi|\ge
\alpha_0|\theta^2-\phi^2|/2\).
The remaining double integral is exactly
\[
 \int_0^R\int_0^R\theta^4\phi^4(\theta^2-\phi^2)^2\,d\theta\,d\phi
 =2R^{14}\left(\frac1{45}-\frac1{49}\right)
 =\frac{8R^{14}}{2205}.
\]
Combining (PG9)–(PG11) proves
\[
 E_0\ge\frac{\alpha_0^{10}R^6}{147}|C|^2
       =\frac{\alpha_0^{10}}{588}\,r^6\,\frac{|C|^2}{16}.
 \tag{PG12}
\]

For the vector block the two exact squared channels are
\[
 E_1=\frac1{12J_0}\int_0^R\sin^2\theta
 \bigl(|c\,b+(2c^2-1)d|^2+2|b+c\,d|^2\bigr)d\theta .
 \tag{PG13}
\]
For each component and each \(\theta\), the Gram determinant of
the two vectors \((c,\sqrt2)\) and \((2c^2-1,\sqrt2c)\) is
\(2\sin^4\theta\). Completing the square in \(b_\alpha\) yields
the pointwise lower bound
\(2\sin^4\theta|d_\alpha|^2/(c^2+2)\).
This is at least \(2\sin^4\theta|d_\alpha|^2/3\).
Since \(\int_0^R\sin^6\theta\,d\theta\ge\alpha_0^6R^7/7\),
\[
 E_1\ge\frac{\alpha_0^6R^4}{42}|d|^2
       =\frac{\alpha_0^6}{28}\,r^4\,\frac{|d|^2}{24}.
 \tag{PG14}
\]
The tensor block in (PG7) retains both contributions. Its exact
integrand contains \(4c^2+6\ge6\), so
\[
 E_2\ge\frac{\|T\|_{\rm HS}^2I_0}{5J_0}
       \ge\frac{3\alpha_0^4R^2}{25}\|T\|_{\rm HS}^2
       =\frac{9\alpha_0^4}{25}\,r^2
                       \frac{\|T\|_{\rm HS}^2}{12}.
 \tag{PG15}
\]
Define the exact positive constant
\[
 c_6=\frac{(23/24)^{10}}{588}
     =\frac{41426511213649}{37281188007641088}
     >2^{-10}.
 \tag{PG16}
\]
The two other constants in (PG14)–(PG15) exceed \(c_6\).
Since \(r\le1\), (PG3), (PG12), (PG14) and (PG15) imply
\[
 \frac1{v(r)}\int_{d(z,U)\le r}|\nabla P(U)|^2dU
                  \ge c_6r^6\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2
 \quad\text{for every }z\in SU(2).
 \tag{PG17}
\]
To prove the passage to every centre, left multiplication by \(z\)
is a real orthogonal linear map of the four quaternion coordinates.
It preserves the radius-two metric, Haar measure, harmonic degree
and the norm of \(\Pi_2P\). Apply the identity-centred proof to
\(U\mapsto P(zU)\). Its affine coefficients can change, but (PG10)
and (PG13) hold for every value of those coefficients.

\subsection*{4. A unit density with bounded score}

Let \(\rho\) be a positive \(C^1\) density with
\(\int\rho\,dU=1\) and \(|\nabla\log\rho|\le B\), \(B\ge0\),
in precisely the metric (PG4). The geodesic integral of its score
gives \(\rho(U)\ge e^{-Br}\rho(z)\) whenever \(d(U,z)\le r\).
Multiply (PG17) by \(\rho(z)\), and integrate in \(z\).
Fubini and the reverse estimate
\(\rho(z)\le e^{Br}\rho(U)\) give
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 c_6r^6\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2
 &\le\frac1{v(r)}\int|\nabla P(U)|^2
                      \int_{d(z,U)\le r}\rho(z)\,dz\,dU\\
 &\le e^{Br}\int|\nabla P(U)|^2\rho(U)\,dU .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG18}
\]
The cap volume has cancelled exactly. Thus no dimension-three
volume penalty is required.

Choose \(r=2/(B+2)\), and put \(t=B/(B+2)\in[0,1)\).
Then \(r(1+B)=1+t\), \(Br=2t\), and
\(\log(1+t)\ge t/2\), by integrating
\((1+t)^{-1}\ge1/2\) over \([0,t]\). It follows that
\((1+t)^6e^{-2t}\ge1\). We have proved
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \int|\nabla P|^2\rho\,dU\ge
       \eta_6(B)\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2,\qquad
 \eta_6(B)=c_6(1+B)^{-6}.}
 \tag{PG19}
\]
The constant part has zero gradient; arbitrary affine terms are
still present on the left. No orthogonality under \(\rho\) was assumed.
Optimizing (PG18) over \(0<r\le1\) also proves
\[
 \int|\nabla P|^2\rho\,dU\ge\Lambda_6(B)\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2,\qquad
 \Lambda_6(B)=
 \begin{cases}c_6e^{-B},&0\le B\le6,\\
 c_6(6/(eB))^6,&B\ge6.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{PG20}
\]
For a finite vector of such polynomials, summing the proved
inequality component by component proves the same result.

\subsection*{5. The exponent six is necessary}

The full polynomial \(P_*=(1-c)^2=1-2c+c^2\) has
\(\|\Pi_2P_*\|_2^2=1/16\). Its affine term is essential to its
third-order gradient zero at \(I\). For \(B\ge1\), retain
\[
 t(U)=\sqrt{8(1-c(U))},\quad
 f_B(U)=\sqrt{B^{-2}+t(U)^2},\quad
 \rho_B(U)=Z_B^{-1}e^{-Bf_B(U)},\quad
 Z_B=\int e^{-Bf_B(U)}dU .
 \tag{PG21}
\]
These are smooth positive densities because \(f_B\) is the square
root of a strictly positive smooth function. Direct differentiation
in the radius-two metric gives
\[
 |\nabla t|^2=1-t^2/16,\qquad
 |\nabla f_B|^2=
 \frac{t^2(1-t^2/16)}{B^{-2}+t^2}\le1,\qquad
 |\nabla P_*|^2=\frac{t^6}{256}-\frac{t^8}{4096}.
 \tag{PG22}
\]
The first equality is used away from \(I\) and continued in the
second equality; \(t\) itself need not be smooth at \(I\).
Thus \(|\nabla\log\rho_B|\le B\). The exact radial Haar measure is
\[
 dU=\frac{t^2}{4\pi}\sqrt{1-t^2/16}\,dt,\quad0\le t\le4,\qquad
 I_k=\int_0^\infty u^k e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}\,du .
 \tag{PG23}
\]
This follows from \(t=4\sin(\theta/2)\) and (PG4).
Substituting \(u=Bt\), the denominator \(4\pi B^3Z_B\) is
\(\int_0^{4B}u^2\sqrt{1-u^2/(16B^2)}
e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}du\), which tends to \(I_2>0\).
For the gradient numerator the same substitution and (PG22) give
\[
 B^6\int|\nabla P_*|^2\rho_BdU
 =\frac{\displaystyle\int_0^{4B}
  \left(\frac{u^8}{256}-\frac{u^{10}}{4096B^2}\right)
       \sqrt{1-\frac{u^2}{16B^2}}e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}du}
 {\displaystyle\int_0^{4B}u^2
       \sqrt{1-\frac{u^2}{16B^2}}e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}du}
 \longrightarrow \frac{I_8}{256I_2}>0 .
 \tag{PG24}
\]
Dominated convergence is justified by \(u^k e^{-u}\), for each
displayed power, after extending each integrand by zero past \(4B\).
The numerator can equivalently be written as
\(u^8(1-u^2/(16B^2))^{3/2}e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}/256\).
Consequently no positive constant times \((1+B)^{-k}\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2\)
can hold uniformly for \(k<6\). This proves optimality of the
exponent, not optimality of the numerical constant (PG16).

\subsection*{6. Complete original lattice and score receiving map}

Let \(m=2L\), \(n=m+1\), \(h=\pi/(2n)\), with \(L\ge2\).
Vertices have indices \(0\le i,k,l\le m\) and physical positions
\(o+a(i-L,k-L,l-L)\). All positively oriented nearest-neighbour
edges and every elementary face of the open cube are retained.
The spanning tree consists of every direction-one edge, every
direction-two edge at first coordinate zero and every direction-three
edge at first two coordinates zero. Root paths run in directions
three, two, one from \((0,0,0)\). With ordered path transport \(t_s\),
the exact rooted word on a chord \(e=(s,t)\) is \(Z_e=t_sU_et_t^{-1}\).
The two chord sets and every original row coefficient are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal C_2=\{(i,k,l;2):1\le i\le m,\ 0\le k<m,\ 0\le l\le m\},\\
 \mathcal C_3=\{(i,k,l;3):0\le i,k\le m,\ (i,k)\ne(0,0),\
                                      0\le l<m\},\\
 v_0(t)=n^{-1/2},\quad
 v_1(t)=\sqrt{2/n}\cos\frac{\pi(t+1/2)}n,\quad
 w_1(t)=-\sqrt{2/n}\sin\frac{\pi(t+1)}n,\\
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}=-v_1(i)w_1(k)v_0(l)/\sqrt2,\quad
 a_{(i,k,l;3)}=0,\\
 b_{(i,k,l;2)}=v_0(i)w_1(k)v_1(l)/\sqrt2,\quad
 b_{(i,k,l;3)}=-v_0(i)v_1(k)w_1(l)/\sqrt2,\\
 u_1=\sum_{e\in\mathcal C_2\cup\mathcal C_3}a_eq(Z_e),\quad
 u_3=\sum_{e\in\mathcal C_2\cup\mathcal C_3}b_eq(Z_e),\\
 S=u_1\cdot u_3,\quad S_{\mathcal R}=S\circ\mathcal R,\quad
 \sigma_*=\sqrt8\sin h,\quad\beta_0=\frac{\sigma_*}{2g^2},\quad
 F=p-r=\beta_0(S-S_{\mathcal R}).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG25}
\]
The symbol \(\mathcal R\) is the original direction-one graph
reflection, including its reflected root and every inverse edge
orientation. The polynomial coefficient \(B_{\rm aff}\) of Section 1 is
distinct from the score bound \(B\) and all boundary link indices.

The complete Hamiltonian and actual positive unit vacuum are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 H=\kappa H_0+b_{\rm mag}\sum_{\text{all faces }p}
                       (2-\operatorname{tr}U_p),\quad
 H_0=-\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}^2,\quad H\psi=E_0\psi,\quad
 d\mu=\psi^2dU,\\
 \kappa=\frac{2g^2}{a},\quad b_{\rm mag}=\frac1{2g^2a},\quad
 \xi=\frac1{4g^4},\quad
 C_F=\langle F\psi,(H-E_0)F\psi\rangle
                      =\kappa\mu(|\nabla F|^2),\quad G=\mu(F^2).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG26}
\]
The constant \(2b_{\rm mag}M\), \(M=3m^2(m+1)\), remains in the
Hamiltonian and vacuum equation. The displayed energy identity follows
by expanding \(H(F\psi)\), substituting \(H\psi=E_0\psi\), and
integrating the two derivative cross terms by parts on the compact
physical-link product.

The exact score statement read in the retained original TeX is
\[
 |\nabla_e\log\psi^2|\le4r_e\xi ,
 \tag{PG27}
\]
where \(r_e\) is the actual number of faces incident to the physical
edge. Its proof, including every mixed derivative, is in
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/sources/spatial/spatial_continuum.tex#L1415}{equations (77)–(79) of the original TeX}
and is repeated here to specify precisely what is used.
Writing \(u=\log\psi\), the vacuum equation implies
\(\Delta u+|\nabla u|^2=(V-E_0)/\kappa\).
The product Bochner formula gives
\[
 (\Delta+2\nabla u\cdot\nabla)|\nabla_eu|^2
 =2\sum_f|\nabla_f\nabla_eu|_{\rm HS}^2+
       |\nabla_eu|^2+2\xi\langle\nabla_eu,\nabla_eW\rangle ,
 \qquad W=\sum_p(2-\operatorname{tr}U_p).
\]
The coefficient one is twice the Ricci curvature \(1/2\) in the
radius-two metric. An incident face word is \(AUB\) or \(AU^{-1}B\);
bi-invariance reduces its trace gradient to that of \(2c\), whose
norm is at most one. Hence \(|\nabla_eW|\le r_e\). At a maximum
of \(|\nabla_eu|^2\), the Bochner identity gives
\(0\ge|\nabla_eu|^2-2\xi r_e|\nabla_eu|\).
This proves (PG27).

The tree-coordinate map sends physical product Haar to product Haar
in tree coordinates and all chords: solve successively
\(U_{(s,t)}=t_s^{-1}t_t\) for tree edges, and
\(U_e=t_s^{-1}Z_et_t\) for chords, using left and right Haar
invariance at each step. Gauge invariance of the positive vacuum
follows from uniqueness of the ground state and its positivity, so its
tree-coordinate density is independent of tree coordinates.
For a fixed chord and fixed tree coordinates, varying \(U_e\) varies
only \(Z_e=t_sU_et_t^{-1}\). Conjugation by \(t_s\) rotates the
three left derivative components orthogonally. Consequently the
physical squared derivative norm equals the chord squared derivative
norm, and the actual one-chord conditional score is bounded by
(PG27). The denominator in a conditional density is constant in that
chord variable. Integrating the density over other chords also preserves
the score bound: differentiate the integral and apply
\(|\nabla\rho|\le B\rho\). These facts justify both conditional and
marginal uses below without asserting independence.

\subsection*{7. Exact quadratic harmonic at every direction-two bulk chord}

Take
\[
 e=(i,k,l;2),\quad1\le i\le m-1,\quad0\le k<m,\quad0\le l\le m,
 \qquad e_\partial(e)=(m,k,l;2).
 \tag{PG28}
\]
Write \(K=Z_e\), \(H_\partial=Z_{e_\partial(e)}\). Boundary links
are denoted by \(e_\partial\), while \(b_e\) always denotes the
original row coefficient in (PG25).

For completeness define the ordered boundary comb
\[
 A_{k,l}=
 \left(\prod_{t=0}^{l-1}Z_{(m,0,t;3)}\right)
 \left(\prod_{s=0}^{k-1}Z_{(m,s,l;2)}\right),\qquad A_{0,0}=I.
 \tag{PG29}
\]
Every product is in increasing displayed order, and an empty product
is identity. After evaluating the original root paths in tree gauge,
their reflected transports are \(A_{k,l}\), independently of the first
coordinate. Direction-one paths have identity transport in that gauge.
The unique reflected source containing the target chord \(K\) is
\((m-i,k,l;2)\), with the same edge orientation. Since
\(A_{k+1,l}=A_{k,l}H_\partial\), its entire reflected word is
\[
 Z^{\mathcal R}_{(m-i,k,l;2)}
       =A_{k,l}K H_\partial^{-1}A_{k,l}^{-1}.
 \tag{PG30}
\]
No other reflected target is \(K\), and \(K\) occurs in no boundary
comb because \(i<m\). The comb \(A_{k,l}\) is independent of
\(H_\partial\) as well: its direction-two factors end at \(k-1\).
The adjoint action of \(A_{k,l}\) on \(q\) is orthogonal, since
\(-2\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha T_\beta)=\delta_{\alpha\beta}\).
It preserves both \(c\) and \(|q|^2\).

Let \(U_1,U_3\) be the complete original row sums excluding \(e\),
and \(V_1,V_3\) the complete reflected row sums excluding
\((m-i,k,l;2)\), respectively. All four depend on the exterior and
are independent of \(K\). Put \(Q=q(K)\),
\(Q'=\operatorname{Ad}_{A_{k,l}}q(KH_\partial^{-1})\).
The exact coefficient reflection is
\(a_{(m-i,k,l;2)}=-a_e\), \(b_{(m-i,k,l;2)}=b_e\), by
\(v_1(m-i)=-v_1(i)\). The full expansions, including constants, are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 S={}&U_1\cdot U_3+(b_eU_1+a_eU_3)\cdot Q
                              +a_eb_e|Q|^2,\\
 S_{\mathcal R}={}&V_1\cdot V_3+
                    (b_eV_1-a_eV_3)\cdot Q'-a_eb_e|Q'|^2,\\
 F={}&\beta_0\bigl[U_1\cdot U_3-V_1\cdot V_3
       +(b_eU_1+a_eU_3)\cdot Q
       -(b_eV_1-a_eV_3)\cdot Q'
       +a_eb_e(|Q|^2+|Q'|^2)\bigr].
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG31}
\]
Thus, for each exterior, \(F\) is in the complete space treated by
(PG19). Its remainder is affine in all four quaternion coordinates
of \(K\). Derivatives in the physical chord \(e\) differentiate
exactly this polynomial through the isometry already proved.

Set \(\chi_1(U)=4c(U)^2-1\), a degree-two harmonic of Haar squared
norm one. Since \(|q(U)|^2=3-\chi_1(U)\),
\[
 \Pi_2^{(e)}F=-\beta_0a_eb_e
          [\chi_1(K)+\chi_1(KH_\partial^{-1})],\qquad
 \|\Pi_2^{(e)}F\|_{L^2(dK)}^2
 =\frac43\beta_0^2a_e^2b_e^2[1+2c(H_\partial)^2].
 \tag{PG32}
\]
Indeed, multiplication by \(H_\partial^{-1}\) is an orthogonal
linear map of quaternion coordinates. If its scalar coordinate is
\(\ell\cdot z\), then \(|\ell|=1\) and
\(\ell\cdot(1,0,0,0)=c(H_\partial)\).
The fourth-moment identity preceding (PG4) gives
\(\int z_0^2(\ell\cdot z)^2=(1+2c(H_\partial)^2)/24\).
Expanding the two factors \(4z_0^2-1\) and
\(4(\ell\cdot z)^2-1\) yields their inner product
\((4c(H_\partial)^2-1)/3\).
Adding their two unit squared norms proves (PG32), with every
constant term of the original polynomial still retained in (PG31).

\subsection*{8. Distinct physical energies and all boundary face counts}

For (PG28), the two direction-\((1,2)\) faces always exist, because
\(0<i<m\). Direction-\((2,3)\) faces exist below and above exactly
when \(l>0\) and \(l<m\), respectively. For \(e_\partial(e)\)
only one direction-\((1,2)\) face exists. Therefore
\[
 r_e=2+\mathbf1_{l>0}+\mathbf1_{l<m},\qquad
 r_{e_\partial(e)}=1+\mathbf1_{l>0}+\mathbf1_{l<m},\qquad
 B_l=\begin{cases}12\xi,&l=0,m,\\16\xi,&0<l<m.\end{cases}
 \tag{PG33}
\]
There is no extra \(k\)-face penalty: the edge points in direction
two, so only the two transverse coordinates determine incidence.

Apply (PG19) to the actual \(K\)-conditional density and full
polynomial (PG31), then integrate the exterior. Its \(\Pi_2\) norm
is exactly (PG32). Since \(H_\partial\) is a different chord, the
exterior marginal of its squared scalar coordinate is precisely
the full \(\mu\) marginal. Distinct \(e\)'s are distinct physical
derivative summands in (PG26); summing positive quantities requires
no independence. Hence
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 C_F\ge\frac{4\kappa\beta_0^2}{3}
 \sum_{\substack{1\le i\le m-1\\0\le k<m\\0\le l\le m}}
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}^2b_{(i,k,l;2)}^2\,\eta_6(B_l)
     [1+2\mu(c(Z_{(m,k,l;2)})^2)].}
 \tag{PG34}
\]
This relates the original energy directly to its actual conditional
quadratic harmonic data. Every other physical derivative remains a
nonnegative summand of the original energy. Replacing \(\eta_6\)
by \(\Lambda_6\) throughout (PG34) is also valid, by (PG20).

The exact coefficient and row sums are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}^2b_{(i,k,l;2)}^2
 =\frac4{n^6}\cos^2\frac{\pi(i+1/2)}n
       \cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n\sin^4\frac{\pi(k+1)}n,\\
 J_n=\frac n2-2\cos^2h,\qquad
 \sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\cos^2\frac{\pi(i+1/2)}n=J_n,\qquad
 \sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sin^4\frac{\pi(k+1)}n=\frac{3n}{8},\\
 \sum_{l\in\{0,m\}}\cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n=2\cos^2h,\qquad
 \sum_{l=1}^{m-1}\cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n=J_n .
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG35}
\]
For the cosine sums, expand \(\cos^2x=(1+\cos2x)/2\) and sum
the complete \(n\)-term geometric progression; its oscillatory sum
is zero. Remove exactly the two endpoint terms \(\cos^2h\).
For the sine sum, use
\(\sin^4x=(3-4\cos2x+\cos4x)/8\).
On the nonzero residues \(1,\ldots,n-1\), each of the two cosine
sums is \(-1\). This uses \(n\ge5\), so neither frequency is zero
modulo \(n\). The result is
\([3(n-1)+4-1]/8=3n/8\).
The original family includes \(2m(m-1)\) links with \(r_e=3\)
and \(m(m-1)^2\) links with \(r_e=4\); their sum is
\(m(m-1)(m+1)\), exactly the cardinality of (PG28).

Using only \(1+2\mu c^2\ge1\), (PG34) becomes the fully evaluated
bound
\[
 C_F\ge\frac{2\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{n^5}
       \left[2\cos^2h\,\eta_6(12\xi)+J_n\eta_6(16\xi)\right].
 \tag{PG36}
\]
Before this last inequality, the exact receiving expression with its
remaining boundary expectations is
\[
 \frac{16\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{3n^6}
 \sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sin^4\frac{\pi(k+1)}n
 \sum_{l=0}^{m}\cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n\,\eta_6(B_l)
             [1+2\mu(c(Z_{(m,k,l;2)})^2)] .
 \tag{PG37}
\]
It is the right side of (PG34), not an assumed independent product.

\subsection*{9. A further calculation of the boundary-coordinate contribution}

The unresolved expectations in (PG37) can themselves be bounded
polynomially from the proved score estimate. For any centre \(z\),
radial symmetry and quaternion multiplication give exactly
\[
 \frac1{v(r)}\int_{d(z,U)\le r}c(U)^2dU
 =c(z)^2t_r+\frac{1-c(z)^2}{3}(1-t_r),\qquad
 t_r=\frac{\int_0^{r/2}\sin^2\theta\cos^2\theta\,d\theta}
            {\int_0^{r/2}\sin^2\theta\,d\theta}.
 \tag{PG38}
\]
For \(r\le1\), \(t_r\ge\cos^2(1/2)>1/4\).
Thus its minimum over \(z\) is \((1-t_r)/3\). By (PG9),
\[
 \frac{1-t_r}{3}
 =\frac{I_0}{3J_0}
 \ge\frac{\alpha_0^4r^2}{20}.
 \tag{PG39}
\]
Averaging centres exactly as in (PG18) proves
\(\int c^2\rho\ge\alpha_0^4 e^{-Br}r^2/20\).
For \(B\le2\), choose \(r=1\); for \(B\ge2\), choose \(r=2/B\).
In the first range, the minimum of
\(e^{-B}(1+B)^2\) over \([0,2]\) is one, as differentiation
shows. In the second range \((1+B)/B\ge1\).
Consequently both ranges give
\[
 \int c^2\rho\,dU\ge\frac{\eta_q(B)}4,\qquad
 \eta_q(B)=c_q(1+B)^{-2},\qquad
 c_q=\frac4{5e^2}(23/24)^4 .
 \tag{PG40}
\]
For the first range the chosen constant is valid because
\(4/e^2<1\). This proof explicitly controls the original scalar
coordinate, not a colour-vector replacement.

The actual boundary marginal has score \(8\xi\) at \(l=0,m\)
and \(12\xi\) at \(0<l<m\), by (PG27), (PG33) and the marginal
score argument following (PG27). Inserting (PG40) into (PG37),
then evaluating the same exact row sums, gives the strengthening
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 C_F\ge {\cal C}(L,a,g):=
 \frac{2\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{n^5}
 \left[
 2\cos^2h\,\eta_6(12\xi)\left(1+\frac{\eta_q(8\xi)}2\right)
 +J_n\eta_6(16\xi)\left(1+\frac{\eta_q(12\xi)}2\right)
 \right].}
 \tag{PG41}
\]
This retains both boundary classes and every displayed positive
contribution. The version with \(\Lambda_6\) in place of \(\eta_6\)
is also proved. The remaining physical derivatives and the stronger
actual expectations (PG37) are additional nonnegative contributions,
not assumed equalities in (PG41).

\subsection*{10. The unchanged original joint path and the endpoint}

Use the original path, with integer \(j\ge2\):
\[
 L_j=j^4,\quad a_j=\frac1{100j},\quad
 g_j^2=\frac{\kappa_*}{200j},\quad s_j=\frac{\kappa_*}{j},\quad
 n_j=2j^4+1,\quad
 \kappa_j=\kappa_*,\quad \xi_j=\frac{10000j^2}{\kappa_*^2},
 \quad\kappa_*>0.
 \tag{PG42}
\]
In particular
\(j^3\beta_{0,j}\to50\sqrt2\pi/\kappa_*\),
\(J_{n_j}/n_j\to1/2\) and \(n_j/j^4\to2\).
Let \({\cal C}_{\partial,j}\) and \({\cal C}_{{\rm int},j}\)
be respectively the first and second summands on the right of
(PG41), including their respective \(\eta_q\) factors. Direct
substitution into their full formulas gives the positive limits
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 j^{34}{\cal C}_{\partial,j}
 &\longrightarrow
 \frac{625\pi^2}{\kappa_*}\,c_6
               \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{120000}\right)^6,\\
 j^{30}{\cal C}_{{\rm int},j}
 &\longrightarrow
 \frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*}\,c_6
               \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{160000}\right)^6,\\
 j^{30}{\cal C}(L_j,a_j,g_j)
 &\longrightarrow
 \frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*}\,c_6
               \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{160000}\right)^6.
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG43}
\]
For example, the interior prefactor
\(2\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2J_{n_j}^2/n_j^5\) times \(j^{18}\)
tends to \(625\pi^2/(2\kappa_*)\), while
\(j^{12}\eta_6(16\xi_j)\to
c_6(\kappa_*^2/160000)^6\).
Both \(\eta_q\) factors tend to zero. This proves every limit
in (PG43) from the original coefficients, without selecting a
different coupling. For the \(\Lambda_6\) version, replace the
factor \(c_6\) in both limits by \(c_6(6/e)^6\).

The previous single-link energy estimate (PC49), proved in
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_CONDITIONAL_BOUNDS.md}{the complete retained PC note},
was of order \(j^{-46}\). The complete gradient inequality and
the exact bulk sum improve this to the proved lower bound
\(C_{F,j}\ge{\cal C}(L_j,a_j,g_j)\) with order \(j^{-30}\).
These are asymptotics of the stated bounds, not equalities for the
actual energy.

Finally preserve the original moments
\[
 Q_4=\mu(S^2),\quad Q_4^{\mathcal R}=\mu(SS_{\mathcal R}),\quad
 K_1=\mu(|\nabla S|^2),\quad
 K_{\mathcal R}=\mu(\nabla S\cdot\nabla S_{\mathcal R}).
 \tag{PG44}
\]
Reflection invariance of the actual vacuum and kinetic form gives
\(G=2\beta_0^2(Q_4-Q_4^{\mathcal R})\) and
\(C_F=2\kappa\beta_0^2(K_1-K_{\mathcal R})\), by expanding
the complete original \(F\) of (PG25). Therefore the requested
raw expression satisfies the exact receiving inequality
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 {\cal E}_j(s_j)
 &:=
 2\beta_{0,j}^2(Q_{4,j}-Q_{4,j}^{\mathcal R})
 -\frac{2\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2}{s_j}
                         (K_{1,j}-K_{\mathcal R,j})\\
 &=G_j-\frac{C_{F,j}}{s_j}
 \ \le\ G_j-\frac{j}{\kappa_*}{\cal C}(L_j,a_j,g_j).
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG45}
\]
The subtracted positive term in the last line has order \(j^{-29}\)
and exact leading coefficient
\[
 \frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*^2}\,c_6
                 \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{160000}\right)^6.
 \tag{PG46}
\]
A separate lower bound on \(G_j\) cannot be inserted into this upper
bound to infer its sign. Neither (PG34) nor (PG41) determines a
quotient \(C_F/G\), and neither identifies spectral mass in
\((0,s_j]\). What is proved is a uniform complete-polynomial
gradient inequality with optimal exponent, its exact original-word
receiving map, the entire bulk coefficient sum, a further bound on
its boundary-coordinate remainder, and the endpoint inequality
(PG45). The upper control or exact joint-path evaluation of \(G_j\)
and the distribution of the actual spectral measure remain unresolved.

The source-reading ledger records the original TeX read and the
existing disk-index routing used before the new derivation. No
unread source inequality or claim of external novelty enters this proof.

\subsection*{11. Preserve the density floor and its full Casimir factor}

The preceding \(\eta_6\) estimates remain valid. A further improvement
keeps the full Haar Dirichlet energy in the density-floor branch.
For a unit density with score bound \(B\), the diameter \(2\pi\)
gives \(\rho\ge e^{-2\pi B}\). Indeed its maximum is at least one,
and the logarithmic oscillation is at most \(2\pi B\).
For the complete polynomial of (PG2), the exact Haar energy is
\[
 \int|\nabla P|^2dU
   =\frac34\|\Pi_1P\|_2^2+2\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2 .
 \tag{PG47}
\]
To verify the two eigenvalues, the Euclidean polar-coordinate
Laplacian in dimension four sends \(r^kY(\omega)\) to
\(r^{k-2}[k(k+2)Y+\Delta_{S^3}Y]\). Harmonicity gives the
radius-one eigenvalue \(k(k+2)\); the original radius-two metric
divides it by four. Integration by parts and harmonic
orthogonality give (PG47), including the affine contribution.
Combining it with (PG20) proves the stronger complete-polynomial
inequality
\[
 \boxed{\int|\nabla P|^2\rho\,dU
        \ge\Psi_6(B)\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2,\qquad
 \Psi_6(B)=\max\{2e^{-2\pi B},\Lambda_6(B)\}.}
 \tag{PG48}
\]
In particular \(\Psi_6(0)=2\). The factor two in its first branch
cannot be replaced by one when recording the full available bound.
Since \(\Lambda_6\ge\eta_6\), all of (PG34), (PG36), (PG37),
(PG41) and (PG45) hold with \(\eta_6\) replaced by \(\Psi_6\),
and the original displayed versions are preserved.

For clarity, the full evaluations with the original \(\eta_q\)
retained are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 C_F&\ge\frac{4\kappa\beta_0^2}{3}
       \sum_ea_e^2b_e^2\Psi_6(B_l)
                       [1+2\mu(c(H_\partial)^2)]\\
 &\ge\frac{2\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{n^5}
 \left[
 2\cos^2h\,\Psi_6(12\xi)(1+\tfrac12\eta_q(8\xi))
 +J_n\Psi_6(16\xi)(1+\tfrac12\eta_q(12\xi))
 \right].
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG49}
\]
The sum is over exactly (PG28), with \(H_\partial=Z_{(m,k,l;2)}\)
and the exact face classes of (PG33). Thus the stronger factor is
propagated through the actual harmonic-data sum as well as its
fully evaluated version.

\subsection*{12. A stronger pure-linear marginal bound, with its proof}

The following constant and optimized factor were independently
proved in
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_SCORE_AND_ORIGINAL_ENDPOINT.md}{the complete PS derivation, PS17–PS18}.
The complete argument used here is repeated to keep every comparison
explicit. For a physical cap of radius \(r\le1\), put
\(\delta=1-\cos(r/2)\) and \(u=1-\cos\theta\). Its exact radial
probability measure is
\[
 d\nu_\delta(u)=
 \frac{\sqrt{u(2-u)}\,du}{\int_0^\delta\sqrt{v(2-v)}\,dv}
 \ge\frac{15}{16}\frac{3u^{1/2}}{2\delta^{3/2}}\,du,
 \qquad
 \frac{47r^2}{384}\le\delta\le\frac{r^2}{8}\le\frac18 .
 \tag{PG50}
\]
The cosine bounds follow from its alternating series. For the
measure inequality, bound the numerator factor \(\sqrt{2-u}\)
below by \(\sqrt{2-\delta}\), and its denominator factor above
by \(\sqrt2\). Their ratio is at least
\(\sqrt{15}/4\ge15/16\); integrating \(u^{1/2}\) gives the
remaining coefficient.

For a pure linear quaternion function \(\ell(z)=a\cdot z\), the
cap second-moment eigenvalues are \(\operatorname{Av}\cos^2\theta\)
in the central direction and \(\operatorname{Av}\sin^2\theta/3\)
in its orthogonal complement. The latter is smaller for
\(\theta\le1/2\), because \(\cos\theta\ge7/8\).
Equation (PG50) gives \(\operatorname{Av}u\ge9\delta/16\),
and \(u(2-u)\ge15u/8\). Since the full Haar norm is
\(|a|^2/4\), the cap average is at least
\[
 \operatorname{Av}|\ell|^2
 \ge C_1r^2\|\ell\|_2^2,\qquad C_1=\frac{705}{4096}.
 \tag{PG51}
\]
The exact product is
\((4/3)(15/8)(9/16)(47/384)=705/4096\).
The argument is valid for complex coefficients by the Hermitian
second-moment form.

Define \(r_k(B)=1\) for \(B\le k\), and \(r_k(B)=k/B\)
for \(B>k\). The cap-centre averaging proof (PG18), applied
to \(|\ell|^2\), and the Haar density floor give
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \Theta_1(B)=\max\{e^{-2\pi B},
            C_1e^{-B r_2(B)}r_2(B)^2\},\\
 \int|\ell|^2\rho\,dU\ge\Theta_1(B)\|\ell\|_2^2,\qquad
 \mu(c(H_\partial)^2)\ge\tfrac14\Theta_1(B_\partial),\qquad
 \mu(|q(H_\partial)|^2)\ge3\Theta_1(B_\partial).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG52}
\]
The last two assertions apply the pure-linear result to the scalar
coordinate and to each of the three coordinates \(2x_\alpha\);
they use the actual boundary marginal and its score
\(B_\partial=8\xi\) at \(l=0,m\), \(12\xi\) otherwise.
They are not assertions about an arbitrary affine polynomial.
The new factor is at least \(\eta_q\): the exact inequality
\(C_1\ge\alpha_0^4/5\) and optimization over \(r\le1\)
dominate the cap bound used in (PG39)–(PG40).

\subsection*{13. The complete even polynomial and both evaluated bulk sums}

Define \(D=S+S_{\mathcal R}\), with the same full original rows.
Adding the first two lines of (PG31), rather than subtracting
them, gives its exact conditional polynomial
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 D={}&U_1\cdot U_3+V_1\cdot V_3
 +(b_eU_1+a_eU_3)\cdot Q
 +(b_eV_1-a_eV_3)\cdot Q'\\
 &\hspace{20mm}+a_eb_e(|Q|^2-|Q'|^2),\\
 \Pi_2^{(e)}D={}&-a_eb_e[\chi_1(K)-\chi_1(KH_\partial^{-1})],\\
 \|\Pi_2^{(e)}D\|_{L^2(dK)}^2
 ={}&a_e^2b_e^2\left[2-\frac23(4c(H_\partial)^2-1)\right]
   =\frac23a_e^2b_e^2|q(H_\partial)|^2 .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG53}
\]
The fourth-moment calculation in (PG32) proves the last two
equalities. All constants and affine terms remain in the first
two lines. Applying (PG48) to this complete polynomial at each
bulk chord, then integrating its actual exterior, gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eD|^2\right)
 &\ge\frac23\sum_ea_e^2b_e^2\Psi_6(B_l)
                            \mu(|q(H_\partial)|^2),\\
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eF|^2\right)
 &\ge\frac{4\beta_0^2}{3}
       \sum_ea_e^2b_e^2\Psi_6(B_l)
                            [1+2\mu(c(H_\partial)^2)] .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG54}
\]
Using (PG52) and every coefficient sum in (PG35) evaluates these
as follows:
\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 A_D={}&\frac{3J_n}{n^5}
   [2\cos^2h\,\Psi_6(12\xi)\Theta_1(8\xi)
                 +J_n\Psi_6(16\xi)\Theta_1(12\xi)],\\
 A_F={}&\frac{2J_n}{n^5}
   [2\cos^2h\,\Psi_6(12\xi)(1+\tfrac12\Theta_1(8\xi))
                 +J_n\Psi_6(16\xi)(1+\tfrac12\Theta_1(12\xi))],\\
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eD|^2\right)&\ge A_D,
 \qquad
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eF|^2\right)
                                                    \ge\beta_0^2A_F .
 \end{aligned}}\tag{PG55}
\]
For the even prefactor, \((2/3)\mu|q|^2\ge2\Theta_1\);
multiplying the coefficient sum \(3J_n/(2n^5)\) yields
\(3J_n/n^5\). For the odd prefactor the corresponding product
is \((4/3)\,3J_n/(2n^5)=2J_n/n^5\).
This proves the constants without a comparison of independent
mass and energy lower bounds.

\subsection*{14. Add the disjoint boundary derivatives with their complete vector bound}

The separately proved boundary estimate is
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_SCORE_AND_ORIGINAL_ENDPOINT.md}{PS19–PS26 and PS40–PS59}.
Its particular vector argument and exact original receiving map are
repeated here. For the complete vector polynomial
\[
 {\cal P}_\alpha(U)=a\,c(U)q_\alpha(U)+R_\alpha(U),
 \qquad\alpha=1,2,3,
 \tag{PG56}
\]
each \(R_\alpha\) is an arbitrary affine function of all four
quaternion coordinates. For a unit cap centre \(z\in S^3\), set
\(M_\alpha=e_0e_\alpha^{\mathsf T}+e_\alpha e_0^{\mathsf T}\)
and \(b_\alpha=2(I-zz^{\mathsf T})M_\alpha z\).
Writing \(x=t z+\sqrt{1-t^2}\,\omega\), the angular vector
block of the full polynomial is
\(\sqrt{1-t^2}(v_\alpha+a\,t b_\alpha)\cdot\omega\).
Its other scalar and tensor blocks retain their nonnegative
squared norms by angular orthogonality. Put \(u=1-t\).
In the vector block,
\((1-t^2)/3\ge5u/8\). Using (PG50), the radial matrix for
the free coefficient and the fixed slope, after scaling \(u/\delta\),
has the exact entries \(3/5,3/7,1/3\). Its Schur complement is
\[
 \frac13-\frac{(3/7)^2}{3/5}=\frac4{147}.
 \tag{PG57}
\]
Thus completing the square gives the cap lower bound
\((15/16)(5/8)(4/147)\delta^3|a|^2|b_\alpha|^2
=(25/1568)\delta^3|a|^2|b_\alpha|^2\).
For a spatial rotation making \(z=(c,s,0,0)\), the three cross
vectors have squared lengths
\(4(c^2-s^2)^2,4c^2,4c^2\). Therefore
\[
 \sum_\alpha|b_\alpha|^2
 =3+16(s^2-\tfrac34)^2\ge3,\qquad
 C_6^{\rm vec}=\frac{75}{784}\left(\frac{47}{384}\right)^3 .
 \tag{PG58}
\]
The same identity holds before rotation, since rotating the three
spatial axes rotates the family \(M_\alpha\) orthogonally.
The cap average of the full sum of squares is consequently
at least \((|a|^2/2)C_6^{\rm vec}r^6\). Its full-Haar quadratic
norm is \(|a|^2/2\), since
\(\int\sum_\alpha|cq_\alpha|^2=4(1/4-1/8)=1/2\);
these harmonics are orthogonal to all the affine remainders.
The cap averaging and the density floor hence give
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B)
 =\max\{e^{-2\pi B},
      C_6^{\rm vec}e^{-B r_6(B)}r_6(B)^6\},\\
 \int\sum_\alpha|{\cal P}_\alpha|^2\rho\,dU
                       \ge\frac{|a|^2}{2}\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG59}
\]
This is exactly the PS function \(\Theta_6\); the superscript
distinguishes it from the stronger complete-gradient factor
\(\Psi_6\), which applies to a different integrand.

For each boundary chord \(e_\partial=(m,k,l;2)\), condition on
the bulk chord \(e=(1,k,l;2)\), with \(0\le k<m\), \(0\le l\le m\).
This single selected bulk chord serves only as the conditional
variable for a boundary derivative. It is not an extra summand
in (PG55). Its exact row coefficient is
\[
 d_{k,l}=-4a_{(1,k,l;2)}b_{(1,k,l;2)}
 =\frac{8\cos(3h)\cos((2l+1)h)\sin^2(\pi(k+1)/n)}{n^3}.
 \tag{PG60}
\]
The zero at \(l=L\) and both signs remain in the formula.
The left boundary variation \(H_\partial(t)=e^{tT_\alpha}H_\partial\)
gives \(KH_\partial(t)^{-1}=KH_\partial^{-1}e^{-tT_\alpha}\).
Direct quaternion multiplication yields
\(\partial_t c(Ve^{-tT_\alpha})|_0=q_\alpha(V)/4\) and
\(\partial_t|q(Ve^{-tT_\alpha})|^2|_{t=0}=-2c(V)q_\alpha(V)\).
Differentiate the complete expansions (PG31) and (PG53).
Since \(A_{k,l}\) is independent of this boundary link, the
quadratic terms of the entire physical derivatives are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 X_{e_\partial,\alpha}D
    &=2a_eb_e\,c(KH_\partial^{-1})
                    q_\alpha(KH_\partial^{-1})+R^D_\alpha(K),\\
 X_{e_\partial,\alpha}F
    &=-2\beta_0a_eb_e\,c(KH_\partial^{-1})
                    q_\alpha(KH_\partial^{-1})+R^F_\alpha(K).
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG61}
\]
Every derivative of every exterior row sum remains in
\(R^D_\alpha,R^F_\alpha\). These are affine in \(K\):
right multiplication is linear in its four coordinates, and the
only quadratic terms of the original expansions were displayed
explicitly. Thus no term of the actual derivative is set to zero.
Right translation \(K\mapsto KH_\partial^{-1}\) preserves its
conditional score bound \(B_l\), metric and Haar measure.
Applying (PG59) and integrating the actual exterior proves
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mu(|\nabla_{e_\partial}D|^2)
           \ge\frac{d_{k,l}^2}{8}\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B_l),\qquad
 \mu(|\nabla_{e_\partial}F|^2)
           \ge\frac{\beta_0^2d_{k,l}^2}{8}
                                      \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B_l),\\
 B_6=\frac{3\cos^2(3h)}{n^5}
       [2\cos^2h\,\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(12\xi)
                         +J_n\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(16\xi)] .
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG62}
\]
The last line is the exact sum of the first bound over all
\(m(m+1)\) distinct boundary chords. Substituting (PG60) gives
the common coefficient \(64(3n/8)\cos^2(3h)/(8n^6)\);
the endpoint and interior \(l\)-sums are exactly those in (PG35).

Every boundary chord here has first coordinate \(m\), whereas
every bulk derivative in (PG55) has first coordinate at most
\(m-1\). These physical derivative sets are disjoint. Their
conditional variables may overlap, but their derivative energies
do not. Adding the already integrated expectations therefore proves
\[
 \boxed{\mu(|\nabla D|^2)\ge B_6+A_D,\qquad
 C_F\ge\kappa\beta_0^2(B_6+A_F).}
 \tag{PG63}
\]
This addition does not add two bounds on the same derivative,
does not add mass bounds, and does not posit independence.

\subsection*{15. Every path contribution and both original endpoint inequalities}

All factors in (PG63) have explicit limits on (PG42). Define
\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 P_3=c_6(\kappa_*^2/(20000e))^6,&
 P_4=c_6(3\kappa_*^2/(80000e))^6,\\
 T_{1,2}=C_1(\kappa_*^2/(40000e))^2,&
 T_{1,3}=C_1(\kappa_*^2/(60000e))^2,\\
 T_{6,3}=C_6^{\rm vec}(\kappa_*^2/(20000e))^6,&
 T_{6,4}=C_6^{\rm vec}(3\kappa_*^2/(80000e))^6 .
 \end{array}\tag{PG64}
\]
For each fixed \(\kappa_*>0\), eventually all score arguments
exceed the respective optimization breakpoints. Their polynomial
branches then dominate the exponential branches, since
\(j^k e^{-b j^2}\to0\) for every \(b>0\) and fixed \(k\);
this follows by taking logarithms and using \(\log j\le j\).
Consequently the following are eventually exact:
\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \Psi_6(12\xi_j)=P_3j^{-12},&
 \Psi_6(16\xi_j)=P_4j^{-12},\\
 \Theta_1(8\xi_j)=T_{1,2}j^{-4},&
 \Theta_1(12\xi_j)=T_{1,3}j^{-4},\\
 \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(12\xi_j)=T_{6,3}j^{-12},&
 \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(16\xi_j)=T_{6,4}j^{-12}.
 \end{array}\tag{PG65}
\]
The factor two in the exponential branch of \(\Psi_6\) changes
none of these eventual equalities.

To retain all summands, write \(B_6=B_{6,\partial}+B_{6,{\rm int}}\)
and \(A_D=A_{D,\partial}+A_{D,{\rm int}}\) according to their
two displayed brackets. Write the odd bound as
\(A_F=A_{F,\partial,0}+A_{F,\partial,1}
+A_{F,{\rm int},0}+A_{F,{\rm int},1}\), where index zero
selects the factor one and index one selects the displayed
\(\Theta_1/2\). Substitution of (PG65), \(n_j/j^4\to2\),
\(J_{n_j}/j^4\to1\), and both cosine limits gives the complete
table of nonzero leading coefficients:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c}
 \text{exact positive summand}&\text{power }p&
                 \lim_{j\to\infty}j^p(\text{summand})\\ \hline
 B_{6,\partial}&32&(3/16)T_{6,3}\\
 B_{6,{\rm int}}&28&(3/32)T_{6,4}\\
 A_{D,\partial}&32&(3/16)P_3T_{1,2}\\
 A_{D,{\rm int}}&28&(3/32)P_4T_{1,3}\\
 A_{F,\partial,0}&28&P_3/8\\
 A_{F,\partial,1}&32&P_3T_{1,2}/16\\
 A_{F,{\rm int},0}&24&P_4/16\\
 A_{F,{\rm int},1}&28&P_4T_{1,3}/32
 \end{array}\tag{PG66}
\]
Each row is an asymptotic statement about its displayed exact
summand. The table does not discard finite-size corrections of
other summands or assert a multi-term expansion of the total.
For example \(3J_n^2/n^5\) times \(j^{12}\) tends to \(3/32\),
and \(2J_n^2/n^5\) times \(j^{12}\) tends to \(1/16\);
these prove the interior \(A_D\) and \(A_F\) entries. The other
entries follow from the same products with one less \(J_n\).

It follows that the final lower-bound functions satisfy
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 j^{28}(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
       &\longrightarrow\frac3{32}(T_{6,4}+P_4T_{1,3}),\\
 j^{24}(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
       &\longrightarrow P_4/16,\\
 j^{30}\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
       &\longrightarrow\frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*}P_4,\\
 j^{34}\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
       &\longrightarrow\frac{1875\pi^2}{4\kappa_*}
                                      (T_{6,4}+P_4T_{1,3}) .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG67}
\]
Here \(j^6\beta_{0,j}^2\to5000\pi^2/\kappa_*^2\).
In the last line the quantity bounded below is the energy of
the unchanged even raw state \(\beta_0D\psi\); no division by
its mass is made.

The complete endpoint also has the exact even identity
\[
 {\cal E}(s)=
 4\beta_0^2[Q_4-(\kappa/s)K_1]
          -\beta_0^2\mu(D^2)
          +(\kappa\beta_0^2/s)\mu(|\nabla D|^2).
 \tag{PG68}
\]
Indeed, expanding the full squares gives
\(\mu(D^2)=2(Q_4+Q_4^{\mathcal R})\) and
\(\mu(|\nabla D|^2)=2(K_1+K_{\mathcal R})\);
inserting them gives exactly (PG45). Thus the strengthened
original-path receiving statements, with their signs intact, are
\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 {\cal E}_j(s_j)&\le
      G_j-j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j}),\\
 {\cal E}_j(s_j)&\ge
      4\beta_{0,j}^2[Q_{4,j}-jK_{1,j}]
       -\beta_{0,j}^2\mu_j(D_j^2)
       +j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j}).
 \end{aligned}}\tag{PG69}
\]
Their explicit negative and positive contributions have the limits
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \lim_{j\to\infty}j^{29}
       j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
           &=\frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*^2}P_4,\\
 \lim_{j\to\infty}j^{33}
       j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
           &=\frac{1875\pi^2}{4\kappa_*^2}
                                      (T_{6,4}+P_4T_{1,3}) .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG70}
\]
The positive correction has the additional strictly positive
coefficient \(P_4T_{1,3}\) beyond the boundary-only leading
coefficient. The raw odd energy has the bulk leading power
\(j^{-30}\), with every boundary contribution still retained.
The remaining \(G_j\), \(Q_{4,j}-jK_{1,j}\), and \(\mu_j(D_j^2)\)
are the original expectations, not placeholders replaced by
assumptions. Their signs in (PG69) forbid substituting lower mass
bounds into negative mass terms to infer a lower endpoint bound.
No energy-to-mass quotient or positive limiting spectral-window
mass is concluded.
\clearpage
\section*{Full vacuum response and a direct bound for the original raw phase quotient}

11 October 2026. The result below keeps the original finite open box, rooted words, reflection, raw phase, Hamiltonian, and simultaneous path. It proves an exact response representation of the complete endpoint and a direct quantitative estimate for its energy-to-mass quotient. The new estimate is \(O(j^{16})\), compared with the \(O(j^{68})\) estimate obtained by dividing the preceding independent energy and mass bounds. Neither estimate proves positivity in the window \(s_j=\kappa_*/j\).

The receiving programme sources are \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/PERIOD_COUPLING_AND_PHYSICAL_KERNELS.md#L3500}{the original phase and endpoint, PK158–PK160 and PK199–PK202}, \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/442786391ab942ad0c829c0ebf32e952585915e0/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/PHASE_MOMENTS_AND_ORIGINAL_PATH.md}{IP1–IP22}, \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/442786391ab942ad0c829c0ebf32e952585915e0/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/BOUNDARY_REFLECTION_AND_RAW_ENDPOINT.md}{JP1–JP49}, \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/442786391ab942ad0c829c0ebf32e952585915e0/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_CONDITIONAL_BOUNDS.md}{PC1–PC52}, and \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/442786391ab942ad0c829c0ebf32e952585915e0/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/GLOBAL_PHYSICAL_RESPONSE.md}{the full physical inverse, GPR1–GPR31}. The actual vacuum score is proved in the retained original \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/sources/spatial/spatial_continuum.tex#L1415}{spatial TeX, equations (77)–(79)}; its proof is reproduced below. These are programme derivations, not attributed human-paper results. The source ledger records the bounded source-index routing and actual reading coverage. No novelty or exhaustive-literature claim is made.

\subsection*{1. Every original object and the actual centered inverse}

Let \(L\ge2\), \(m=2L\), \(n=m+1\). Vertices \((i,k,l)\in\{0,\ldots,m\}^3\) stand for the original physical points \(o+a(i-L,k-L,l-L)\). Positive edges join consecutive vertices, and every positively oriented face has its original ordered holonomy
\[
 U_{(z;i,j)}=U_i(z)U_j(z+e_i)U_i(z+e_j)^{-1}U_j(z)^{-1},
 \quad W_p=\operatorname{tr}U_p,\quad
 N=3m(m+1)^2,\quad M=3m^2(m+1).
 \tag{PRE1}
\]
Write \(Q_L=SU(2)^{E_L}\), with product Haar probability \(dU\), and retain
\[
 T_\alpha=-i\sigma_\alpha/2,\quad
 X_{e,\alpha}f=\left.\frac d{dt}f(\ldots,e^{tT_\alpha}U_e,\ldots)\right|_0,
 \quad K=-\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}^2,\quad
 \Gamma(f,h)=\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}f\,X_{e,\alpha}h.
 \tag{PRE2}
\]
The three fields give the radius-two metric on each group factor. The gauge constraint is invariance under \(U_{(s,t)}\mapsto q_sU_{(s,t)}q_t^{-1}\) at every vertex, including the boundary vertices. Denote the gauge average by \(\Pi\). The complete original Hamiltonian, including its scalar potential, is
\[
 H=\kappa K+b_{\rm mag}\sum_p(2-W_p),\qquad
 \kappa=\frac{2g^2}{a}>0,\quad b_{\rm mag}=\frac1{2g^2a},\quad
 \xi=\frac{b_{\rm mag}}{\kappa}=\frac1{4g^4},\qquad
 H\psi=E_0\psi,\quad \int\psi^2dU=1.
 \tag{PRE3}
\]
The ground state is strictly positive, smooth, gauge invariant, and unique with this positive phase. One proof is the positive heat kernel on the connected compact link product, preserved by multiplication with the bounded real potential through the product formula; the lowest spectral eigenspace is consequently simple and positive. Elliptic regularity makes it smooth. Gauge invariance follows by uniqueness. Put
\[
 d\mu=\rho\,dU=\psi^2dU,\quad A=H-E_0,\quad
 P_Hf=\int f\,dU,\quad v=\log\psi-P_H\log\psi,\quad
 c=P_H\log\psi=-\tfrac12\log\int e^{2v}dU.
 \tag{PRE4}
\]
Thus the constant \(2\kappa\xi M\), the scalar \(E_0\), and the logarithmic scalar \(c\) have all been retained.

Use the unchanged Sobolev scale
\[
 H^s(Q_L)=\{f:(I+K)^{s/2}f\in L^2(dU)\},\quad
 H^s_{{\rm ph},\mu0}=\{f\in H^s:\Pi f=f,\ \langle f,\rho\rangle=0\}.
 \tag{PRE5}
\]
For negative \(s\), the bracket is the distribution pairing with the smooth positive density. Smooth multiplication and its smooth reciprocal act boundedly on every \(H^s\). Multiplication \(U_\psi f=\psi f\) is unitary from \(L^2(\mu)_{\rm ph}\) to \(L^2(dU)_{\rm ph}\), with respective operator and form domains \(H^2_{\rm ph}\) and \(H^1_{\rm ph}\). Its exact conjugate is
\[
 \mathcal L=K-2\Gamma(v,\cdot)
   =\kappa^{-1}\psi^{-1}A\psi,\qquad
 \mu(\mathcal Lh)=0,\qquad
 \mu(f\mathcal Lh)=\mu\Gamma(f,h).
 \tag{PRE6}
\]
The last two identities hold for smooth functions by integration by parts, since \(X\rho=2\rho Xv\), and extend to the indicated Sobolev dualities. Expanding \(H(f\psi)\) proves the conjugation with the entire potential and \(E_0\) present. The vacuum equation cancels precisely their multiplication terms and the \(fK\psi\) term.

The only distributional kernel elements of \(\mathcal L\) are constants: elliptic regularity makes such an element smooth, and the last identity in (PRE6) makes every derivative zero. The compact manifold is connected. Consequently
\[
 \mathcal L:H^{s+2}_{{\rm ph},\mu0}\longrightarrow
 H^s_{{\rm ph},\mu0}
 \quad\hbox{is a bounded isomorphism for every real }s.
 \tag{PRE7}
\]
For completeness, \(K^{-1}Q_H\mathcal L=I-2K^{-1}Q_H\Gamma(v,\cdot)\) on the zero-Haar-mean space is identity minus an operator gaining one derivative, hence a Fredholm operator of index zero. Its kernel is zero: \(\mathcal Lh\) is then constant; its \(\mu\) mean makes that constant zero, and the Haar mean removes the remaining constant in \(h\). Its bounded inverse solves \(\mathcal Lh=f-\mu f\), with a subsequent subtraction of \(\mu h\). Compact Sobolev inclusions follow by cutting off the product-spin spectrum of \(K\). Smooth multiplication, differentiation, and the spectral Sobolev interpolation give the claimed mappings at every real index. This proves (PRE7), including the distribution extension, without a small-source restriction.

For a real physical \(f\in H^s\), define its centered Poisson response
\[
 f^\circ=f-\mu f,\qquad
 u_f=\mathcal L^{-1}f^\circ
       =\kappa\psi^{-1}A^\dagger(\psi f^\circ),\qquad \mu u_f=0.
 \tag{PRE8}
\]
Here \(A^\dagger\) is the full inverse on the physical \(\psi^\perp\), extended to annihilate \(\psi\). It maps \(H^s_{\rm ph}\) to \(H^{s+2}_{\rm ph}\) after its vacuum projection. Its extension follows from (PRE7) by multiplication with \(\psi\). Thus no compressed operator has replaced \(A\).

The exact connection with the earlier Haar-mean Jacobian is
\[
 h_f=(I-2K^{-1}Q_H\Gamma(v,\cdot))^{-1}K^{-1}Q_Hf,\quad
 h_f=u_f-P_Hu_f,\quad
 u_f=h_f-\mu h_f.
 \tag{PRE9}
\]
Indeed both \(h_f\) and \(u_f\) solve \(\mathcal Lh=f-\mu f\); the two specified means fix their difference. In particular the scalar removed by \(Q_H\) is not confused with the actual-vacuum mean.

\subsection*{2. The complete phase, its reflection, and its affine terms}

The original tree is rooted at \((0,0,0)\). Root paths proceed in directions three, two, one; the tree contains every direction-one edge, direction-two edges at \(i=0\), and direction-three edges at \(i=k=0\). Every chord remains the full word \(Z_c=t_sU_ct_t^{-1}\). The two original chord sets are
\[
 \mathcal C_2=\{(i,k,l;2):1\le i\le m,\ 0\le k<m,\ 0\le l\le m\},
 \quad
 \mathcal C_3=\{(i,k,l;3):0\le i,k\le m,\ (i,k)\ne(0,0),\
                                  0\le l<m\}.
 \tag{PRE10}
\]
Keep the original rows and every excluded tree edge:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 h=\frac{\pi}{2n},\quad
 v_0(l)=n^{-1/2},\quad
 v_1(l)=\sqrt{2/n}\cos\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n,\quad
 w_1(l)=-\sqrt{2/n}\sin\frac{\pi(l+1)}n,\\
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}=-v_1(i)w_1(k)v_0(l)/\sqrt2,\quad a_{(i,k,l;3)}=0,\\
 b_{(i,k,l;2)}=v_0(i)w_1(k)v_1(l)/\sqrt2,\quad
 b_{(i,k,l;3)}=-v_0(i)v_1(k)w_1(l)/\sqrt2,\\
 q^\alpha(U)=-2\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha U),\quad
 u_1=\sum_ca_cq(Z_c),\quad u_3=\sum_cb_cq(Z_c),\quad
 \mathcal S=u_1\cdot u_3,\\
 \sigma_*=\sqrt8\sin h,\quad\beta_0=\frac{\sigma_*}{2g^2},\quad
 p=\beta_0\mathcal S,\quad r=\beta_0\mathcal S_{\mathcal R},\quad
 F=p-r,\quad D=\mathcal S+\mathcal S_{\mathcal R}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{PRE11}
\]
The direction-one graph reflection \(\mathcal R\) includes the reflected root and every inverse edge orientation. It is a Haar-preserving isometry, preserves the full potential, and acts on gauge constraints by permuting vertices. Hence it commutes with \(H,K,\mathcal L,A^\dagger\), and uniqueness gives \(\psi\circ\mathcal R=\psi\). Therefore
\[
 F\circ\mathcal R=-F,\quad D\circ\mathcal R=D,\quad
 \mu F=P_HF=0,\quad
 \mu D=2\mu\mathcal S,\quad P_HD=2P_H\mathcal S.
 \tag{PRE12}
\]
These statements do not center or alter the original raw \(D\).

The exact tree-coordinate presentation sets original tree links to identity and keeps each chord equal to its full \(Z_c\). It preserves product Haar after inclusion of all tree transports, by successive left and right invariance; gauge-invariant functions are independent of these transports. In this presentation put
\[
 A_{k,l}=\left(\prod_{t=0}^{l-1}U_3(m,0,t)\right)
          \left(\prod_{b=0}^{k-1}U_2(m,b,l)\right).
 \tag{PRE13}
\]
Both products are in increasing index order, empty products equal identity, and \(U_2(0,k,l)=U_3(0,0,l)=I\). The full reflected words are
\[
 Z^{\mathcal R}_{(i,k,l;2)}
   =A_{k,l}U_2(m-i,k,l)A_{k+1,l}^{-1},\qquad
 Z^{\mathcal R}_{(i,k,l;3)}
   =A_{k,l}U_3(m-i,k,l)A_{k,l+1}^{-1}.
 \tag{PRE14}
\]
Reflection of each of the three original root segments gives the two endpoint products and the target in exactly this order; its direction-one segments are tree links in this presentation. No endpoints are commuted.

For example retain the stronger receiving chord already present in PC48,
\[
 v^\circ=(1,L-1,0;2),\quad e_\partial^\circ=(m,L-1,0;2),\quad
 K_\circ=Z_{v^\circ},\quad H_\partial=Z_{e_\partial^\circ},\quad
 \mathcal T=A_{L-1,0},\quad
 a^\circ=\frac{\sqrt2}{n^{3/2}}\cos(3h)\cos h,\quad
 b^\circ=-\frac{\sqrt2}{n^{3/2}}\cos^2h.
 \tag{PRE15}
\]
The unique reflected target of \(K_\circ\) is
\(\mathcal T K_\circ H_\partial^{-1}\mathcal T^{-1}\).
Writing \(R_\mathcal T=\operatorname{Ad}_{\mathcal T}\), define
\(U_\mu=\sum_{c\ne v^\circ}\mathsf M_{\mu c}q(Z_c)\) and
\(V_\mu=\sum_{c\ne(m-1,L-1,0;2)}\mathsf M_{\mu c}q(Z_c^{\mathcal R})\).
They are independent of \(K_\circ\), retain all other variables, and may depend on \(H_\partial\). The complete two products are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal S={}&U_1\cdot U_3
       +(b^\circ U_1+a^\circ U_3)\cdot q(K_\circ)
       +a^\circ b^\circ|q(K_\circ)|^2,\\
 \mathcal S_{\mathcal R}={}&V_1\cdot V_3
       +(b^\circ V_1-a^\circ V_3)\cdot
                          R_\mathcal Tq(K_\circ H_\partial^{-1})
       -a^\circ b^\circ|q(K_\circ H_\partial^{-1})|^2 .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PRE16}
\]
The second equality uses the original reflected coefficient \(-a^\circ\) and the unchanged coefficient \(b^\circ\); the orthogonal adjoint action preserves the displayed squared norm. All affine terms and constants in (PRE16) will remain in every subsequent operator and integral.

\subsection*{3. Actual source response, including every scalar}

For any fixed smooth real physical observable \(f\), freeze its entire function at the given regulator and introduce the auxiliary source only for differentiation:
\[
 H(t)=H-\kappa t f,\qquad
 H(t)\psi_t=E(t)\psi_t,\quad \int\psi_t^2dU=1,\quad\psi_t>0.
 \tag{PRE17}
\]
This is a response probe of the full Hamiltonian. Evaluation is always at \(t=0\); it does not change the prescribed path. The isolated simple vacuum and a resolvent contour yield real analytic \(E(t),\psi_t\), locally at every finite real \(t\). The resolvent Neumann series is valid on that contour for small bounded perturbations, and Sobolev regularity then gives analytic dependence in every \(C^k\). This also proves analytic dependence on all the original finite real face sources.

Differentiating the complete eigenvalue equation, and differentiating its unit norm, gives
\[
 E'(0)=-\kappa\mu f,\quad
 \dot\psi_0=\kappa A^\dagger(\psi f^\circ)=\psi u_f,\quad
 \mu u_f=0,\quad
 \left.\frac d{dt}\mu_t g\right|_0
       =2\mu(g^\circ u_f).
 \tag{PRE18}
\]
The last formula assumes \(g\) fixed; otherwise the additional term is \(\mu\dot g_0\). The full potential in (PRE3) remains when deriving (PRE18). The subtraction \(E'(0)\psi=-\kappa\mu(f)\psi\) is exactly what centers the source.

For \(v_t=\log\psi_t-P_H\log\psi_t\), \(c_t=P_H\log\psi_t\), the complete logarithmic and scalar equations are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 Kv_t&=\xi\sum_pW_p+tQ_Hf+\Gamma(v_t,v_t)-P_H\Gamma(v_t,v_t),\\
 E(t)&=2\kappa\xi M-\kappa tP_Hf
                         -\kappa P_H\Gamma(v_t,v_t),\\
 c_t&=-\tfrac12\log\int e^{2v_t}dU,\qquad
 \dot v_0=h_f,\qquad \dot c_0=-\mu h_f=P_Hu_f.
 \end{aligned}\tag{PRE19}
\]
These identities follow by dividing the eigenvalue equation by \(\psi_t\), using \(K(e^v)=e^v(Kv-\Gamma(v,v))\), and then taking Haar means. In particular
\[
 E''(0)=-2\kappa\,\mu(f^\circ u_f),\qquad
 \ddot c_0=-\mu\ddot v_0
      -2\{\mu(h_f^2)-(\mu h_f)^2\}.
 \tag{PRE20}
\]
For the second scalar differentiate \(\dot c_t=-\mu_t\dot v_t\) and use \(\partial_t\log\psi_t=\dot v_t-\mu_t\dot v_t\). This proves all normalization contributions.

Define the full response bilinear form
\[
 \mathfrak R(f,g)
  =\kappa\langle \psi f^\circ,A^\dagger\psi g^\circ\rangle_{L^2(dU)}
  =\mu(f^\circ u_g)=\mu\Gamma(u_f,u_g).
 \tag{PRE21}
\]
It is positive on nonconstant physical observables modulo constants, symmetric for real functions, and retains the complete inverse. The equalities follow from (PRE6) and (PRE8). For \(f=F\), reflection maps \(H(t)\) to \(H(-t)\), so \(E(t)=E(-t)\), \(E'(0)=0\), and \(u_F=h_F\) is odd with both Haar and actual means zero. In particular \(\dot c_0=0\). No such zero scalar is imposed for \(\mathcal S\) or \(D\).

For a general real smooth \(f\), \(u_{\mathcal L f}=f-\mu f\). Thus the exact receiving identities are
\[
 \operatorname{Cov}_\mu(f,g)
       =\mu\Gamma(u_f,g)=\mathfrak R(f,\mathcal Lg),\qquad
 \mu\Gamma(f,g)=\mathfrak R(\mathcal Lf,\mathcal Lg).
 \tag{PRE22}
\]
The mean \(\mu(\mathcal Lf)=0\) is part of this statement. Both equalities follow by applying (PRE6), not by replacing the full operator with a finite observable matrix.

\subsection*{4. The complete endpoint as a response and a cancellation identity}

Let
\[
 G=\mu(F^2)>0,\quad
 C_F=\kappa\mu\Gamma(F,F)>0,\quad
 J_F=\mathcal LF=KF-2\Gamma(v,F).
 \tag{PRE23}
\]
All terms in (PRE16), and all their physical derivatives, occur in \(J_F\). In particular
\[
 K\mathcal S=(Ku_1)\cdot u_3+u_1\cdot Ku_3
                   -2\sum_{e,\alpha}(X_{e,\alpha}u_1)
                                      \cdot(X_{e,\alpha}u_3),
 \quad
 J_F=\beta_0(I-\mathcal R^*)\{K\mathcal S-2\Gamma(v,\mathcal S)\}.
 \tag{PRE24}
\]
To verify the strict inequalities in (PRE23) directly, set every original physical link to identity except \(K_\circ=\exp(\pi T_1)\). The boundary products are identity; the two original mode vectors are \(a^\circ q(K_\circ),b^\circ q(K_\circ)\), while their reflected counterparts have coefficients \(-a^\circ,b^\circ\). Thus \(F=8\beta_0a^\circ b^\circ\ne0\), whereas at the identity configuration \(F=0\). Positivity of the smooth vacuum makes \(G>0\). If \(C_F=0\), every derivative of \(F\) would vanish, contradicting this nonconstancy.
Each \(Ku_\mu\) differentiates every occurrence in its full rooted words. For an ordered word \(\prod_{r=1}^{\ell}B_r(t)\), the second derivative is
\(\sum_rB_1\cdots B_r''\cdots B_\ell+
2\sum_{r<s}B_1\cdots B_r'\cdots B_s'\cdots B_\ell\).
For a positive occurrence the first and second differentiated factors are \(T_\alpha U_e,T_\alpha^2U_e\); for an inverse occurrence they are \(-U_e^{-1}T_\alpha,U_e^{-1}T_\alpha^2\). Summing this formula over all three colours and all physical edges with the minus sign of \(K\) specifies every derivative in (PRE24), including repeated occurrences and inverse signs.

The exact response matrix of the two complete sources \(F,J_F\) is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}
 \mathfrak R(F,F)&\mathfrak R(F,J_F)\\
 \mathfrak R(J_F,F)&\mathfrak R(J_F,J_F)
 \end{pmatrix}
 =
 \begin{pmatrix}
 T_F&G\\G&C_F/\kappa
 \end{pmatrix},
 \qquad T_F=\mu\Gamma(u_F,u_F)=\mu(Fu_F)>0.
 \tag{PRE25}
\]
Therefore the original raw endpoint is, exactly,
\[
 \mathscr E(s)=G-\frac{C_F}{s}
 =\mathfrak R(F,J_F)-\frac{\kappa}{s}\mathfrak R(J_F,J_F)
 =\mu\Gamma\left(F,u_F-\frac{\kappa}{s}F\right).
 \tag{PRE26}
\]
This is an identity for the original unscaled vector \(F\psi\). Its spectral measure is \(\nu_F(B)=\langle F\psi,\mathbf1_B(A)F\psi\rangle\); since this vector is odd and vacuum orthogonal,
\(\nu_F((0,s])\ge\max(0,\mathscr E(s))\).
Indeed \(1-\lambda/s\le\mathbf1_{(0,s]}(\lambda)\) for every \(\lambda>0\), and integration proves the claim.

The complete reflection cancellation remains visible in this representation:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr E(s)=\beta_0^2[&
 4\mu(\mathcal S^2)-4\mu(\mathcal SD)+\mu(D^2)\\
 &-\frac{\kappa}{s}\{4\mu\Gamma(\mathcal S,\mathcal S)
           -4\mu\Gamma(\mathcal S,D)+\mu\Gamma(D,D)\}]\\
 ={}&4\beta_0^2\left[\mu(\mathcal S^2)
                     -\frac{\kappa}{s}\mu\Gamma(\mathcal S,\mathcal S)\right]
       -\beta_0^2\mu(D^2)+\frac{\kappa\beta_0^2}{s}\mu\Gamma(D,D).
 \end{aligned}\tag{PRE27}
\]
To prove the second equality, reflect and add the first mixed expectation to obtain \(2\mu(\mathcal SD)=\mu(D^2)\). Reflection preserves the metric, so the same calculation gives \(2\mu\Gamma(\mathcal S,D)=\mu\Gamma(D,D)\). No term of the affine formula (PRE16) is removed.

The constant contributions are also exact. If \(m_{\mathcal S}=\mu\mathcal S\), then
\[
 4\mu(\mathcal S^2)-\mu(D^2)
  =4\operatorname{Var}_\mu\mathcal S-\operatorname{Var}_\mu D
       +(4m_{\mathcal S}^2-(2m_{\mathcal S})^2).
 \tag{PRE28}
\]
The last parenthesis equals zero by the proved mean relation; it is not suppressed by an independent centering choice. With \(P_\pm=(I\pm\mathcal R^*)/2\),
\[
 u_D=2P_+u_{\mathcal S},\quad
 u_F=2\beta_0P_-u_{\mathcal S},\quad
 u_F=\beta_0(2u_{\mathcal S}-u_D).
 \tag{PRE29}
\]
These follow by applying the centered Poisson inverse, which commutes with reflection. They give an exact morphism from the entire even correction and its means to the odd physical response.

The first attempt to turn positivity of (PRE25) into an upper quotient bound has the opposite direction. Its precise defect is
\[
 \frac{C_F}{\kappa}-\frac{G^2}{T_F}
  =\mu\Gamma\left(F-\frac{G}{T_F}u_F,\,
                       F-\frac{G}{T_F}u_F\right)\ge0.
 \tag{PRE30}
\]
Expanding the square uses \(\mu\Gamma(F,u_F)=G\) and
\(\mu\Gamma(u_F,u_F)=T_F\). Equality holds exactly when
\(F=(G/T_F)u_F\), since both are odd and constants have been removed.
Applying \(\mathcal L\) then shows that equality means precisely that the entire \(F\psi\) is an eigenvector of \(A\), of energy \(\kappa G/T_F\). Thus susceptibility positivity alone gives a lower bound for \(C_F/G\).

Completing the square retains the remaining cancellation explicitly:
\[
 \mathscr E(s)
 =\frac{s}{4\kappa}T_F-\frac{\kappa}{s}
       \mu\Gamma\left(F-\frac{s}{2\kappa}u_F,\,
                         F-\frac{s}{2\kappa}u_F\right).
 \tag{PRE31}
\]
This formula proves the exact deficit of the response-only upper bound
\(\mathscr E(s)\le sT_F/(4\kappa)\). It contains the full physical Poisson solution and the original raw phase.

\subsection*{5. The exact tree cancellation lowers the physical-link degree}

The next estimate controls the quotient directly, rather than dividing unrelated lower bounds. First a complete word cancellation improves the elementary degree count on every original physical link, including tree links.

For \(U,V\in SU(2)\), the original quaternion multiplication gives
\[
 q(U)\cdot q(V)=\operatorname{tr}(UV^{-1})-\operatorname{tr}(UV).
 \tag{PRE32}
\]
Indeed \(U=c_UI+q(U)\cdot T\) and
\(\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha T_\beta)=-\delta_{\alpha\beta}/2\).
The two traces are \(2c_Uc_V+q(U)\cdot q(V)/2\) and
\(2c_Uc_V-q(U)\cdot q(V)/2\), respectively. Their scalar terms cancel in their displayed difference. Consequently the full original product is
\[
 \mathcal S=\sum_{c,d}a_cb_d
        [\operatorname{tr}(Z_cZ_d^{-1})
                    -\operatorname{tr}(Z_cZ_d)].
 \tag{PRE33}
\]
Every ordered pair and both trace terms remain.

Fix a physical tree edge \(e\), remove it from the original tree, and let \(\epsilon_z\in\{0,1\}\) indicate whether the root path to \(z\) uses that edge. Under its physical variation \(U_e(t)=e^{tT_\alpha}U_e\), all root transports satisfy
\[
 t_z(t)=C(t)^{\epsilon_z}t_z,\qquad
 C(t)=A_e e^{tT_\alpha}A_e^{-1}.
 \tag{PRE34}
\]
Here \(A_e\) is the unchanged root transport to the starting vertex of \(e\). This holds because the tree root path uses \(e\) exactly once in positive orientation when \(\epsilon_z=1\). For an arbitrary oriented tree, including the reflected one, the same identity holds with a conjugate of \(e^{-tT_\alpha}\) when the traversal is inverse; it is still a one-parameter subgroup. Hence a chord from \(s\) to \(t\) changes exactly as \(Z_c(t)=C(t)^{\epsilon_s}Z_cC(t)^{-\epsilon_t}\).

For a second chord \(d\) from \(u\) to \(w\), cyclicity of trace gives both complete identities
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{tr}(Z_c(t)Z_d(t)^{-1})
   &=\operatorname{tr}\!\left[
       Z_cC(t)^{\epsilon_w-\epsilon_t}
       Z_d^{-1}C(t)^{\epsilon_s-\epsilon_u}\right],\\
 \operatorname{tr}(Z_c(t)Z_d(t))
   &=\operatorname{tr}\!\left[
       Z_cC(t)^{\epsilon_u-\epsilon_t}
       Z_dC(t)^{\epsilon_s-\epsilon_w}\right].
 \end{aligned}\tag{PRE35}
\]
Each displayed exponent belongs to \(\{-1,0,1\}\). Thus each trace is a polynomial of total degree at most two in \(\cos(t/2)\) and \(\sin(t/2)\). This proves the cancellation even when a full rooted word originally contains the same tree edge twice, and when the two full words contain it four times in total.

If \(e\) is a chord, only its own rooted word contains it, exactly once. A product in (PRE33) therefore again has at most two factors depending linearly on \(\cos(t/2),\sin(t/2)\), including \(c=d=e\). Applying precisely the same proof to the reflected tree and root covers every term of \(\mathcal S_{\mathcal R}\). Thus
\[
 t\longmapsto F(\ldots,e^{tT_\alpha}U_e,\ldots),\
 D(\ldots,e^{tT_\alpha}U_e,\ldots)
 \quad\hbox{have trigonometric degree at most two in }t/2
 \tag{PRE36}
\]
for every original \(e,\alpha,U\). The same holds for \(\mathcal S,\mathcal S_{\mathcal R}\). This is a property proved for the complete functions, including every affine remainder, not a projection to their conditional quadratic pieces.

\subsection*{6. A quantitative weighted derivative estimate with only the actual score}

We prove the required estimate directly. Let \(f\) be any real smooth function on the original product satisfying the degree property (PRE36) for a specified \(X=X_{e,\alpha}\). Let \(\rho>0\) be any smooth probability density with \(|X\log\rho|\le B\). In the application these are the actual full \(F\) and actual full vacuum density.

At a fixed configuration \(U\), put \(f_U(t)=f(e^{tT_\alpha}U_e,\ldots)\). The exact stereographic circle substitution is
\[
 z=\tan(t/4),\quad t=4\arctan z,\quad
 \cos(t/2)=\frac{1-z^2}{1+z^2},\quad
 \sin(t/2)=\frac{2z}{1+z^2},\qquad
 f_U(4\arctan z)=\frac{P_U(z)}{(1+z^2)^2},
 \quad \deg P_U\le4.
 \tag{PRE37}
\]
This is an exact change of the integration variable along the original physical one-parameter subgroup, not a change of any physical coordinate or raw function.

For a real polynomial \(P\) of degree at most four and \(0<r\le1/4\), its linear coefficient satisfies
\[
 |P'(0)|^2\le\frac{\gamma_2}{r^3}\int_{-r}^r|P(z)|^2dz,
 \qquad
 \gamma_2=\frac32+\frac72\left(\frac32\right)^2=\frac{75}{8}.
 \tag{PRE38}
\]
To prove this without an unspecified norm constant, expand \(P(ry)\) in the five polynomials \(1,y,(3y^2-1)/2,(5y^3-3y)/2,(35y^4-30y^2+3)/8\). Direct multiplication and \(\int_{-1}^1y^kdy=0\) for odd \(k\), \(2/(k+1)\) for even \(k\), give their orthogonality and squared norms \(2,2/3,2/5,2/7,2/9\). Only degrees one and three have a nonzero derivative at zero, with values \(1,-3/2\). Cauchy–Schwarz for that linear functional gives exactly the displayed sum \(\gamma_2\). The change of variable contributes \(r^{-3}\). Equivalently, the coefficient Gram matrix \(G_{ab}=\int_{-1}^1y^{a+b}dy\), \(0\le a,b\le4\), has \((G^{-1})_{11}=75/8\); direct rational inversion independently checks the same value.

At zero \(P_U'(0)=4Xf(U)\). Since \(dt=4\,dz/(1+z^2)\), keeping the full denominator gives
\[
 |Xf(U)|^2
 \le\frac{\gamma_2(1+r^2)^5}{64r^3}
       \int_{-4\arctan r}^{4\arctan r}|f_U(t)|^2dt .
 \tag{PRE39}
\]
Indeed the integral on the right before estimating its denominator is
\(4\int_{-r}^r|P_U(z)|^2(1+z^2)^{-5}dz\).
Combining it with (PRE38) and dividing by \(16\) proves every factor of (PRE39).

Multiply by \(\rho(U)\) and integrate over every original link. The score gives \(\rho(U)\le e^{B|t|}\rho(e^{tT_\alpha}U_e,\ldots)\). Haar invariance under this same physical left translation then gives the averaged inequality
\[
 \mu(|Xf|^2)\le b_2(B;r)\,\mu(f^2),\qquad
 b_2(B;r)=\frac{75}{64}
      (1+r^2)^5 e^{4B\arctan r}\frac{\arctan r}{r^3},
 \qquad 0<r\le\frac14.
 \tag{PRE40}
\]
The interval length is \(8\arctan r\); it multiplies the coefficient in (PRE39). This is why no lower bound for the raw mass, conditional Haar floor, or full-volume density ratio enters (PRE40). Every exterior correlation remains in \(\rho\).

Two entirely explicit choices are useful:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 r_B=\frac1{2(B+2)},\quad b_2(B)=b_2(B;r_B),\\
 b_2(B;[4(1+B)]^{-1})
      \le C_2(1+B)^2,\qquad
 C_2=\frac{75e}{4}\left(\frac{17}{16}\right)^5,\\
 \lim_{B\to\infty}\frac{b_2(B)}{B^2}
      =\frac{75e^2}{16}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{PRE41}
\]
For the middle bound use \(r\le1/4\), \(\arctan r\le r\), and
\(4Br\le1\) in (PRE40). The last limit follows from
\(Br_B\to1/2\), \(\arctan r_B/r_B\to1\), and \(r_BB\to1/2\).
One may use the minimum of the two explicit bounds, or any smaller value obtained by minimizing the displayed one-variable function in (PRE40). None is an assertion about the unknown vacuum.

\subsection*{7. Application to the actual vacuum and unchanged path}

The required score bound follows from the full vacuum equation. Write \(u=\log\psi\), \(\Delta=-K\), and \(w_e=|\nabla_eu|^2\). The product Bochner identity in the radius-two metric is
\[
 (\Delta+2\nabla u\cdot\nabla)w_e
 =2\sum_f|\nabla_f\nabla_eu|_{\rm HS}^2+w_e
                  +2\xi\langle\nabla_eu,\nabla_e W\rangle,\qquad
 W=\sum_p(2-W_p).
 \tag{PRE42}
\]
To obtain it, differentiate
\(\Delta u+|\nabla u|^2=\xi W-E_0/\kappa\).
Commuting factor-\(e\) covariant derivatives contributes
\(2\operatorname{Ric}_e=w_e\); different factors have zero mixed curvature. Substituting the differentiated vacuum equation cancels the drift term and retains all mixed Hessian squares. For a face incident to \(e\), its trace gradient has norm at most one: bi-invariance and inversion reduce it to \(2c(U)\), whose squared gradient on the radius-two sphere is \(1-c(U)^2\). If \(r_e\) is the actual number of incident faces, then \(|\nabla_eW|\le r_e\). At a maximum of \(w_e\), (PRE42) gives \(0\ge w_e-2r_e\xi\sqrt{w_e}\). Hence
\[
 |\nabla_e\log\psi|\le2r_e\xi,\qquad
 |\nabla_e\log\rho|\le4r_e\xi=:B_e.
 \tag{PRE43}
\]
This proof uses the full Hamiltonian and includes its unchanged scalar through the vacuum equation.

Apply (PRE40) to the full \(F\), using (PRE36), and sum all three colour fields of every original physical edge. Since \(G>0\), the result is the direct quotient estimate
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \frac{C_F}{G}\le\kappa\,\mathcal B_L(\xi),\qquad
 \mathcal B_L(\xi)
 =3\left[
 12m\,b_2(8\xi)
 +12m(m-1)b_2(12\xi)
 +3m(m-1)^2b_2(16\xi)\right].}
 \tag{PRE44}
\]
The finite count is exact. For each of three edge directions, there are \(m\) longitudinal positions and \(n^2\) transverse vertices. Four transverse corners have \(r_e=2\), \(4(m-1)\) noncorner boundary positions have \(r_e=3\), and \((m-1)^2\) interior positions have \(r_e=4\). Multiplying by \(3m\) gives the three multiplicities in (PRE44). All positive edge orientations and boundary incidences are present.

There is also a proved extension with the means retained: for every real \(f\) in the span of \(1,\mathcal S,\mathcal S_{\mathcal R}\), the same proof applied to \(f-\mu f\) gives \(\mu\Gamma(f,f)\le\mathcal B_L(\xi)\{\mu(f^2)-(\mu f)^2\}\). Subtraction of a constant preserves (PRE36). In particular the actual even correction obeys \(\kappa\mu\Gamma(D,D)\le\kappa\mathcal B_L(\xi)\{\mu(D^2)-4(\mu\mathcal S)^2\}\). This explicitly keeps its vacuum component and proves the upper energy comparison complementary to JP38; it makes no assumption that \(\mu D\) vanishes.

The alternate estimate with elementary polynomial dependence on \(\xi\) is
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \frac{C_F}{G}\le
 3\kappa C_2\left[
 N+32\xi M+192\xi^2m(4m^2+m-1)\right].}
 \tag{PRE45}
\]
Indeed the exact sums are
\[
 \sum_er_e=4M,\qquad
 \sum_er_e^2=12m(4m^2+m-1),\qquad
 \sum_e(1+4r_e\xi)^2=N+32\xi M+192\xi^2m(4m^2+m-1).
 \tag{PRE46}
\]
Expanding the three counts proves these equalities. Equation (PRE45) follows by applying the middle choice in (PRE41) independently to each field before summation. In particular both (PRE44) and (PRE45) are evaluated estimates for the ratio itself.

On the unchanged path,
\[
 L_j=j^4,\quad a_j=\frac1{100j},\quad
 g_j^2=\frac{\kappa_*}{200j},\quad
 \kappa_j=\kappa_*,\quad \xi_j=\frac{10000j^2}{\kappa_*^2},\quad
 s_j=\frac{\kappa_*}{j},\quad m_j=2j^4,\quad n_j=2j^4+1,
 \qquad j\ge2,\quad\kappa_*>0,
 \tag{PRE47}
\]
formula (PRE44) proves
\[
 \frac{C_{F,j}}{G_j}\le\kappa_*\mathcal B_{L_j}(\xi_j),\qquad
 \lim_{j\to\infty}
 \frac{\kappa_*\mathcal B_{L_j}(\xi_j)}{j^{16}}
       =\frac{8640000000000\,e^2}{\kappa_*^3}.
 \tag{PRE48}
\]
For the limit, the \(r_e=4\) multiplicity is
\(3m_j(m_j-1)^2\sim24j^{12}\);
its score is \(16\xi_j=160000j^2/\kappa_*^2\).
The other multiplicities are of orders \(j^4,j^8\), so after their squared-score factors they have orders \(j^8,j^{12}\). Inserting the last limit in (PRE41), including the three colour directions, gives
\(3\cdot24\cdot(75e^2/16)\cdot160000^2/\kappa_*^3
=8640000000000e^2/\kappa_*^3\).

The precise comparison with the previously evaluated separate bounds is
\[
 \frac{C_F}{G}
 \le U_{\rm sep}(L,\xi):=
 \frac{48\kappa\,\mathcal C_{\rm occ}}
 {(d^\circ)^2c_{\rm cap}(1+12\xi)^{-8}},
 \qquad
 d^\circ=\frac8{n^3}\cos(3h)\cos^3h.
 \tag{PRE49}
\]
Here IP7 gives \(C_F\le4\kappa\beta_0^2\mathcal C_{\rm occ}\);
PC49 gives \(G\ge\beta_0^2(d^\circ)^2
c_{\rm cap}(1+12\xi)^{-8}/12\), with
\(c_{\rm cap}=\cos^{14}(1/4)/(1024\cdot105041\cdot\pi\cdot3^{9/2})\).
The factor \(\beta_0^2\) cancels only in this quotient calculation; the original state is unchanged.
For an explicit comparison keep all the earlier constants:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 S_1=\sqrt{2/n}\cot h(\cot h-\cos h),\\
 S_3=\sqrt{2/n}\left[(2-1/n)\cot^2h-(\cos h/n)\cot h\right],\\
 A_1=m\sqrt{2/n}\cot h(5\cot h-4\cos h),\\
 A_3=(m/n)\sqrt{2/n}\left[(9m+5)\cot^2h-2\cos h\cot h\right],\\
 \mathcal C_{\rm occ}
  =12\left[S_3\sqrt{S_1A_1}+S_1\sqrt{S_3A_3}\right]^2,\qquad
 \frac{\mathcal C_{\rm occ}}{n^7}
       \longrightarrow\frac{12288(38+12\sqrt{10})}{\pi^8}.
 \end{gathered}\tag{PRE50}
\]
The limit follows from \(\cot h/n\to2/\pi\), giving
\(S_1/n^{3/2}\to4\sqrt2/\pi^2\),
\(S_3/n^{3/2}\to8\sqrt2/\pi^2\),
\(A_1/n^{5/2}\to20\sqrt2/\pi^2\), and
\(A_3/n^{5/2}\to36\sqrt2/\pi^2\).
Thus \(U_{{\rm sep},j}\) has a positive finite leading coefficient times \(j^{68}\), whereas (PRE48) has order \(j^{16}\). Their ratio tends to zero. The minimum of (PRE44), (PRE45), and (PRE49) is a valid bound at every finite regulator, and the new first bound is strictly smaller than (PRE49) for all sufficiently large \(j\).

The same calculation gives the exact receiving inequality
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \mathscr E_j(s_j)\ge
       [\,1-j\mathcal B_{L_j}(\xi_j)\,]G_j.}
 \tag{PRE51}
\]
Its limitation is evaluated, rather than left as an unspecified constant:
\[
 \frac{j\mathcal B_{L_j}(\xi_j)}{j^{17}}
       \longrightarrow\frac{8640000000000\,e^2}{\kappa_*^4}>0.
 \tag{PRE52}
\]
The coefficient in (PRE51) is therefore negative for all sufficiently large \(j\). This estimate does not decide the sign of the actual endpoint. The unscaled raw vector, the positive PC52 correction, the entire six-term cancellation (PRE27), and the original spectral measure all remain available; no substitution of separate lower bounds for their ratio is made.

\subsection*{8. A precise obstruction to using the inverse response alone}

The remaining defect in (PRE30) is not bounded above by the two numbers \(G,T_F\). This can be proved on the same original full Hamiltonian, retaining its potential and vacuum. Work in its physical odd subspace. It is infinite dimensional: the powers \(F,F^3,F^5,\ldots\) are linearly independent. Otherwise a nonzero polynomial would vanish on the image of the nonconstant continuous \(F\); that image contains an interval because the connected configuration space has a connected nontrivial real image, and full support turns an almost-everywhere polynomial equality into a pointwise one. Compact resolvent thus supplies infinitely many distinct positive dimensionless eigenvalues
\(\lambda_1<\lambda_2<\cdots\to\infty\) of \(\mathcal L\), with real orthonormal smooth physical odd eigenfunctions \(\phi_k\) in \(L^2(\mu)\). Here one may choose a subsequence if multiplicities occur.

Fix two such eigenvalues and put
\[
 t_*=\frac{\lambda_2-\lambda_1}{2\lambda_2}>0,\quad
 a_k=t_*\frac{\lambda_2^{-1}-\lambda_k^{-1}}
                    {\lambda_1^{-1}-\lambda_2^{-1}},\quad
 f_k=\sqrt{1+a_k}\,\phi_1
       +\sqrt{1-t_*-a_k}\,\phi_2+\sqrt{t_*}\,\phi_k,\quad k\ge3.
 \tag{PRE53}
\]
The coefficients are real: \(a_k\ge0\) and
\(t_*+a_k\le t_*\lambda_2/(\lambda_2-\lambda_1)=1/2\).
Each \(f_k\) is a complete actual physical odd test function. Direct orthogonality gives
\[
 \mu(f_k^2)=2,\qquad
 \mathfrak R(f_k,f_k)=\lambda_1^{-1}+\lambda_2^{-1},\qquad
 \mu\Gamma(f_k,f_k)
   =(1+a_k)\lambda_1+(1-t_*-a_k)\lambda_2+t_*\lambda_k
       \longrightarrow\infty.
 \tag{PRE54}
\]
The response equality follows by inserting (PRE53): its increment is
\(a_k(\lambda_1^{-1}-\lambda_2^{-1})
-t_*(\lambda_2^{-1}-\lambda_k^{-1})=0\).
Thus, on the domain of nonzero smooth real physical odd functions, the exact map into \((0,\infty)^2\times[0,\infty)\) given by
\[
 f\longmapsto
 \bigl(\mu f^2,\ \mathfrak R(f,f),\
             \mu\Gamma(f-(\mu f^2/\mathfrak R(f,f))u_f,\,
                         f-(\mu f^2/\mathfrak R(f,f))u_f)\bigr)
 \tag{PRE55}
\]
has an unbounded third component on the explicitly constructed fibre of its first two components. Equations (PRE30) and (PRE54) identify this fibre with the unresolved energy defect. This is an obstruction to a proposed inference from mass and susceptibility alone, with an exact connecting map on the original operator. It does not replace \(F\) by \(f_k\) in the requested endpoint and does not show that the actual phase has a large quotient.

The next concrete estimate after that obstruction was the weighted derivative estimate (PRE40), applied through the exact all-link degree cancellation (PRE35). It bounds precisely that previously uncontrolled energy for the original \(F\), yielding (PRE44)–(PRE52). A positive shrinking-window conclusion would require sharper information about the actual alignment in (PRE30) or (PRE31), or the complete six-term expectations in (PRE27). No such conclusion, closing gap, or continuum result follows from the present bounds.
\clearpage
\section*{Conditional edge-path entropy and an unconditional square-root vacuum score}

11 October 2026. This calculation concerns the same full finite-volume Hamiltonian and its actual positive unit vacuum. The entropy estimate below is for the tilted edge path conditional on every outside Brownian path. It does not assert the corresponding bound for the entropy of the entire tilted path law; that entropy contains an additional outside-marginal term.

The exact path transformation proves the proposed derivative inequality with the actual edge oscillation. It also closes that inequality without any additional oscillation theorem, giving an unconditional square-root edge score. All outside paths, the terminal vacuum factor, the original radius-two metric, and the complete potential are retained. The final section applies the result to the original raw phase quotient without changing its path or vector.

The retained original score source is \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/sources/spatial/spatial_continuum.tex#L1415}{Spatial continuum, equations (77)–(79)}. The full inverse and vacuum are \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/442786391ab942ad0c829c0ebf32e952585915e0/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/GLOBAL_PHYSICAL_RESPONSE.md}{GPR1–GPR10}. The receiving raw phase estimate is \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/PHASE_RESPONSE_AND_ENDPOINT.md}{PRE32–PRE52}. These are programme sources. The existing machine-readable literature indexes were consulted; routing hits are not claimed as read papers. The Gaussian change of measure and entropy estimate actually needed here are proved directly below.

\subsection*{1. Original operator, metric, and exact vacuum martingale}

Retain the open cubic box \(L\ge2\), \(m=2L\), \(n=m+1\), with original physical vertices \(o+a(i-L,k-L,l-L)\), \(0\le i,k,l\le m\).
