The definitions of the subscripts are exactly the corresponding bracket terms of (SR26), with index 1 retaining its factor \(\Theta_1/2\). The same substitutions prove this table. In particular PS alone gives \(j^{22}(B_6+B_2^\pm)\to3V_4/32\). The complete PG bound instead proves
\[
\begin{aligned}
j^{20}(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})&\to3P_4T_{1,3}/32,\\
j^{18}(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})&\to P_4/16,\\
j^{26}\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
&\to {1875\pi^2\over4\kappa_*}P_4T_{1,3},\\
j^{24}\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
&\to {625\pi^2\over2\kappa_*}P_4.
\end{aligned}\tag{SR31}
\]
More completely, for every individual energy row with exponent \(p\) and constant \(A\) in either table, multiplication by the original energy factor \(\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2\) changes its exponent to \(p+6\) and constant to \(\kappa_*K_\beta A\). Multiplication by the original endpoint factor \(\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2/s_j=j\beta_{0,j}^2\) changes them to \(p+5\) and \(K_\beta A\). This statement proves all eight physical energy contributions and all eight endpoint contributions individually, rather than retaining only the leading ones.

\subsection*{7. Both endpoint signs, with all original terms}

The reflection commutes with the entire Hamiltonian, preserves its positive unit vacuum and kinetic form, and interchanges \(S,S_{\mathcal R}\). Retain the actual four expectations
\[
Q_4=\mu(S^2),\quad Q_4^{\mathcal R}=\mu(SS_{\mathcal R}),\quad
K_1=\mu(|\nabla S|^2),\quad K_{\mathcal R}=\mu(\nabla S\cdot\nabla S_{\mathcal R}).
\tag{SR32}
\]
Expansion of the full original squares gives
\[
\begin{gathered}
G=2\beta_0^2(Q_4-Q_4^{\mathcal R}),\quad
C_F=2\kappa\beta_0^2(K_1-K_{\mathcal R}),\\
\mu(D^2)=2(Q_4+Q_4^{\mathcal R}),\quad
\mu(|\nabla D|^2)=2(K_1+K_{\mathcal R}),\\
\mathcal E(s):=G-C_F/s
=2\beta_0^2(Q_4-Q_4^{\mathcal R})-{2\kappa\beta_0^2\over s}(K_1-K_{\mathcal R})\\
=4\beta_0^2[Q_4-(\kappa/s)K_1]-\beta_0^2\mu(D^2)
+{\kappa\beta_0^2\over s}\mu(|\nabla D|^2).
\end{gathered}\tag{SR33}
\]
The odd energy has a negative coefficient and the even energy a positive coefficient in these respective identical expressions. Applying (SR25) therefore proves, for every path point,
\[
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
\mathcal E_j(s_j)&\le G_j-j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j}),\\
\mathcal E_j(s_j)&\ge4\beta_{0,j}^2[Q_{4,j}-jK_{1,j}]
-\beta_{0,j}^2\mu_j(D_j^2)+j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j}).
\end{aligned}}\tag{SR34}
\]
Every original signed term remains. The exact evaluated corrections are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\lim_j j^{23}\,j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
&={625\pi^2\over2\kappa_*^2}P_4,\\
\lim_j j^{25}\,j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
&={1875\pi^2\over4\kappa_*^2}P_4T_{1,3},\\
\lim_j j^{27}\,j\beta_{0,j}^2B_{6,j}
&={1875\pi^2\over4\kappa_*^2}V_4.
\end{aligned}\tag{SR35}
\]
Thus a positive-correction calculation retaining only the last line misses the larger proved bulk contribution in the second line. All the smaller contributions remain in the finite formulas and tables.

The odd vector \(F\psi\) is vacuum orthogonal. Integrating \(\mathbf1_{(0,s]}(t)\ge1-t/s\) against its positive spectral measure gives
\[
\langle F\psi,\mathbf1_{(0,s]}(H-E_0)F\psi\rangle\ge\max\{0,\mathcal E(s)\}.
\tag{SR36}
\]
A lower bound on \(\mu(D^2)\) cannot be inserted into its negative term in the lower line of (SR34). A lower bound on \(G\) cannot turn the upper line into a sign test. The asymptotics proved above concern the displayed bound functions; they are not asymptotic equalities for \(G,C_F,\mu(D^2)\), or the endpoint itself.

\subsection*{8. The complete raw quotient upper bound}

ESE33–ESE38 also provide an independent direct estimate of the quotient for this same \(F\), because its restriction along each original physical edge subgroup has degree at most two, including tree links. Retain
\[
r_B={1\over2(B+2)},\qquad
b_2(B)={75\over64}(1+r_B^2)^5e^{4B\arctan r_B}{\arctan r_B\over r_B^3},\qquad
{b_2(B)\over B^2}\to{75e^2\over16}.
\tag{SR37}
\]
The proved one-dimensional full-polynomial inequality applies separately to every colour field, so the exact edge incidence counts give
\[
{C_F\over G}\le\kappa\widetilde{\mathcal B}_L(\xi),\quad
\widetilde{\mathcal B}_L=3[12m\,b_2(B_2)+12m(m-1)b_2(B_3)+3m(m-1)^2b_2(B_4)].
\tag{SR38}
\]
The three factors count every original physical edge with incidence 2, 3, or 4; the leading factor three is the colour count. Using (SR29) and (SR37), their individual orders and constants are
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\text{summand of }\widetilde{\mathcal B}_{L_j}&\text{growth power}&\text{limit after division by that power}\\\hline
36m_jb_2(B_2)&j^6&6750000e^2C_\rho^2/\kappa_*^2\\
36m_j(m_j-1)b_2(B_3)&j^{10}&20250000e^2C_\rho^2/\kappa_*^2\\
9m_j(m_j-1)^2b_2(B_4)&j^{14}&13500000e^2C_\rho^2/\kappa_*^2
\end{array}\tag{SR39}
\]
For example the last count divided by \(j^{12}\) tends to 72 and \(b_4^2=40000C_\rho^2/\kappa_*^2\), giving \(72(75e^2/16)40000\). The other counts tend to 72 and 144 at their respective powers and use \(b_2^2=20000C_\rho^2/\kappa_*^2\), \(b_3^2=30000C_\rho^2/\kappa_*^2\). Consequently
\[
\mathcal E_j(s_j)\ge[1-j\widetilde{\mathcal B}_{L_j}(\xi_j)]G_j,\qquad
{j\widetilde{\mathcal B}_{L_j}(\xi_j)\over j^{15}}
\to{13500000e^2C_\rho^2\over\kappa_*^2}.
\tag{SR40}
\]
The coefficient on the right is eventually negative. This particular estimate therefore does not establish positivity of the actual endpoint, while remaining compatible with both signed inequalities in (SR34).

\subsection*{9. Actual face covariance and response at every finite real source}

For (SR1) define the actual matrices
\[
\begin{gathered}
C(x)_{pq}=\operatorname{Cov}_{\mu_x}(W_p,W_q),\quad
r_p=(W_p-\mu_xW_p)\psi_x,\quad A_x=H(x)-E(x),\\
R^{\rm phys}_{pq}(x)=\kappa\langle r_p,A_x^\dagger r_q\rangle,\quad
\partial_{x_q}\mu_xW_p=2R^{\rm phys}_{pq},\quad
\partial_{x_p}\partial_{x_q}E=-2\kappa R^{\rm phys}_{pq}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SR41}
\]
Here the dagger is the actual physical vacuum-complement inverse, extended by zero on the vacuum. The ground-state transform is \(\mathcal L_x=K-2\Gamma(\log\psi_x,\cdot)=\kappa^{-1}\psi_x^{-1}A_x\psi_x\). All equalities in (SR41) retain their GPR domains and factors. For a face coefficient vector \(a\), let \(F_a=\sum_pa_pW_p\).

Choose two distinct edges \(e,f\) of face \(p\). No other elementary face contains both. Conditional on all other links,
\(F_a=a_pW_p(U_e,U_f)+B_e(U_e)+B_f(U_f)+B_0\),
including the complete neighbouring face terms. Its two-link Haar square after subtraction of any constant is at least \(|a_p|^2\), by integrating the missing edge in every cross term. If \(T=B_e(x)+B_f(x)\), the actual conditional density has ratio at most \(e^{2\pi T}\). This proves conditional variance at least \(e^{-2\pi T}|a_p|^2\).

For the polynomial branch retain GPR's translated stereographic coordinates on each original edge:
\[
U=U^0{(1-|t|^2)I+2i\sum_\alpha t_\alpha\sigma_\alpha\over1+|t|^2},\quad
dU={4\over\pi^2}(1+|t|^2)^{-3}d^3t,\quad d(U,U^0)=4\arctan|t|.
\tag{SR42}
\]
On the two cubes \([-r,r]^3\), \(0<r\le1\), the conditional density is at least its centre value times \(e^{-4\sqrt3rT}\). Multiplying \(F_a-c\) by both denominators produces a polynomial \(P(t,t')\) of degree at most two in each coordinate. Its nine mixed degree-two coefficients are exactly \(a_p\partial_{t_\alpha}\partial_{t'_\beta}W_p(0,0)\). Because each stereographic tangent is \(-4T_\alpha\), the original orientations and fixed exterior words act by signs and orthogonal adjoint rotations; the full Frobenius sum is
\[
\sum_{\alpha,\beta}|b_{t_\alpha t'_\beta}|^2
=|a_p|^2[128+16(W_p^0)^2]\ge128|a_p|^2.
\tag{SR43}
\]
Indeed \(\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha T_\beta G_0)=-q_0\delta_{\alpha\beta}/2+\sum_\gamma\varepsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}q_\gamma/2\) for \(G_0=q_0I+i\sum q_\gamma\sigma_\gamma\). Its squared sum is \((3q_0^2+2\sum q_\gamma^2)/4\), multiplied by \(16^2\); \(W_p^0=2q_0\) proves (SR43).

The one-variable Gram matrix of \(1,z,z^2\) on \([-1,1]\) is
\(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}2&0&2/3\\0&2/3&0\\2/3&0&2/5\end{smallmatrix}\right)\ge I/8\).
Subtracting \(I/8\) gives a positive middle entry and a remaining block determinant \(41/576\). Tensor six copies, rescale each variable by \(r\), and retain the mixed coefficients: \(\int|P|^2\ge128\,8^{-6}r^{10}|a_p|^2\). The two Haar densities and squared denominators contribute \((4/\pi^2)^2(1+3r^2)^{-10}\). Averaging all two-link centres divides by their exact translated cube volume squared, at most \([(4/\pi^2)8r^3]^2\). Therefore the actual conditional variance satisfies
\[
\operatorname{Var}(F_a\mid\hbox{outside})
\ge {2\over8^6}{r^4e^{-4\sqrt3rT}\over(1+3r^2)^{10}}|a_p|^2.
\tag{SR44}
\]
Every Haar factor and power is retained; the six-dimensional volume cancels only through the exact centre averaging. This is precisely the GPR proof with the new proved conditional score.

Put
\[
\begin{gathered}
C_*={1\over e2^{37}},\qquad
T_p(x)=\min_{e\ne f\in\partial p}[B_e(x)+B_f(x)],\qquad
\widetilde\eta_p(x)=\max\{e^{-2\pi T_p},C_*(1+4\sqrt3T_p)^{-4}\},\\
\widetilde\Omega(x)=2\pi\sum_eB_e(x),\qquad
\widetilde c_0(x)=\max\{e^{-\widetilde\Omega(x)},[\sum_p\widetilde\eta_p(x)^{-1}]^{-1}\}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SR45}
\]
Choose \(r=(1+4\sqrt3T_p)^{-1}\) in (SR44). Its exponential is at least \(e^{-1}\), and \((1+3r^2)^{10}\le4^{10}\), giving exactly \(C_*\). The law of total variance gives \(\operatorname{Var}_{\mu_x}F_a\ge\widetilde\eta_p|a_p|^2\) for each face. Summing the inequalities \(|a_p|^2\le\operatorname{Var}(F_a)/\widetilde\eta_p\) gives the second branch of \(\widetilde c_0\). The first follows by moving every original edge, obtaining \(\rho_x\ge e^{-\widetilde\Omega}\), and using the original Haar orthonormal face basis. Hence
\[
C(x)\ge\widetilde c_0(x)I_M.
\tag{SR46}
\]
The exact geometry constant is \(C_L^\nabla=\max_p\sum_{e\in\partial p}r_e=16\) for \(m\ge4\): the upper bound is four times four, and an entirely interior face attains it. Cauchy–Schwarz over every incident face, with \(|\nabla_eW_p|\le1\), gives \(\mu_x\Gamma(\bar F_a,F_a)\le16\|a\|_2^2\). Spectral Cauchy–Schwarz on the complement of constants in \(L^2(\mu_x)\) gives
\(\operatorname{Var}(F_a)^2\le(a^*R^{\rm phys}a)\mu_x\Gamma(\bar F_a,F_a)\).
Together with (SR46) this proves
\[
\boxed{R^{\rm phys}(x)\ge\widetilde c_0(x)^2I_M/16,\quad
\|(R^{\rm phys}(x))^{-1}\|\le16\widetilde c_0(x)^{-2},\quad
\|D(m^{-1})\|=\|(2R^{\rm phys})^{-1}\|\le8\widetilde c_0(x)^{-2}.}
\tag{SR47}
\]
The norms are the unchanged Euclidean face-coefficient norms. This does not assign the same polynomial bound to the full physical spectral inverse.

\subsection*{10. Exact face boundary classes and path constants}

For the homogeneous source all \(B_e=B_{r_e}(\xi)\). An original face has third, transverse coordinate \(t\in\{0,\ldots,m\}\). Put \(d=1\) for \(t=0,m\), and \(d=2\) otherwise. Among the two lower coordinates within the face, let \(k\in\{0,1,2\}\) count those in \(\{0,m-1\}\). Exactly \(k\) of its four edges have incidence \(d+1\), and the other \(4-k\) have incidence \(d+2\). The function \(r\mapsto B_r\) is nondecreasing. Selecting the two least-score edges therefore gives the following exact face table:

\begingroup\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*3)/4\relax}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*3)/4\relax}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*3)/4\relax}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*3)/4\relax}@{}}\toprule
Transverse class & \(k\) & Face count, all three orientations & \(T_p\)\\
\(t=0,m\) & 0 & \(6(m-2)^2\) & \(2B_3\)\\
\(t=0,m\) & 1 & \(24(m-2)\) & \(B_2+B_3\)\\
\(t=0,m\) & 2 & \(24\) & \(2B_2\)\\
\(0<t<m\) & 0 & \(3(m-1)(m-2)^2\) & \(2B_4\)\\
\(0<t<m\) & 1 & \(12(m-1)(m-2)\) & \(B_3+B_4\)\\
\(0<t<m\) & 2 & \(12(m-1)\) & \(2B_3\)\\
\bottomrule\end{longtable}\endgroup

For fixed orientation and \(t\), the numbers of the two lower-coordinate choices are \((m-2)^2,4(m-2),4\). Multiplication by two boundary levels or \(m-1\) interior levels and three orientations proves all six counts. They sum to \(3m^2(m+1)=M\). This evaluates exactly the harmonic sum in (SR45), including every boundary face.

For a class with \(T_p\sim tj\), the explicit \(\widetilde\eta_p^{-1}\) is asymptotic to \(C_*^{-1}(4\sqrt3t)^4j^4\). The complete contribution table for \(H_j:=\sum_p\widetilde\eta_p^{-1}\) is therefore

\begingroup\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*2)/3\relax}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*2)/3\relax}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*2)/3\relax}@{}}\toprule
Class in preceding order & Growth power & Limit after division by that power\\
1 & \(j^{12}\) & \(24C_*^{-1}(8\sqrt3b_3)^4\)\\
2 & \(j^8\) & \(48C_*^{-1}[4\sqrt3(b_2+b_3)]^4\)\\
3 & \(j^4\) & \(24C_*^{-1}(8\sqrt3b_2)^4\)\\
4 & \(j^{16}\) & \(24C_*^{-1}(8\sqrt3b_4)^4\)\\
5 & \(j^{12}\) & \(48C_*^{-1}[4\sqrt3(b_3+b_4)]^4\)\\
6 & \(j^8\) & \(24C_*^{-1}(8\sqrt3b_3)^4\)\\
\bottomrule\end{longtable}\endgroup

These follow directly from \(m_j=2j^4\) and the six finite counts. Set
\[
\mathfrak a={1600\sqrt3C_\rho\over\kappa_*}=8\sqrt3b_4.
\tag{SR48}
\]
Then \(H_j/j^{16}\to24C_*^{-1}\mathfrak a^4\). The global exponential floor in (SR45) is eventually smaller than \(H_j^{-1}\), as the explicit oscillation growth proved in the next section shows. Consequently
\[
j^{16}\widetilde c_0(x_j)\to{C_*\over24\mathfrak a^4},\qquad
{16\widetilde c_0(x_j)^{-2}\over j^{32}}
\to9216C_*^{-2}\mathfrak a^8.
\tag{SR49}
\]
The covariance inverse bound has growth power 16 with constant \(24C_*^{-1}\mathfrak a^4\), and the source-coordinate inverse bound in (SR47) has growth power 32 with constant \(4608C_*^{-2}\mathfrak a^8\). Again these are exact limits of the explicit bounds, not asserted limits of the actual matrices.

A shorter finite bound, retaining GPR50–GPR52's uniform-face presentation, follows from \(T_p\le2B_4(\xi_j)\):
\[
\begin{gathered}
\eta_j^{\rm unif}=\max\{e^{-4\pi B_4(\xi_j)},C_*(1+8\sqrt3B_4(\xi_j))^{-4}\},\quad
c_j^{\rm unif}=\max\{e^{-\widetilde\Omega_j},\eta_j^{\rm unif}/M_j\},\\
\boxed{\|(R^{\rm phys}(x_j))^{-1}\|
\le16C_*^{-2}M_j^2[1+8\sqrt3B_4(\xi_j)]^8
\le16C_*^{-2}M_j^2(1+\mathfrak a j)^8=O(j^{32}).}
\end{gathered}\tag{SR50}
\]
Every finite \(B_r\) still contains the original minimum in (SR10); the last inequality uses its square-root branch as an upper bound at every \(j\). The original comparison GPR52 had the factor \((1+128\sqrt3\xi_j)^8\); this exact replacement reduces its growth from \(j^{40}\) to \(j^{32}\).

\subsection*{11. Full physical and union-labelled inverse constants}

Keep the GPR, UL and UF inverse operators, their domains, all label relations and every source restriction. The improved score gives the proved quantities
\[
\widetilde S(x)={1\over2}\left(\sum_eB_e(x)^2\right)^{1/2},\qquad
\widetilde\Omega(x)=2\pi\sum_eB_e(x),\qquad
\|\nabla\log\psi_x\|_\infty\le\widetilde S,
\quad q_\rho:={\max\rho_x\over\min\rho_x}\le e^{\widetilde\Omega}.
\tag{SR51}
\]
The first estimate sums the squares of the pointwise bounds; the second moves one original edge at a time along a shortest geodesic. Since \(\int\rho_x=1\), it also gives \(e^{-\widetilde\Omega}\le\rho_x\le e^{\widetilde\Omega}\). The physical Haar Casimir gap is three: every nonzero gauge-invariant spin support contains a cycle, the shortest original lattice cycle has four edges, and each nontrivial edge contributes at least \(3/4\). Minimizing the weighted variance over constants and applying this original gap gives \(\gamma_x=\delta(x)/\kappa\ge3/q_\rho\). Thus
\[
\delta(x)\ge3\kappa e^{-\widetilde\Omega},\qquad
\|A_x^\dagger\|_{2\to2}\le {e^{\widetilde\Omega}\over3\kappa}.
\tag{SR52}
\]

Write \(P_Hf=\int f\,dU\), \(Q_H=I-P_H\), \(v_x=\log\psi_x-P_H\log\psi_x\). The exact physical inverse is still
\[
\mathscr L_x=I-2K^{-1}Q_H\Gamma(v_x,\cdot),\qquad
\mathscr L_x^{-1}f=Q_H\kappa\psi_x^{-1}A_x^\dagger\psi_x[Kf-\mu_x(Kf)].
\tag{SR53}
\]
It has the original supported or full physical zero-Haar-mean Sobolev domain at every real index, and the GPR Fourier \(Y_1\) domain. Both different means and the factor \(\kappa\) remain necessary. For smooth \(f\), its solution satisfies \(\mathcal L_xh=Kf-\mu_x(Kf)\). Test against \(h-\mu_xh\). Integrating \(Kf\) in Haar measure with its complete density factor gives
\(\int\rho_x\nabla\bar h\cdot\nabla f+2\int\rho_x(\bar h-\mu_x\bar h)\nabla v_x\cdot\nabla f\).
Weighted Cauchy–Schwarz and the weighted gap \(\gamma_x\) bound this by
\((1+2\widetilde S/\sqrt{\gamma_x})\|\nabla h\|_{\mu_x}\|\nabla f\|_{\mu_x}\).
Converting both gradient norms to their original Haar norms yields
\[
\boxed{\|\mathscr L_x^{-1}\|_{\dot H^1_H\to\dot H^1_H}
\le\sqrt{q_\rho}\left(1+{2\widetilde S\over\sqrt{\gamma_x}}\right)
\le e^{\widetilde\Omega/2}\left(1+{2\widetilde S\over\sqrt3}e^{\widetilde\Omega/2}\right).}
\tag{SR54}
\]
Density extends this bound to the complete energy spaces. It is a bound in the specified Haar energy norm, not an asserted estimate in the different \(Y_1\) norm.

For every original edge subset \(A\), retain \((r_Ax)_p=\mathbf1_{\partial p\subseteq A}x_p\) and \(H_A=H(r_Ax)\) on the full product, including its outside kinetic operators. Define
\[
\beta_{e,A}=\sum_{p:e\in\partial p,\,\partial p\subseteq A}|x_p|,\quad
B_{e,A}=\min\{4\beta_{e,A},C_\rho\sqrt{\beta_{e,A}}\},\quad
\widetilde S_A={1\over2}\sqrt{\sum_eB_{e,A}^2},\quad
\widetilde\Omega_A=2\pi\sum_eB_{e,A}.
\tag{SR55}
\]
All of (SR51)–(SR54) apply to its actual vacuum. Monotonicity of both branches of the minimum gives \(B_{e,A}\le B_e\), and hence \(\widetilde S_A\le\widetilde S\), \(\widetilde\Omega_A\le\widetilde\Omega\). The positive subset vacuum is constant outside its active variables by translation invariance and uniqueness; every zero term remains a zero in the original operator.

The full original labelled space is \(\mathfrak B=\prod_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq E}Y_1(S)\), with \(\|F\|_{\rm loc}=\max_e\sum_{S\ni e}\|F_S\|_{Y_1}\), retaining support strictly contained in a label. The Boolean maps are \((\zeta F)_A=\sum_{S\subseteq A}F_S\) and \((\mathfrak mG)_S=\sum_{A\subseteq S}(-1)^{|S\setminus A|}G_A\). The original union-label map obeys
\(\zeta\mathcal B(F,G)_A=K^{-1}Q_H\Gamma((\zeta F)_A,(\zeta G)_A)\).
The exact full inverse consequently remains
\[
(\mathfrak L_x^{-1}F)_S
=\sum_{A\subseteq S}(-1)^{|S\setminus A|}\mathscr L_A^{-1}
\left(\sum_{T\subseteq A}F_T\right).
\tag{SR56}
\]
Every original label, every relation direction, and each supported inverse from (SR53) is retained. The physical support cycle bound forces every nonzero coordinate to have at least four edges. For each fixed anchor the exact counting coefficient \(2^{N-|S\cup\{e\}|}\) is at most
\(2^{N-7}[5\mathbf1_{e\in S}+\sum_{f\in S\setminus\{e\}}1]\).
Summing over anchor capacities proves both Boolean norm bounds \(C_N^{\rm cyc}=(N+4)2^{N-7}\). With the original less precise UL constant \(C_N=(N+1)2^{N-2}\) also retained, the full energy inverse bounds are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\|\mathfrak L_x^{-1}\|_{{\rm loc},\dot H^1_H\to{\rm loc},\dot H^1_H}
&\le C_N^2 e^{\widetilde\Omega/2}
\left(1+{2\widetilde S\over\sqrt3}e^{\widetilde\Omega/2}\right),\\
\|\mathfrak L_x^{-1}\|_{{\rm loc},\dot H^1_H\to{\rm loc},\dot H^1_H}
&\le (N+4)^2 2^{2N-14} e^{\widetilde\Omega/2}
\left(1+{2\widetilde S\over\sqrt3}e^{\widetilde\Omega/2}\right).
\end{aligned}\tag{SR57}
\]
The \(Y_1\) assertion remains exactly \(\|\mathfrak L_x^{-1}\|_{\rm loc}\le(C_N^{\rm cyc})^2\max_A\|\mathscr L_A^{-1}\|_{Y_1(A)}<\infty\), with finiteness proved in GPR and UL. The score alone does not identify this maximum with the energy estimate (SR54).

UF's component factorization gives an additional exact check. If the active-face hypergraph of a subset has disjoint edge components \(S_i\), its actual positive unit vacuum is \(\prod_i\psi_{A\cap S_i}\), with all outside factors one. The logarithms, energies, original Haar means and removed kinetic scalars add exactly. The oscillation of the logarithm is the sum of the component oscillations, because each continuous factor reaches its extrema independently on the compact product. The squared gradient maximum is also the sum of the component squared gradient maxima. Therefore the same score sums (SR55) respect this exact factorization; no scalar or relation-kernel coefficient is removed. The connected-label cancellations of UF8–UF13 continue to hold unchanged.

The exact homogeneous edge multiplicities are \(12m\), \(12m(m-1)\), \(3m(m-1)^2\) for incidences 2, 3, 4. They follow by counting zero, one or two boundary transverse coordinates for each of the three edge orientations. Hence at every finite path point
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widetilde\Omega_j
&=2\pi[12m_jB_2(\xi_j)+12m_j(m_j-1)B_3(\xi_j)
+3m_j(m_j-1)^2B_4(\xi_j)],\\
\widetilde S_j
&={1\over2}\{12m_jB_2(\xi_j)^2+12m_j(m_j-1)B_3(\xi_j)^2
+3m_j(m_j-1)^2B_4(\xi_j)^2\}^{1/2}.
\end{aligned}\tag{SR58}
\]
Eventually the square-root branch holds in all three classes, and the exact expressions become
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widetilde\Omega_j
&={200\pi C_\rho j\over\kappa_*}
[12m_j\sqrt2+12m_j(m_j-1)\sqrt3+6m_j(m_j-1)^2],\\
\widetilde S_j&=C_\rho\sqrt{\xi_jM_j}
={100C_\rho\over\kappa_*}j\sqrt{M_j},\\
{\widetilde\Omega_j\over j^{13}}&\to{9600\pi C_\rho\over\kappa_*},\qquad
{\widetilde S_j\over j^7}\to{200\sqrt6C_\rho\over\kappa_*}.
\end{aligned}\tag{SR59}
\]
The identity for \(\widetilde S_j\) uses the complete incidence count \(\sum_er_e=4M_j\), including boundaries. These eventually exact expressions prove the comparison used in (SR49). Insert (SR58), or their eventual form (SR59), into (SR52), (SR54) and (SR57) to obtain the full finite-path estimates. The logarithm of the explicit physical energy-inverse upper bound divided by \(j^{13}\) tends to \(9600\pi C_\rho/\kappa_*\); the same is true of the explicit UF full-labelled bound, since \(N_j=O(j^{12})\) and the Boolean factor contributes \((2N_j-14)\log2+2\log(N_j+4)\). These are still deteriorating full-space bounds, although their vacuum oscillation exponent improves from order \(j^{14}\) to order \(j^{13}\).

Finally all GPR upper response estimates receive this same oscillation improvement:
\[
R^{\rm phys}(x)\le {\min(4M,\max\rho_x)\over\gamma_x}I_M
\le {4M\over3}e^{\widetilde\Omega(x)}I_M,
\tag{SR60}
\]
and at homogeneous source
\[
C(\xi)\le\min\{4M,12\sqrt{NM}/\sqrt\xi\}I_M,\qquad
R^{\rm phys}(\xi)\le {e^{\widetilde\Omega(\xi)}\over3}
\min\{4M,12\sqrt{NM}/\sqrt\xi\}I_M.
\tag{SR61}
\]
The weak-coupling trial energy, actual deficit bound and integrated identity GPR53–GPR55 are unchanged:
\(E(\xi)\le3\kappa\sqrt{\xi NM}\),
\(\mu_\xi\sum_p(2-W_p)\le3\sqrt{NM}/\sqrt\xi\), and
\(\int_\xi^\infty\mathbf1^tR^{\rm phys}(t)\mathbf1\,dt=\tfrac12\mu_\xi\sum_p(2-W_p)\).
They concern the actual same vacuum; no source, path, potential scalar, marginal factor or support relation has been substituted.

The complete receiving calculation leaves a concrete next mathematical target: control the actual remaining joint expectations in (SR33), or the actual spectral distribution beyond the global quotient estimate (SR40). The presently proved positive correction of order \(j^{-25}\) and negative correction of order \(j^{-23}\) do not alone determine those signed expressions. They provide exact improved terms for that continuing calculation.

\subsection*{12. The stronger proved heat-kernel score and its separate oscillation floor}

The derivation above remains independent of the additional heat-kernel calculation. Subsequently \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/SQUARE_ROOT_LOCAL_SCORE.md}{LS1–LS57} and \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/HEAT_RATIO_PROOF.md}{the independent HR1–HR25 proof} were read completely. Their short-time proofs give another actual-vacuum score and a stronger slice oscillation estimate; HR also proves the all-time heat ratio, though the short-time range suffices here. This section propagates those additional proved estimates through the complete bulk calculation; it does not replace the retained entropy-only proof.

To record the additional analytic input precisely, the original radius-two heat kernel has image representation
\[
p_\tau(\theta)={2\sqrt\pi e^{\tau/4}\over\tau^{3/2}}
{\sum_{k\in\mathbb Z}(\theta+2\pi k)e^{-(\theta+2\pi k)^2/\tau}\over\sin\theta},
\qquad0\le\theta\le\pi.
\tag{SR62}
\]
It follows from the character series with eigenvalues \(\ell(\ell+2)/4\) by Gaussian periodization and differentiation, retaining the full multiplier. The radial derivative is nonpositive: the derivative of the heat solution from \(e^{\cos\theta/\epsilon}\), divided by its integral, solves
\(\partial_\tau v=\tfrac14(v''+2\cot\theta\,v'-2\csc^2\theta\,v)\),
with zero endpoint values and nonpositive initial values. A first positive maximum would occur in the interior and contradict this equation; the singular endpoint coefficients are not used at that maximum. The positive-time smooth kernel limit as \(\epsilon\downarrow0\) preserves the sign. Its maximum and minimum are therefore at 0 and \(\pi\). The endpoint image sums, retaining their signs, give for \(0<\tau\le\pi^2\)
\[
{p_\tau(0)\over A_\tau}
=1+2\sum_{k\ge1}(1-8\pi^2k^2/\tau)e^{-4\pi^2k^2/\tau}\le1,
\quad
{p_\tau(\pi)\over A_\tau}
=2\sum_{k\ge0}[2(2k+1)^2\pi^2/\tau-1]e^{-(2k+1)^2\pi^2/\tau}
\ge2(2\pi^2/\tau-1)e^{-\pi^2/\tau},
\tag{SR63}
\]
where \(A_\tau=2\sqrt\pi e^{\tau/4}/\tau^{3/2}\). Thus the global kernel ratio is at most \(e^{\pi^2/\tau}\). The actual-vacuum Feynman–Kac comparison retains the same outside-path integral in numerator and denominator, so its one-edge ratio is at most \(e^{4\beta_e\tau}\) times this kernel ratio. For \(\beta_e\ge1\) choose \(\tau=\pi/(2\sqrt{\beta_e})\le\pi^2\); for \(\beta_e\le1\) use (SR6)'s score and diameter. It proves
\[
\operatorname{osc}_e\log\psi_x\le4\pi\min\{\beta_e,\sqrt{\beta_e}\}.
\tag{SR64}
\]
This argument extends to every real source in (SR1). For signed sources the incident potential lies between the two constants
\(\kappa\sum_{p\ni e}(2x_p-2|x_p|)\) and
\(\kappa\sum_{p\ni e}(2x_p+2|x_p|)\).
Their difference is exactly \(4\kappa\beta_e\), and both are independent of the outside path. Using these two constants in the full Feynman–Kac sandwich gives the same ratio \(e^{4\beta_e\tau}\), without discarding any original \(2\kappa x_p\). All martingale, positivity and domination statements in Section 1 apply unchanged. Thus this signed-source extension is proved here rather than assumed from the homogeneous statement in LS.

Using (SR64) in the already verified exact directional entropy formula (SR5), at time \(\tau=2/\sqrt{\beta_e}\), yields
\[
C_{\rm heat}=2+2\sqrt{4+2\pi},\quad
\lambda_e(x)=\min\{4\beta_e(x),C_{\rm heat}\sqrt{\beta_e(x)}\},\quad
\omega_e^{\rho}(x)=8\pi\min\{\beta_e(x),\sqrt{\beta_e(x)}\},
\tag{SR65}
\]
with \(|\nabla_e\log\rho_x|\le\lambda_e\) and
\(\operatorname{osc}_e\log\rho_x\le\omega_e^\rho\).
At \(\beta_e=0\), translation invariance proves both to be zero. For \(\beta_e>0\), the substitution in (SR5) is exactly
\(\sqrt{(4\pi\sqrt\beta)/(2/\sqrt\beta)+4\beta}+(2/\sqrt\beta)\beta/2=(1+\sqrt{4+2\pi})\sqrt\beta\).
Conditional scores and oscillations inherit the same bounds. The marginal score follows from (SR7), and its ratio bound follows by integrating the pointwise inequality \(\rho_x(u,z)\le e^{\omega_e^\rho}\rho_x(v,z)\) in \(z\). Thus all actual chord conditionals and marginals are covered.

For homogeneous source put \(\lambda_r=\min\{4r\xi,C_{\rm heat}\sqrt{r\xi}\}\), \(\omega_r=8\pi\min\{r\xi,\sqrt{r\xi}\}\). Define all four distinct factors
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat\Theta_{2,r}&=\max\{e^{-\omega_r},C_2e^{-\lambda_rR_8(\lambda_r)}R_8(\lambda_r)^8\},\\
\widehat\Theta_{1,r}&=\max\{e^{-\omega_r},C_1e^{-\lambda_rR_2(\lambda_r)}R_2(\lambda_r)^2\},\\
\widehat\Theta_{6,r}&=\max\{e^{-\omega_r},C_6e^{-\lambda_rR_6(\lambda_r)}R_6(\lambda_r)^6\},\\
\widehat\Psi_{6,r}&=\max\{2e^{-\omega_r},c_6e^{-\lambda_rR_6(\lambda_r)}R_6(\lambda_r)^6\}.
\end{aligned}\tag{SR66}
\]
The mass and special-vector floors have coefficient one; the complete gradient floor has coefficient two. This distinction is forced by the full Haar Casimir identity and must remain in the receiving formulas.

Every identity of original rows, words and states (SR11)–(SR20) is unchanged. The exact receiving substitutions are
\[
\Theta_2(B_r)\mapsto\widehat\Theta_{2,r},\qquad
\Theta_1(B_r)\mapsto\widehat\Theta_{1,r},\qquad
\Theta_6(B_r)\mapsto\widehat\Theta_{6,r},\qquad
\Psi_6(B_r)\mapsto\widehat\Psi_{6,r}.
\tag{SR67}
\]
They are justified independently for each complete polynomial, vector, derivative and actual marginal by its finite-radius score comparison and its now stronger density floor. In particular the full bulk and disjoint boundary bounds are explicitly
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widehat A_D&={3J_n\over n^5}[2\cos^2h\,\widehat\Psi_{6,3}\widehat\Theta_{1,2}
+J_n\widehat\Psi_{6,4}\widehat\Theta_{1,3}],\\
\widehat A_F&={2J_n\over n^5}[2\cos^2h\,\widehat\Psi_{6,3}(1+\tfrac12\widehat\Theta_{1,2})
+J_n\widehat\Psi_{6,4}(1+\tfrac12\widehat\Theta_{1,3})],\\
\widehat B_6&={3\cos^2(3h)\over n^5}[2\cos^2h\,\widehat\Theta_{6,3}+J_n\widehat\Theta_{6,4}],\\
\mu(|\nabla D|^2)&\ge\widehat B_6+\widehat A_D,\quad
C_F\ge\kappa\beta_0^2(\widehat B_6+\widehat A_F).
\end{aligned}\tag{SR68}
\]
The conditional formulas (SR21) receive \(\widehat\Psi\) and keep their actual \(\mu c^2\) and \(\mu|q|^2\) before the explicit marginal lower bound. All raw masses and Gram factors in (SR17) and all selected-row sums (SR26) receive exactly (SR67); no rows are added to the same mass or counted twice in the energy.

Set
\[
\widehat b_r={100C_{\rm heat}\sqrt r\over\kappa_*},\quad
\widehat T_{2,r}=C_2(8/(e\widehat b_r))^8,\quad
\widehat T_{1,r}=C_1(2/(e\widehat b_r))^2,\quad
\widehat V_r=C_6(6/(e\widehat b_r))^6,\quad
\widehat P_r=c_6(6/(e\widehat b_r))^6.
\tag{SR69}
\]
Eventually \(\lambda_r(\xi_j)=\widehat b_rj\), while \(\omega_r(\xi_j)=800\pi\sqrt r\,j/\kappa_*\). The exponential floors, including the gradient's factor two, are eventually smaller than the respective optimized polynomial factors. Therefore every table in Section 6 holds with exactly the same exponents and with the constants replaced by their hats. This assertion follows term by term from the eventual equalities of the four factors, with no other altered quantity. The full original endpoint statements are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal E_j(s_j)&\le G_j-j\beta_{0,j}^2(\widehat B_{6,j}+\widehat A_{F,j}),\\
\mathcal E_j(s_j)&\ge4\beta_{0,j}^2[Q_{4,j}-jK_{1,j}]-\beta_{0,j}^2\mu_j(D_j^2)
+j\beta_{0,j}^2(\widehat B_{6,j}+\widehat A_{D,j}),\\
\lim_jj^{23}j\beta_{0,j}^2(\widehat B_{6,j}+\widehat A_{F,j})
&={625\pi^2\over2\kappa_*^2}\widehat P_4,\\
\lim_jj^{25}j\beta_{0,j}^2(\widehat B_{6,j}+\widehat A_{D,j})
&={1875\pi^2\over4\kappa_*^2}\widehat P_4\widehat T_{1,3}.
\end{aligned}\tag{SR70}
\]
Thus the stronger score alone changes the constants, whereas completing the LS receiving calculation with the PG bulk contribution changes the leading positive correction from LS56's valid boundary-derived order \(j^{-27}\) to the larger order \(j^{-25}\). This is a strengthening of the available lower bound, not a correction to the valid selected-row estimate. The negative mass and both original cross expectations remain in (SR70).

For the raw quotient (SR37)–(SR40), substitute \(\lambda_r\) into \(b_2\); all three growth powers remain 6, 10 and 14, with \(C_\rho^2\) replaced by \(C_{\rm heat}^2\) in their exact constants. Its direct subgroup proof depends on the score; the new global floor is unnecessary for that inequality.

The actual face-conditioning proof (SR42)–(SR44) receives \(\lambda_e+\lambda_f\) in its local exponential, while its global density floor receives the different \(\omega_e^\rho+\omega_f^\rho\). Preserve both by defining
\[
\begin{gathered}
\eta_p^{\rm heat}(x)=\max_{e\ne f\in\partial p}
\max\{e^{-(\omega_e^\rho+\omega_f^\rho)},
C_*[1+4\sqrt3(\lambda_e+\lambda_f)]^{-4}\},\\
\Omega^{\rm heat}(x)=\sum_e\omega_e^\rho(x),\quad
S^{\rm heat}(x)={1\over2}\sqrt{\sum_e\lambda_e(x)^2},\quad
c_0^{\rm heat}(x)=\max\{e^{-\Omega^{\rm heat}(x)},[\sum_p(\eta_p^{\rm heat})^{-1}]^{-1}\}.
\end{gathered}\tag{SR71}
\]
The maximum over the finite original pairs permits different pairs to optimize the floor and polynomial branch at signed inhomogeneous sources. Both resulting variance bounds are valid separately, so their maximum is valid. At homogeneous source both functions are nondecreasing in incidence; the six exact face classes of Section 10 apply with \(B_r\) replaced by \(\lambda_r\) in the local branch and their corresponding two \(\omega\) values in the floor. Hence (SR46)–(SR50) hold with these heat superscripts, \(\mathfrak a\) replaced by \(\mathfrak a_{\rm heat}=1600\sqrt3C_{\rm heat}/\kappa_*\), and the same powers and numerical factors 24, 9216 and 4608. Every actual covariance/response and Euclidean norm is unchanged.

For the full physical and full-labelled inverse bounds, replace only \((\widetilde S,\widetilde\Omega)\) by \((S^{\rm heat},\Omega^{\rm heat})\) in (SR51)–(SR61). For each subset use its own \(\beta_{e,A}\) in (SR65); both functions are monotone in \(\beta\), so the subset maximum is controlled by the full-source values exactly as before. The original Boolean factors, Haar-energy norms, scalar terms, supports and coefficient-norm distinction remain intact. On the path the eventually exact expressions and limits are
\[
\begin{aligned}
\Omega_j^{\rm heat}
&={800\pi j\over\kappa_*}[12m_j\sqrt2+12m_j(m_j-1)\sqrt3+6m_j(m_j-1)^2],\\
S_j^{\rm heat}&={100C_{\rm heat}\over\kappa_*}j\sqrt{M_j},\\
{\Omega_j^{\rm heat}\over j^{13}}&\to{38400\pi\over\kappa_*},\qquad
{S_j^{\rm heat}\over j^7}\to{200\sqrt6C_{\rm heat}\over\kappa_*}.
\end{aligned}\tag{SR72}
\]
The direct oscillation estimate (SR64) is essential here: replacing only the score in the diameter argument would instead give the weaker coefficient \(9600\pi C_{\rm heat}/\kappa_*\). The exact local oscillations improve that coefficient to \(38400\pi/\kappa_*\). This improvement applies to the exponential full-space estimates; it does not turn them into the polynomial face-response bound.

\section*{Full rooted words, actual curvature, and the original phase}

This note extends the existing PK177a–PK177f calculation. Those results
already give the two face supports, their unsigned incidence weights,
and a fourth-moment estimate. The additions here are the complete matrix
transport factors and their order, an exact signed summation identity,
a stronger actual-vacuum phase bound, and an explicit configuration
family showing why a mean curvature estimate by itself cannot force
the original rooted phase to be small. The actual vacuum is used in
every expectation stated as a vacuum expectation.

\subsection*{CW1. The unchanged box, operator, vacuum and rows}

Let \(L\ge2\) be an integer, \(m=2L\), \(n=m+1\), and
\(\Lambda=\{0,\ldots,m\}^3\). Its physical vertex is
\(o+a(i-L,k-L,l-L)\). A positively oriented link is
\((i,k,l;d)\), with coordinate \(d\) less than \(m\); reverse
traversal uses its inverse matrix. Write \(U_d(x)\in SU(2)\).
There are exactly
\[
 N=3mn^2,\qquad M=3m^2n.                                    \tag{CW1}
\]
The original generator has orthonormal
\(T_\alpha=-i\sigma_\alpha/2\), with the radius-two metric.
The original Hamiltonian and its positive unit vacuum are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 H&=-\kappa\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}^2+bW,\qquad
 W=\sum_{p\in\mathsf P}D_p,\quad D_p=2-\operatorname{tr}P_p,\\
 \kappa&=2g^2/a,\qquad b=1/(2g^2a),\qquad
 \xi=b/\kappa=1/(4g^4),\\
 H\psi&=E_0\psi,\quad \psi>0,\quad\int\psi^2dH^{\otimes N}=1,
 \qquad d\mu=\psi^2dH^{\otimes N}.
 \end{aligned}                                                 \tag{CW2}
\]
Every \(D_p\) is in \([0,4]\). The scalar \(2bM\) remains in
the potential and in the vacuum equation. All faces are present.

The tree consists of every direction-one link, direction-two links
at \(i=0\), and direction-three links at \(i=k=0\). Define the
following products in increasing order from left to right:
\[
 C_l=\prod_{r=0}^{l-1}U_3(0,0,r),\quad
 B_k(l)=\prod_{b=0}^{k-1}U_2(0,b,l),\quad
 A_i(k,l)=\prod_{a=0}^{i-1}U_1(a,k,l),\quad
 t_{i,k,l}=C_lB_k(l)A_i(k,l).                                \tag{CW3}
\]
Every empty product is \(I\). For every positive link define
\(Z_d(x)=t_xU_d(x)t_{x+e_d}^{-1}\). Tree values are identically
\(I\); all other values are the original chord words. In particular
\[
 \mathcal C_2=\{1\le i\le m,\ 0\le k<m,\ 0\le l\le m\},\quad
 \mathcal C_3=\{0\le i,k\le m,\ (i,k)\ne(0,0),\ 0\le l<m\}.
                                                               \tag{CW4}
\]
The rooted words are retained as the matrix expressions CW3–CW4,
including every repeated root segment.

For \(U=cI+\sum q^\alpha T_\alpha\), let
\(q(U)=(q^1,q^2,q^3)\), so \(|q(U)|\le2\). Put
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 h=\pi/(2n),\quad s=\sin h,\quad c=\cos h,\quad C=\cot h,\\
 v_0(r)=n^{-1/2},\quad v(r)=\sqrt{2/n}\cos[\pi(r+1/2)/n],\\
 w(r)=-\sqrt{2/n}\sin[\pi(r+1)/n]\quad(0\le r<m),
 \quad w(-1)=w(m)=0,\\
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}=-v(i)w(k)v_0(l)/\sqrt2,\quad a_{(i,k,l;3)}=0,\\
 b_{(i,k,l;2)}=v_0(i)w(k)v(l)/\sqrt2,\quad
 b_{(i,k,l;3)}=-v_0(i)v(k)w(l)/\sqrt2.
 \end{gathered}                                                \tag{CW5}
\]
The scalar \(c=\cos h\) in CW5 is not a chord label. Keep exactly
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 u_1=\sum_c a_cq(Z_c),\quad u_3=\sum_c b_cq(Z_c),\quad
 \mathcal S=u_1\cdot u_3,\quad
 \mathcal S_{\mathcal R}=\mathcal S\circ\mathcal R,\\
 D=\mathcal S+\mathcal S_{\mathcal R},\quad
 \beta_0=\sqrt8\sin h/(2g^2),\quad
 F=\beta_0(\mathcal S-\mathcal S_{\mathcal R}).
 \end{gathered}                                                \tag{CW6}
\]
Here \(\mathcal R\) is the original direction-one reflection,
including inverses on reflected direction-one links.

\subsection*{CW2. Explicit ordered plaquette factorization}

For \(d<e\), the positively oriented face based at \(x\) is
\[
 P_{de}(x)=U_d(x)U_e(x+e_d)U_d(x+e_e)^{-1}U_e(x)^{-1},
 \qquad \widehat P_{de}(x)=t_xP_{de}(x)t_x^{-1}.             \tag{CW7}
\]
The exact rooted identities, with decreasing indices from left
to right, are
\[
 \boxed{Z_2(i,k,l)=
 \widehat P_{12}(i-1,k,l)\widehat P_{12}(i-2,k,l)
       \cdots\widehat P_{12}(0,k,l),}                        \tag{CW8}
\]
\[
 \boxed{Z_3(i,k,l)=
 [\widehat P_{13}(i-1,k,l)\cdots\widehat P_{13}(0,k,l)]
 [\widehat P_{23}(0,k-1,l)\cdots\widehat P_{23}(0,0,l)].}  \tag{CW9}
\]
Every transport in these products is the full original \(t_x\)
in CW3. For example a direction-13 factor is
\(C_lB_k(l)A_a(k,l)P_{13}(a,k,l)
A_a(k,l)^{-1}B_k(l)^{-1}C_l^{-1}\).

Here is a direct proof that checks the order. Cancellation of
successive endpoint transports in CW7 gives
\[
 \widehat P_{de}(x)=
 Z_d(x)Z_e(x+e_d)Z_d(x+e_e)^{-1}Z_e(x)^{-1}.               \tag{CW10}
\]
Since every \(Z_1\) is \(I\), CW10 gives
\(Z_2(i+1,k,l)=\widehat P_{12}(i,k,l)Z_2(i,k,l)\), with
\(Z_2(0,k,l)=I\). Induction proves CW8. The same calculation
gives \(Z_3(i+1,k,l)=\widehat P_{13}(i,k,l)Z_3(i,k,l)\).
At \(i=0\), every \(Z_2\) is \(I\), so
\(Z_3(0,k+1,l)=\widehat P_{23}(0,k,l)Z_3(0,k,l)\), with
\(Z_3(0,0,l)=I\). These two inductions give CW9. They explain
why attaching 23 faces first and then 13 faces gives the latter
factors on the left of the final matrix product. Nothing is
commuted. The cancellations prove the identity between the full
rooted words and the full transported faces; they do not remove
root occurrences from any physical derivative count.

All boundary cases are included:

\begingroup\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}\begin{longtable}{@{}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*1)/2\relax}p{\dimexpr(\linewidth-6pt*1)/2\relax}@{}}\toprule
Original link or face class & Exact range or value\\
Direction-two tree link & \(i=0\): CW8 is the empty product \(I\).\\
Direction-two chord & \(1\le i\le m\), \(0\le k<m\), \(0\le l\le m\), including both \(l=0,m\).\\
Direction-three tree link & \(i=k=0\): both products in CW9 are empty.\\
Direction-three chord at \(i=0,k>0\) & Only the full boundary-23 product remains.\\
Direction-three chord at \(k=0,i>0\) & Only the full 13 product remains.\\
Direction-three chord at \(i,k>0\) & Both products remain, in the displayed order, including \(i=m\) or \(k=m\).\\
Face-12 strip & \((a,k,l;12)\), \(0\le a<i\), including the two transverse boundary heights \(l=0,m\).\\
Face-13 strip & \((a,k,l;13)\), \(0\le a<i\), \(0\le l<m\), including \(k=0,m\).\\
Face-23 strip & \((0,b,l;23)\), \(0\le b<k\), \(0\le l<m\): the original boundary plane \(i=0\).\\
\bottomrule\end{longtable}\endgroup

Reversing any rooted chord gives exactly the reversed factor
order with every factor inverted. Face defects are unchanged
by inverse or conjugation because the \(SU(2)\) trace is real.

\subsection*{CW3. Reflection with its original transports and orientations}

Put \(\bar x=(m-i,k,l)\). The reflected link matrices are
\[
 U_1^{\mathcal R}(i,k,l)=U_1(m-i-1,k,l)^{-1},\quad
 U_d^{\mathcal R}(i,k,l)=U_d(m-i,k,l),\ d=2,3.              \tag{CW11}
\]
The full reflected root transport is
\[
 t^{\mathcal R}_{i,k,l}=
 \left[\prod_{r=0}^{l-1}U_3(m,0,r)\right]
 \left[\prod_{b=0}^{k-1}U_2(m,b,l)\right]
 \left[\prod_{a=0}^{i-1}U_1(m-a-1,k,l)^{-1}\right].        \tag{CW12}
\]
Thus no boundary chord in the first two products is fixed to
identity. For \(e=2,3\), and \(y=(m-a-1,k,l)\), direct insertion
in CW7 gives
\[
 P_{1e}^{\mathcal R}(a,k,l)
 =U_1(y)^{-1}P_{1e}(y)^{-1}U_1(y),\qquad
 P_{23}^{\mathcal R}(i,k,l)=P_{23}(m-i,k,l).                \tag{CW13}
\]
Applying CW8–CW9 to the matrices CW11 gives the full reflected
factorization with factors
\(t_x^{\mathcal R}P_{de}^{\mathcal R}(x)(t_x^{\mathcal R})^{-1}\)
in exactly the same decreasing index order. CW13 identifies their
original physical faces and orientations. In particular the 23
boundary strip moves to \(i=m\), and the 12 and 13 strips use
\(m-i\le a<m\), with reversed face orientation. These formulas
prove the actual reflection map rather than replacing it by a
sign on the quaternion coordinates.

\subsection*{CW4. What the retained mean defect really states}

Define the actual total expectation
\(\mathfrak d=\mu(W)\). The source's equations (93)–(99),
with \(r=N/M=n/m\), give
\[
 \mathfrak d\le B_{L,g}:=\min\{2M,6g^2\sqrt{NM}\},\qquad
 \mathfrak d/M\le\min\{2,6g^2\sqrt{n/m}\}.                \tag{CW14}
\]
For completeness, this is a bound on the actual vacuum obtained
as follows. Probability Haar has marginal
\((2/\pi)\sqrt{1-x^2}\,dx\) for \(x=\operatorname{tr}U/2\).
For \(Z_t=\int e^{2tx}dH\), let
\(f_t=Z_t^{-1/2}e^{tx}\) and
\(q_t=Z_t^{-1}\int xe^{2tx}dH\). Pairing \(x\) and \(-x\)
gives \(0<q_t<1\). With \(z=1-x\), the tilted density is a
Gamma density proportional to \(e^{-2tz}z^{1/2}\), multiplied
by the decreasing nonnegative factor
\(\sqrt{2-z}\mathbf1_{[0,2]}\). For independent \(z,z'\),
the integral of \((z-z')[w(z)-w(z')]\) is nonpositive; expanding
it proves that multiplication by \(w\) lowers the mean.
Integration by parts gives the Gamma mean \(3/(4t)\). Hence
\(1-q_t\le3/(4t)\).

The original radius-two identities are
\(|\nabla x|^2=(1-x^2)/4\), \(\Delta x=-3x/4\). Integrating
\(\operatorname{div}(e^{2tx}\nabla x)\) gives one-link kinetic
energy \(3tq_t/8\). The product comparison vector has exact
energy
\(3\kappa Ntq_t/8+2bM(1-q_t^4)\), at most
\(3\kappa Nt/8+6bM/t\). It is used only in the variational
principle, not as the density in any later expectation. The choice
\(t=4\sqrt{bM/(\kappa N)}\) and \(\kappa b=a^{-2}\) gives
\(E_0\le3\sqrt{NM}/a\); the constant comparison gives
\(E_0\le2bM\). Finally \(b\mathfrak d=\mu(V)\le E_0\)
proves CW14 with every face, boundary count and scalar retained.
If a gauge-invariant comparison vector is desired, average its
squared density over the vertex gauge group and take its positive
square root. Cauchy–Schwarz in that group integral bounds its
kinetic form by the original product form, while gauge invariance
leaves the potential integral unchanged.

There is an exact useful symmetry which is weaker than facewise
uniformity. Coordinate permutations act by link permutations and,
when needed, inversion. They preserve product Haar, the link
metric and the whole Hamiltonian. The strictly positive ground
state is unique: positivity improvement of the heat semigroup
with its bounded positive Feynman–Kac weight gives simplicity of
its largest eigenvalue, hence of the ground eigenvalue of \(H\).
Each coordinate symmetry therefore fixes the positive unit
\(\psi\). It follows that, with
\(W_{de}=\sum_{p\text{ of type }de}D_p\),
\[
 \mu(W_{12})=\mu(W_{13})=\mu(W_{23})=\mathfrak d/3.
                                                               \tag{CW15}
\]
This makes no assertion that two faces at inequivalent boundary
locations have the same expectation.

\subsection*{CW5. Exact chord inequalities before a global budget is inserted}

For any \(U\in SU(2)\),
\[
 \|I-U\|_{\rm op}^2=D(U),\quad
 \|I-U\|_{\rm HS}^2=2D(U),\quad
 |q(U)|^2=D(U)[4-D(U)].                                     \tag{CW16}
\]
These follow by diagonalizing its two eigenvalues
\(e^{i\theta},e^{-i\theta}\), or by its quaternion coordinates.
For a full ordered product of unitary matrices, iterating
\(I-AB=(I-A)+A(I-B)\) gives
\[
 \sqrt{D(Z_c)}\le\sum_{p\in\mathcal F(c)}\sqrt{D_p},
 \qquad
 \mathcal F(c_2)=\{(a,k,l;12):0\le a<i\},
\]
\[
 \mathcal F(c_3)=\{(a,k,l;13):0\le a<i\}
                \cup\{(0,b,l;23):0\le b<k\}.              \tag{CW17}
\]
CW8–CW9 prove exactly why these faces, with their conjugating
transports, occur. Their cardinalities are \(i\) and \(i+k\).

Let \(d_p=\mu(D_p)\) retain each individual face expectation.
Minkowski's inequality in \(L^2(\mu)\), applied before replacing
any \(d_p\), gives
\[
 \mu(D(Z_c))\le b_c:=
 \min\left\{4,\left(\sum_{p\in\mathcal F(c)}\sqrt{d_p}\right)^2\right\}.
                                                               \tag{CW18}
\]
Because \(x(4-x)\) is concave on \([0,4]\), Jensen's inequality
and CW16 imply the stronger quaternion estimate
\[
 \mu(|q(Z_c)|^2)\le Q(b_c),\qquad
 Q(b)=\begin{cases}4b-b^2,&0\le b\le2,\\4,&2\le b\le4.\end{cases}
                                                               \tag{CW19}
\]
Indeed maximize \(4d-d^2\) over \(0\le d\le b_c\).
CW15 gives the following completely evaluated alternatives:
\[
 b_{c_2}\le\min\{4,i\mathfrak d/3\},\qquad
 b_{c_3}\le\min\{4,(\sqrt i+\sqrt k)^2\mathfrak d/3\}.
                                                               \tag{CW20}
\]
For CW20, apply Cauchy–Schwarz within each of the two orientation
strips, then use its inclusion in the full orientation sum. The
same bounds hold for reflected chords by CW11–CW15. The more
informative CW18 keeps all individual boundary-face data.

The actual second moments of the original rows therefore also obey
\[
 \|u_\nu\|_{L^2(\mu)}
 \le\sum_c|\mathsf M_{\nu c}|\sqrt{Q(b_c)},\qquad \nu=1,3.
                                                               \tag{CW21}
\]
This is a quantitative result for the actual vacuum, with its
complete face-expectation vector still visible.

\subsection*{CW6. A signed summation that improves the first original row}

The exact one-dimensional identities are
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^m v(i)=0,\quad \sum_{r=0}^{m-1}w(r)^2=1,
 \quad v(i)=\frac{w(i-1)-w(i)}{2s},
 \quad \sum_{i=a+1}^m v(i)=\frac{w(a)}{2s}.                \tag{CW22}
\]
The difference identity follows from the sine subtraction formula;
the two sums follow by summing it and by the finite cosine
geometric series for \(\sin^2\). For any vector sequence
\(Q_0,\ldots,Q_m\), expansion of its successive differences gives
\[
 \sum_{i=0}^m v(i)Q_i
 =\frac1{2s}\sum_{a=0}^{m-1}w(a)(Q_{a+1}-Q_a).            \tag{CW23}
\]
Both boundary terms in the finite summation are retained through
\(w(-1)=w(m)=0\). Use \(Q_i=q(Z_2(i,k,l))\); its \(i=0\)
value is exactly zero because that link is a tree link. Then
the original first row, with no coefficient changed, is
\[
 \boxed{u_1=-\frac1{2s\sqrt{2n}}
 \sum_{a=0}^{m-1}\sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sum_{l=0}^m
 w(a)w(k)[q(Z_2(a+1,k,l))-q(Z_2(a,k,l))].}                 \tag{CW24}
\]
CW8 implies
\(Z_2(a+1,k,l)=\widehat P_{12}(a,k,l)Z_2(a,k,l)\).
The quaternion-vector map is real linear and satisfies
\(|q(A)-q(B)|\le\sqrt2\|A-B\|_{\rm HS}\) for two quaternion
matrices. Unitary invariance and CW16 therefore give
\[
 |q(Z_2(a+1,k,l))-q(Z_2(a,k,l))|\le2\sqrt{D_{12}(a,k,l)}.
                                                               \tag{CW25}
\]
Define, on every actual 12 face,
\[
 K_{a,k,l}=\frac{|w(a)w(k)|}{2s\sqrt{2n}},\qquad
 \sum_{a,k,l}K_{a,k,l}^2=\frac1{8s^2}.                    \tag{CW26}
\]
The sum in CW26 uses \(m\) values of \(a,k\) and all \(n\)
values of \(l\), including both boundary heights. Equations
CW24–CW26 and Cauchy–Schwarz now prove the pointwise and actual
vacuum estimates
\[
 \boxed{|u_1|^2\le\frac{W_{12}}{2s^2},\qquad
 \mu(|u_1|^2)\le\frac{\mathfrak d}{6s^2}
                 \le\frac{B_{L,g}}{6s^2}.}               \tag{CW27}
\]
Before CW15 is used, the more precise bound is
\(\|u_1\|_{L^2}\le2\sum_{a,k,l}K_{a,k,l}\sqrt{d_{12}(a,k,l)}\).
This is stronger than taking absolute values of each original
coefficient before summing the full strip. The exact signed
tail in CW22 is the reason for the improvement.

\subsection*{CW7. The complete third-row curl identity and its nonlinear term}

One can perform signed summation for both differences in the
third row. Include tree values \(Z_2(0,k,l)=I\) and
\(Z_3(0,0,l)=I\); their \(q\) is zero, so this inclusion is
an exact notation for the original excluded entries. CW23 gives
\[
 u_3=\frac1{2s\sqrt{2n}}\sum_{i=0}^m\sum_{k,l=0}^{m-1}
 w(k)w(l)\{q(Z_2(i,k,l+1))-q(Z_2(i,k,l))
           -q(Z_3(i,k+1,l))+q(Z_3(i,k,l))\}.              \tag{CW28}
\]
For one summand put
\(A=Z_2(i,k,l)\), \(B=Z_3(i,k+1,l)\),
\(C'=Z_2(i,k,l+1)\), \(D'=Z_3(i,k,l)\), and
\(P=\widehat P_{23}(i,k,l)=AB(C')^{-1}(D')^{-1}\).
Then its braces are exactly
\[
 \boxed{-q((P-I)D'C')
       +q((A-I)(B-I))-q((D'-I)(C'-I)).}                   \tag{CW29}
\]
Here \(q\) denotes the same real-linear quaternion-vector map,
extended to the real quaternion matrix algebra. To prove CW29,
use \(AB=PD'C'\) and expand
\(AB=A+B-I+(A-I)(B-I)\) and
\(D'C'=D'+C'-I+(D'-I)(C'-I)\). Subtraction gives CW29
without interchanging any factors.

Let \(u_{3,\mathrm{curv}}\) be the full CW28 sum of the first
term of CW29, and \(R_3\) its full sum of the last two terms.
This defines an exact equality \(u_3=u_{3,\mathrm{curv}}+R_3\).
The first part obeys
\[
 |u_{3,\mathrm{curv}}|^2\le\frac{W_{23}}{2s^2},\qquad
 \|u_{3,\mathrm{curv}}\|_{L^2}\le\sqrt{\mathfrak d/(6s^2)}.
                                                               \tag{CW30}
\]
This follows by the same squared coefficient sum as CW26,
now summed over all \(n\) values of \(i\). The original
quadratic term \(R_3\) is not set to zero.

There is also a closed bound for that term. A quaternion matrix
has operator norm equal to its Euclidean quaternion norm, so
\[
 |q((A-I)(B-I))|\le2\sqrt{D(A)D(B)}\le4\sqrt{D(A)},
\]
and the other term is at most \(4\sqrt{D(C')}\). Insert the
complete 12 fillings CW17 for \(A,C'\). The resulting weights
on a face \((a,k,r;12)\) are
\[
 H_{a,k,r}=\frac{(m-a)|w(k)|(|w(r)|+|w(r-1)|)}{2s\sqrt{2n}}.
\]
All endpoint values use \(w(-1)=w(m)=0\). Thus
\[
 |R_3|\le4\sum_{a,k,r}H_{a,k,r}\sqrt{D_{12}(a,k,r)},\quad
 \sum H_{a,k,r}^2=\frac{m(2m+1)c^2}{12s^2}=:\mathcal H.
                                                               \tag{CW31}
\]
For the squared sum, \(\sum_{a=0}^{m-1}(m-a)^2=mn(2m+1)/6\),
\(\sum_kw(k)^2=1\), and
\(\sum_{r=0}^m(w(r)+w(r-1))^2=4c^2\). The latter identity
follows either from the sine addition formula or by expanding
the square and using \(\sum_{r=1}^{m-1}w(r)w(r-1)=\cos(2h)\).
Consequently
\[
 \boxed{\|R_3\|_{L^2}\le4\sqrt{\mathcal H\mathfrak d/3},\qquad
 \|u_3\|_{L^2}\le\sqrt{\mathfrak d/(6s^2)}
                          +4\sqrt{\mathcal H\mathfrak d/3}.}   \tag{CW32}
\]
This closes the estimate using the given mean defect. CW29
also identifies exactly which nonlinear rooted products a finer
calculation would have to control; none has been replaced by an
abelian expression in obtaining CW32.

\subsection*{CW8. Concrete stronger bounds for the unchanged phase and endpoint}

The exact absolute row sums, from CW5, are
\[
 S_1=\sqrt{2/n}\,C(C-c),\qquad
 S_3=\sqrt{2/n}[(2-1/n)C^2-(c/n)C].                       \tag{CW33}
\]
To verify them, finite geometric series give
\(\sum|v|=\sum|w|=\sqrt{2/n}C\); the omitted direction-two
tree value subtracts \(|v(0)|=\sqrt{2/n}c\), and the excluded
direction-three corner subtracts exactly
\(\sqrt{2/n}cC/n\). Thus \(|u_\nu|\le2S_\nu\).

Define the completely explicit numbers
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 U_1&=\min\{4S_1^2,B_{L,g}/(6s^2)\},\\
 U_3&=\min\{4S_3^2,
 B_{L,g}[1/(\sqrt6s)+4\sqrt{\mathcal H/3}]^2\},\\
 U_{\mathrm{new}}&=\min\{16S_1^2S_3^2,\ 4S_3^2U_1,\ 4S_1^2U_3\}.
 \end{aligned}                                                 \tag{CW34}
\]
Equations CW27 and CW32 imply
\[
 \boxed{\mathcal Q_4:=\mu(\mathcal S^2)\le U_{\mathrm{new}}
 \le\min\left\{16S_1^2S_3^2,
                \frac{2S_3^2}{3s^2}B_{L,g}\right\}.}     \tag{CW35}
\]
For example \(\mathcal S^2\le4S_3^2|u_1|^2\); integrating
and using CW27 proves the second branch. This replaces the
earlier use of \(\mu(W^2)\le4M\mu(W)\) by a direct linear
bound while also improving its coefficient by signed summation.
CW18, CW21 and the facewise form after CW27 may be inserted into
CW34 to give still more precise bounds when actual individual
face expectations are available. No such uniformity is assumed.

Reflection preserves \(\mu\). Expanding the two squares in CW6
therefore yields the exact simultaneous identity and bounds
\[
 \boxed{\mu(D^2)+\beta_0^{-2}\mu(F^2)=4\mathcal Q_4
 \le4U_{\mathrm{new}},\qquad
 G:=\mu(F^2)\le4\beta_0^2U_{\mathrm{new}}.}                \tag{CW36}
\]
There is no substitution \(\mathcal S_{\mathcal R}=-\mathcal S\)
in this actual-vacuum statement.

For a concrete accompanying energy bound, retain the full word
occurrence counts \(n_{ce}\) (both orientations counted) and set
\(D_{\nu e}=\sum_c|\mathsf M_{\nu c}|n_{ce}\). A unit generator
acting on one occurrence changes \(q\) by a vector of norm at
most one: the change of the unit-quaternion coordinates has
speed \(1/2\), and \(q\) is twice the vector coordinates.
The product rule, unitary multiplication and summing all
occurrences give \(|X_{e,\alpha}u_\nu|\le D_{\nu e}\).
Consequently
\[
 \mu(|\nabla\mathcal S|^2)
 \le3\left[\sqrt{U_3\sum_eD_{1e}^2}
           +\sqrt{U_1\sum_eD_{3e}^2}\right]^2=:\mathcal K_*.
                                                               \tag{CW37}
\]
To prove it, first bound each colour derivative of the dot
product by \(|u_3|D_{1e}+|u_1|D_{3e}\), then use the triangle
inequality in the combined \(L^2(\mu)\) and finite-link space.
The factor three retains all colours. This is a finite exact
sum on the original words; it requires no independence.

The ground-state form identity follows by expanding
\(H(f\psi)-fH\psi\) and integration by parts:
\(\langle f\psi,(H-E_0)f\psi\rangle=\kappa\mu(|\nabla f|^2)\).
The full potential and scalar cancel only after using the full
vacuum equation. Reflection is an isometry of that form, hence
\(C_F:=\kappa\mu(|\nabla F|^2)\le4\kappa\beta_0^2\mathcal K_*\).
The unchanged spectral endpoint therefore has the concrete bound
\[
 -\frac{4\kappa\beta_0^2}{s_{\rm spec}}\mathcal K_*
 \le\mathscr E(s_{\rm spec}):=G-C_F/s_{\rm spec}
 \le4\beta_0^2U_{\mathrm{new}}.                            \tag{CW38}
\]
Its exact expectation identity remains
\[
 \mathscr E(s_{\rm spec})
 =2\beta_0^2[\mathcal Q_4-\mu(\mathcal S\mathcal S_{\mathcal R})]
 -\frac{2\kappa\beta_0^2}{s_{\rm spec}}
 [\mu(|\nabla\mathcal S|^2)
      -\mu(\nabla\mathcal S\cdot\nabla\mathcal S_{\mathcal R})].
                                                               \tag{CW39}
\]
In particular, a positive upper bound for the first square cannot
give the sign of this difference. CW29, CW36 and CW39 retain
the precise nonlinear and reflected quantities involved.

\subsection*{CW9. Evaluation on the unchanged simultaneous path}

Keep, for every integer \(j\ge2\),
\[
 L_j=j^4,\quad a_j=1/(100j),\quad
 g_j^2=\kappa_*/(200j),\quad s_{{\rm spec},j}=\kappa_*/j,
 \quad\kappa_j=\kappa_*>0,\quad n_j=2j^4+1.               \tag{CW40}
\]
All finite formulas CW1–CW39 retain the corresponding
\(m_j=2j^4\), \(h_j=\pi/(2n_j)\), and
\(\beta_{0,j}=100j\sqrt8\sin h_j/\kappa_*\).
Write \(U_{\mathrm{point}}=16S_1^2S_3^2\) and
\(U_{\mathrm{linear}}=2S_3^2B_{L,g}/(3s^2)\), the new branch
in CW35. The exact limits are
\[
 \frac{U_{\mathrm{point},j}}{j^{24}}\longrightarrow\frac{2^{22}}{\pi^8},
 \qquad
 \boxed{\frac{U_{\mathrm{linear},j}}
 {j^7U_{\mathrm{point},j}}\longrightarrow
       \frac{3\pi^2\kappa_*}{1600}.}                     \tag{CW41}
\]
Indeed \(S_1/n^{3/2}\to4\sqrt2/\pi^2\),
\(S_3/n^{3/2}\to8\sqrt2/\pi^2\),
\(n\sin h\to\pi/2\), and
\(B_{L,g}/(g^2n^3)\to18\). The latter uses the smaller
variational branch, since \(3g_j^2\sqrt{n_j/m_j}\to0\).
Thus
\(U_{\mathrm{linear}}/U_{\mathrm{point}}
=B_{L,g}/(24s^2S_1^2)\sim(3\pi^2/32)g^2n^2\),
which gives CW41 after substituting CW40. This improves the
earlier PK177f comparison, whose excess factor was \(j^{19}\).
Both branches in CW34 eventually take their pointwise caps
on CW40: \(B/(24s^2S_1^2)\to\infty\), and the ratio of the
uncapped \(U_3\) branch to \(4S_3^2\) also tends to infinity,
already from its positive \(B/(6s^2)\) summand. Thus this
stronger mean-defect estimate still gives
\(U_{\mathrm{new}}=U_{\mathrm{point}}\) eventually.
This is a proved property of these upper-bound functions; it
does not state growth of the actual vacuum moment.

\subsection*{CW10. Exact small-curvature configurations with a large original odd phase}

The next calculation tests what information CW14 can imply by
itself. It is a deterministic configuration family, not a
replacement measure for the actual vacuum. Fix one of the
original \(T_\alpha\), write \(T=T_1\), and put
\[
 \theta_r=\frac{\pi r}{2m},\qquad
 U_1(i,k,l)=I,\quad
 U_2(i,k,l)=e^{2\theta_iT},\quad
 U_3(i,k,l)=e^{2\theta_kT}.                               \tag{CW42}
\]
The tree links in CW3 are exactly \(I\), including
\(U_2(0,k,l)\) and \(U_3(0,0,l)\). Thus CW42 gives the original
\(Z_2=e^{2\theta_iT}\), \(Z_3=e^{2\theta_kT}\), not an
altered chart. All factors happen to commute in this exhibited
configuration; none of the general identities assumes that.

With \(\alpha=\pi/(2m)\), direct multiplication gives every
face, including its orientation and boundary values:
\[
 P_{12}=e^{2\alpha T},\qquad P_{13}=I,\qquad
 P_{23}=e^{2\alpha T},\qquad
 W=4m^2n(1-\cos\alpha),\quad
 \frac WM=\frac43(1-\cos\alpha)\le\frac{\pi^2}{6m^2}.
                                                               \tag{CW43}
\]
There are \(m^2n\) faces of each orientation. The inequality
uses \(1-\cos\alpha\le\alpha^2/2\), which follows by
integrating \(\sin t\le t\).

Let
\[
 A_m=2\sqrt{2/n}\sum_{r=0}^m
 \cos\frac{\pi(r+1/2)}n\sin\frac{\pi r}{2m}.
                                                               \tag{CW44}
\]
This is an exact finite sum with its original central zero and
both endpoints. Writing \(b_0=\pi/n\), the sine-product sum gives
the equivalent full expression
\[
 A_m=\frac{\cos(n\alpha/2)}{\sqrt n}
 \left[\csc\frac{\alpha+b_0}{2}
       +\csc\frac{\alpha-b_0}{2}\right].                 \tag{CW45}
\]
To verify CW45, use
\(2\sin(\alpha r)\cos(b_0r+b_0/2)
=\sin((\alpha+b_0)r+b_0/2)
+\sin((\alpha-b_0)r-b_0/2)\), and then the finite formula
\(\sum_{r=0}^m\sin(tr+\phi)
=\sin(nt/2)\sin(\phi+mt/2)/\sin(t/2)\).
Its two second sine factors are \(\sin(3\pi/4)\) and
\(\sin(-\pi/4)\); the two first sine factors are opposite.
This proves the stated sum, with the negative denominator in
its second summand retained.

Summing all coefficients in CW5 gives exactly
\[
 u_1=A_mC\,e_1,\qquad u_3=A_mC\,e_1,\qquad
 \mathcal S=A_m^2C^2.                                     \tag{CW46}
\]
For the first equality use \(\sum w=-\sqrt{2/n}C\) and
\(\sum v_0=\sqrt n\). The direction-two contribution to
\(u_3\) vanishes because \(\sum_l v(l)=0\); its direction-three
contribution is the same complete product of sums as for \(u_1\).
Its excluded corner already has \(q(Z_3)=0\).

CW12 gives the reflected root factors
\(t^{\mathcal R}_{i,k,l}=e^{\pi kT}\); the reflected 3-axis
root factors are \(I\). Therefore the full reflected words are
\[
 Z_2^{\mathcal R}(i,k,l)=e^{-2\theta_iT},\qquad
 Z_3^{\mathcal R}(i,k,l)=e^{2\theta_kT}.
\]
The direction-two contribution to \(u_3^{\mathcal R}\) still
vanishes after summing \(v(l)\). It follows exactly that
\[
 \mathcal S_{\mathcal R}=-\mathcal S,\qquad
 D=0,\qquad F=2\beta_0A_m^2C^2.                           \tag{CW47}
\]
These signs are proved only for CW42; they are not installed
as a global reflection rule.

The full sums in CW45 give
\[
 \frac{A_m}{\sqrt n}\longrightarrow-\frac{4\sqrt2}{3\pi},
 \quad \frac{\mathcal S}{n^3}\longrightarrow\frac{128}{9\pi^4},
 \quad \frac{\mathcal S^2}{U_{\mathrm{point}}}\longrightarrow\frac1{324},
 \quad \frac{F^2}{\beta_0^2U_{\mathrm{point}}}\longrightarrow\frac1{81}.
                                                               \tag{CW48}
\]
For the first limit, \(n\alpha\to\pi/2\), and the two cosecants
divided by \(n\) tend to \(4/(3\pi)\) and \(-4/\pi\).
The remaining limits follow from \(C/n\to2/\pi\) and the
full constants preceding CW41. In particular the power \(n^5\)
in an inequality \(\mathcal S^2\le\mathrm{constant}\,n^5W\)
cannot be reduced to a smaller power uniformly in configurations:
CW43 gives \(W/n\to\pi^2/2\), whereas CW48 gives a positive
limit for \(\mathcal S^2/n^6\). More precisely,
\(\mathcal S^2/(n^5W)\to32768/(81\pi^{10})>0\).

On the exact path CW40, CW43 gives
\(W/M\le\pi^2/(24j^8)\). For every fixed \(\kappa_*>0\),
this is strictly below the source bound in CW14 for all
sufficiently large \(j\). Meanwhile CW47–CW48 retain an odd
phase of the same power as its global pointwise bound.
This makes the insufficiency of the mean-defect information
quantitative even when the original phase and reflection are kept.
The exact path constants for this exhibited family are
\[
 j^8\frac{W_j}{M_j}\longrightarrow\frac{\pi^2}{24},\qquad
 \frac{F_j^2}{j^{18}}\longrightarrow
       \frac{20000\,2^{20}}{81\kappa_*^2\pi^6}>0.          \tag{CW49}
\]
They follow from CW43, CW48, \(n_j/j^4\to2\), and
\(j^6\beta_{0,j}^2\to5000\pi^2/\kappa_*^2\).

One may also impose the symmetries used in CW15 without changing
that conclusion. Average the point mass of CW42 over its gauge
orbit and the finite cube symmetry group. There are at most
48 geometric images; all have the same \(W\). The gauge-invariant
nonnegative observable \(F^2\) has average at least its value
on CW42 divided by 48, since the identity symmetry occurs.
Reflection ensures \(F\) has mean zero. This comparison measure
is not the vacuum and is used solely to disprove an inference
from mean defect and symmetries alone. If a positive smooth
density is desired for that logical test, convolve the finite
symmetry average with a small positive group heat kernel and
average over the same symmetries. Compactness and continuity
make its \(W\) and \(F^2\) expectations converge to the values
just proved; the strict margin below CW14 survives at sufficiently
small smoothing time. No ground-state equation is asserted for
these comparison measures.

\subsection*{CW11. Source use, checks and the precise endpoint consequence}

The original programme sources read for this calculation are:

- \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/PERIOD_COUPLING_AND_PHYSICAL_KERNELS.md#L3500}{PK158–PK177f, original rows and existing face bounds}.
- \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/sources/spatial/spatial_continuum.tex#L1752}{Original spatial TeX, Section 12, equations (93)–(100)}.
- The local original-word occurrence calculation IP2–IP9 and
  the full reflection derivation PS36–PS39 were read for comparison;
  the identities used here are proved explicitly in CW3–CW13
  and CW22–CW39.

The existing machine-readable research and local-literature indexes
were queried before importing a new external theorem; no external
theorem beyond the retained programme source is required for the
new matrix and finite-sum calculations. `CURVATURE\_SOURCE\_USE\_LEDGER.json`
records exact local files, hashes, reading coverage and result maps.
`verify\_curvature\_words.py` freely reduces every original and
factored noncommutative word on small complete boxes, checks the
reflected transports, tests the quadratic quaternion identity over
exact rational arithmetic, and checks the finite coefficients and
exhibited configurations numerically at high precision. These
checks supplement the proof and do not replace it.

`CURVATURE\_ROOTED\_WORDS\_FIGURE.png`, reproduced by
`draw\_curvature\_words.py`, shows the exact five-face bent strip
for \(Z_3(3,2,1)\), with the shared rooted stem retained, and
the two quantitative properties of the explicit family CW42.
Its geometry axes are integer lattice indices; the physical
position is the full map \(o+a(i-L,k-L,l-L)\) in CW1.
The plotted phase values come from that deterministic family,
not from sampling the vacuum. CW8–CW13 and CW42–CW48 are the
complete proof locators for the geometry and plotted quantities.

The actual-vacuum conclusion is CW18–CW21, CW27, CW32, and
CW34–CW39, with the improved exact path comparison CW41.
The missing information in the spectral endpoint is now visible
as the actual joint rooted/reflected moments and their energy
difference in CW39, with the exact nonabelian remainder CW29
retained. CW42–CW48 prove that adding only the known mean defect
and the proved cube symmetries cannot control those quantities
well enough to make the large rooted phase small. They do not
assert any asymptotic behaviour of the actual interacting vacuum,
nor any sign for its full spectral endpoint.


\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering\includegraphics[width=\linewidth,height=.72\textheight,keepaspectratio]{CURVATURE_ROOTED_WORDS_FIGURE.png}\caption{Exact original rooted transport and factor order; deterministic family CW42 has small mean curvature and a large original odd phase. This figure does not plot vacuum samples.}\end{figure}

In the factor-order label, Q13,a denotes the transported face in CW9 at (a,2,1), and Q23,b denotes the transported face at (0,b,1). The complete physical coordinate map is CW1.
