\section*{The ordered Wilson action through its cubic term}

This note reads and repairs three local proof defects in Sourav Chatterjee's original author source, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/1602.01222v6}{*The leading term of the Yang–Mills free energy*, arXiv:1602.01222v6}, Section 16, Lemmas 16.1–16.2 and Theorem 16.3 (source labels `huerr`, `utohlmm`, `utohthm`). The complete comparison below is proved directly. No free-energy theorem, Gaussian approximation of the actual vacuum, or change of the original Hamiltonian is inferred from it.

\subsection*{OW1. Original ordered matrices and the source step}

For Hermitian matrices \(H_1,H_2,H_3,H_4\) of order \(r\), keep the ordered product
\[
 P(t)=e^{itH_1}e^{itH_2}e^{itH_3}e^{itH_4},\qquad
 \Phi(t)=r-\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{Tr}P(t),\qquad
 A=H_1+H_2+H_3+H_4.                                      \tag{OW1}
\]
For a face the four matrices are respectively the positive first edge, positive second edge, negative opposite first edge, and negative opposite second edge. These signs are part of their definitions. Products in OW1 are not commuted. The source's norm is the Hilbert–Schmidt norm; write \(\|\cdot\|_{\rm HS}\) and \(\|\cdot\|_{\rm op}\) separately here.

Two printed source definitions require coordinate corrections before making this comparison. At author-source line 1762, the last two edges in the definition of \(H(x,j,k)\) run from \(x+e_j+e_k\) through \(x+e_j\) to \(x\). They backtrack along the first two edges. Their displayed sum is therefore \(H_1+H_2-H_2-H_1=0\) for every assignment. The actual face returns through \(x+e_k\). Line 1832 gives the same backtracking coordinates in the definition of \(R(x,j,k)\). In contrast, the four matrices listed at lines 1848–1851 use the intended face coordinates. The exact repairs at lines 1762 and 1832 replace both occurrences of the intermediate return vertex \(x+e_j\) by \(x+e_k\); OW1 uses that complete oriented face. The earlier Wilson face definition at lines 235–241 also uses this intended face.

This is a nonzero mathematical defect, not an innocuous alternate path: assign a nonzero Hermitian matrix to the first positive face edge and zero to the other three. The intended circulation equals that matrix, whereas the printed backtracking sum is zero. The two paths and their quadratic forms are thus explicitly compared. This note does not silently attribute the repaired path to the printed definition.

Independently of those coordinate errors, the source defines \(\phi(U)=\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{Tr}(I-U)\). For a unitary matrix \(U\),
\[
 \phi(U)=\tfrac12\|I-U\|_{\rm HS}^{2}.
\]
For an arbitrary square matrix \(R\), the exact identity instead is
\[
 \tfrac12\|I-R\|_{\rm HS}^{2}
 =\phi(R)+\tfrac12\operatorname{Tr}(R^*R-I).              \tag{OW2}
\]
Expansion of \((I-R)^*(I-R)\) proves OW2 term by term. At author-source lines 1843–1844, equation `phiur` identifies \(\phi(P)-\phi(R)\) with half the difference of these squared norms, where \(R\) is a product of the quadratic Taylor polynomials. Such \(R\) need not be unitary. For example, set \(H_1=\operatorname{diag}(x,-x)\) and \(H_2=H_3=H_4=0\), for real \(x\ne0\). Then
\[
 R=I+iH_1-H_1^2/2,\quad
 R^*R=(1+x^4/4)I,\quad
 \phi(R)=x^2,\quad
 \tfrac12\|I-R\|_{\rm HS}^{2}=x^2+x^4/4.                 \tag{OW3}
\]
Thus that equality has a nonzero defect even with traceless Hermitian matrices and the intended face coordinates. It also fails if the backtracking definition is retained: in the same assignment that definition gives \(R_{\rm back}=(1+x^4/4)I\), so \(\phi(R_{\rm back})=-x^4/2\), while \(\|I-R_{\rm back}\|_{\rm HS}^2/2=x^8/16\). The cubic-order conclusion can be retained after the coordinate repair: directly,
\[
 |\phi(P)-\phi(R)|
 =|\operatorname{Re}\operatorname{Tr}(R-P)|
 \le\sqrt r\,\|R-P\|_{\rm HS}.                          \tag{OW4}
\]
Cauchy–Schwarz against the identity matrix proves the last inequality. The following calculation supplies a complete alternative proof, explicit constants and the ordered cubic contribution, so it does not depend on the invalid intermediate equality. The correction is to this v6 proof step, not a claim that the paper's free-energy theorem is false.

\subsection*{OW2. Exact Taylor coefficients and integral remainders}

Put \(q=\sum_{a=1}^4\|H_a\|_{\rm op}\). For every integer \(k\ge0\), the product rule gives
\[
 P^{(k)}(t)=\sum_{k_1+\cdots+k_4=k}
 \frac{k!}{k_1!k_2!k_3!k_4!}
 \prod_{a=1}^4\big[(iH_a)^{k_a}e^{itH_a}\big].          \tag{OW5}
\]
Every product is in the original order. Since each exponential is unitary for real \(t\), submultiplicativity and the scalar multinomial formula prove
\[
 \|P^{(k)}(t)\|_{\rm op}\le q^k,\qquad
 |\Phi^{(k)}(t)|\le r q^k\quad(k\ge1).                 \tag{OW6}
\]
The trace bound follows by summing the \(r\) diagonal matrix elements in an orthonormal basis. No smallness assumption on the \(H_a\) is used.

At zero, \(\Phi(0)=\Phi'(0)=0\). The full quadratic and cubic coefficients before taking the real trace are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 P(t)={}&I+itA-t^2\left[\tfrac12\sum_aH_a^2+
                  \sum_{a<b}H_aH_b\right]-it^3 B_3
                  +\text{the exact fourth-order integral remainder},\\
 B_3={}&\tfrac16\sum_aH_a^3+
 \tfrac12\sum_{a<b}(H_a^2H_b+H_aH_b^2)
                  +\sum_{a<b<c}H_aH_bH_c .
 \end{aligned}                                               \tag{OW7}
\]
This follows by selecting all ordered degree partitions in OW5 for \(k=1,2,3\). The traces of \(H_a^3\), \(H_a^2H_b\), and \(H_aH_b^2\) are real: take the adjoint and use cyclicity of the trace. Similarly \(\operatorname{Tr}(H_aH_b)=\operatorname{Tr}(H_bH_a)\), so the quadratic trace is \(\operatorname{Tr}(A^2)/2\). Consequently the scalar identity with its entire remainder is
\[
 \boxed{\Phi(1)=\tfrac12\operatorname{Tr}(A^2)
 -\sum_{a<b<c}\operatorname{Im}\operatorname{Tr}(H_aH_bH_c)
 +\mathcal R_4,\quad
 \mathcal R_4=\frac16\int_0^1(1-t)^3\Phi^{(4)}(t)\,dt,\quad
 |\mathcal R_4|\le\frac{r q^4}{24}.}                     \tag{OW8}
\]
Taylor's integral formula follows by integrating the fourth derivative four times; \(\int_0^1(1-t)^3dt=1/4\) gives the stated constant. Keeping instead the third derivative gives the complete quadratic comparison
\[
 \boxed{\Phi(1)=\tfrac12\|A\|_{\rm HS}^{2}+\mathcal R_3,
 \quad \mathcal R_3=\tfrac12\int_0^1(1-t)^2\Phi'''(t)\,dt,
 \quad |\mathcal R_3|\le\frac{r q^3}{6}.}                \tag{OW9}
\]
If all four original Hilbert–Schmidt norms are at most \(\eta\), then \(q\le4\eta\), and OW9 gives the explicit constant \(32r\eta^3/3\). This holds for every \(\eta\ge0\), strengthening the small-radius scope of the source lemma. The integral remainders remain the original remainders; their bounds do not replace them in identities.

For completeness, the ordered imaginary trace is the exact commutator term
\[
 \operatorname{Im}\operatorname{Tr}(H_aH_bH_c)
 =\frac{1}{2i}\operatorname{Tr}(H_a[H_b,H_c]).            \tag{OW10}
\]
Indeed the complex conjugate of the first trace is \(\operatorname{Tr}(H_cH_bH_a)=\operatorname{Tr}(H_aH_cH_b)\). Subtraction proves OW10 and retains its order and sign.

\subsection*{OW3. Exact map to the original SU(2) link coordinates}

Retain the programme generators \(T_\alpha=-i\sigma_\alpha/2\), with their radius-two metric. For every original positive link choose a vector \(x_e\in\mathbb R^3\) and put
\[
 X_e=\sum_{\alpha=1}^3x_e^\alpha T_\alpha,
 \qquad U_e=e^{X_e},\qquad H_e=-\tfrac12\sum_\alpha x_e^\alpha\sigma_\alpha,
 \qquad iH_e=X_e.                                        \tag{OW11}
\]
This is the original exponential map on all of \(\mathbb R^3\), without an injectivity assertion. It is a one-to-one coordinate chart on \(|x_e|<2\pi\) with image \(SU(2)\setminus\{-I\}\): the formula \(e^{X_e}=\cos(|x_e|/2)I+2\sin(|x_e|/2)X_e/|x_e|\), with its continuous value at zero, identifies the angle in \([0,\pi)\) and its direction uniquely. On a reverse edge use \(-x_e\) and \(U_e^{-1}\).

On the positively oriented original face \((z;d,e)\), \(d<e\), let
\[
 y_1=x_{(z;d)},\quad y_2=x_{(z+e_d;e)},\quad
 y_3=-x_{(z+e_e;d)},\quad y_4=-x_{(z;e)},\quad
 C_p=y_1+y_2+y_3+y_4,\quad q_p=\tfrac12\sum_{a=1}^4|y_a|.
                                                               \tag{OW12}
\]
All positive, negative and boundary edges are retained in this definition. The Pauli multiplication rule \((\sigma\cdot u)(\sigma\cdot v)=(u\cdot v)I+i\sigma\cdot(u\times v)\) gives
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}(H_aH_b)=\tfrac12y_a\cdot y_b,\quad
 \operatorname{Tr}(H_aH_bH_c)=-\tfrac{i}{4}y_a\cdot(y_b\times y_c),
 \quad \|H_a\|_{\rm op}=|y_a|/2.                         \tag{OW13}
\]
Insert every factor in OW8–OW9 to obtain, for the actual face defect \(D_p=2-\operatorname{Tr}P_p\),
\[
 \boxed{D_p=\tfrac14|C_p|^2+
 \tfrac14\sum_{a<b<c}y_a\cdot(y_b\times y_c)+R_{4,p},
 \qquad |R_{4,p}|\le\frac{(\sum_a|y_a|)^4}{192}.}        \tag{OW14}
\]
Here \(R_{4,p}\) is exactly the integral in OW8 for the four matrices in OW11–OW12. The comparison with only the quadratic term is
\[
 D_p=\tfrac14|C_p|^2+R_{3,p},\qquad
 |R_{3,p}|\le\frac{(\sum_a|y_a|)^3}{24}.                 \tag{OW15}
\]
Both statements hold for all selected exponential representatives, not only in a small-field event. The cubic term includes all four triples \(123,124,134,234\). It is not zero in general. Reversing the face replaces the list by \((-y_4,-y_3,-y_2,-y_1)\); the change of sign from each triple and the reversal of its order cancel. Thus OW14 has the same cubic scalar on the reversed face, as required by \(D(P^{-1})=D(P)\).

The full original Hamiltonian remains
\[
 H=\kappa K+b\sum_pD_p,\qquad
 \kappa=2g^2/a,\quad b=1/(2g^2a),\quad
 K=-\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}^2.
\]
Consequently the exact potential, including its original scalar contribution, is
\[
 \boxed{b\sum_pD_p=\frac b4\sum_p|C_p|^2+
 \frac b4\sum_p\sum_{a<b<c}y_a\cdot(y_b\times y_c)+b\sum_pR_{4,p}.}
                                                               \tag{OW16}
\]
This is equality to the original \(b\sum_p(2-\operatorname{Tr}P_p)\); it has not discarded \(2bM\) from the operator or its vacuum equation. The electric operator is untouched. In particular, replacing it by a constant-coordinate Laplacian is not part of OW16.

For any selected representatives, OW16 and the triangle inequality prove the actual-vacuum comparison
\[
 \left|b\mu\sum_pD_p-
 \frac b4\mu\sum_p|C_p|^2-
 \frac b4\mu\sum_p\sum_{a<b<c}y_a\cdot(y_b\times y_c)\right|
 \le\frac b{192}\mu\sum_p\left(\sum_a|y_a|\right)^4.    \tag{OW17}
\]
One may use the principal chart outside the measure-zero link set \(U_e=-I\), assigning any representative on that set. The smooth positive vacuum is absolutely continuous with respect to product Haar, so these assignments do not change any expectation. The chart gives \(|x_e|<2\pi\) almost everywhere and makes all terms integrable. This supplies a finite exact bound, not an assertion that its right side is small. No independence or Gaussian law is used.

\subsection*{OW4. Two fully evaluated original-coordinate tests}

First set the ordered face list to \((t e_1,t e_2,t e_3,0)\). Direct quaternion multiplication gives
\[
 D_+(t)=2-2\cos^3(t/2)+2\sin^3(t/2),\quad
 D_-(t)=2-2\cos^3(t/2)-2\sin^3(t/2),                    \tag{OW18}
\]
where \(D_-\) uses the list \((t e_1,t e_3,t e_2,0)\). Both have quadratic term \(3t^2/4\), whereas their cubic terms are \(+t^3/4\) and \(-t^3/4\). Their exact difference is \(4\sin^3(t/2)\). This is an ordered nonabelian test, not a gauge-field probability measure. The remainder bound in each case is \(81t^4/192\), by OW14. Reversing an entire face preserves its defect; exchanging two entries is a different operation, and OW18 computes its effect.

Second retain the complete configuration CW42, with \(m=2L\), \(n=m+1\), \(\theta_i=\pi i/(2m)\), \(T=T_1\),
\[
 U_1=I,\qquad U_2(i,k,l)=e^{2\theta_iT},\qquad
 U_3(i,k,l)=e^{2\theta_kT},\qquad \alpha=\pi/(2m).
\]
All logarithm vectors are parallel. Each of the \(m^2n\) original 12 faces and \(m^2n\) original 23 faces has \(C_p=2\alpha e_1\); every 13 face has \(C_p=0\). Therefore every cubic term is zero, and direct multiplication, retaining all faces, gives
\[
 W=4m^2n(1-\cos\alpha),\quad
 W_{\rm quad}=2m^2n\alpha^2,\quad
 -\frac{m^2n\alpha^4}{6}\le W-W_{\rm quad}\le0.          \tag{OW19}
\]
For a proof of the bound, \(1-\cos\alpha\le\alpha^2/2\) follows by integrating \(\sin t\le t\); Taylor's fourth-order integral bound gives \(1-\cos\alpha\ge\alpha^2/2-\alpha^4/24\). Multiply both by \(4m^2n\). This uses the full commuting face sum, so its remainder is smaller than a bound based on four separate large link coordinates.

On the unchanged path \(m_j=2j^4\), \(a_j=1/(100j)\), \(g_j^2=\kappa_*/(200j)\), \(b_j=10000j^2/\kappa_*\), the finite potential comparison and its limits are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 b_jW_{{\rm quad},j}=\frac{5000\pi^2}{\kappa_*}j^2(2j^4+1),\\
 -\frac{10000j^2}{\kappa_*}\frac{\pi^4(2j^4+1)}{384j^8}
 \le b_j(W_j-W_{{\rm quad},j})\le0,\\
 \frac{b_jW_j}{j^6}\longrightarrow\frac{10000\pi^2}{\kappa_*},\qquad
 j^2b_j(W_j-W_{{\rm quad},j})\longrightarrow-\frac{625\pi^4}{12\kappa_*}.
 \end{gathered}                                             \tag{OW20}
\]
Indeed \((1-\cos\alpha)-\alpha^2/2=-\alpha^4/24+O(\alpha^6)\), with the sixth derivative uniformly bounded by one; thus the omitted sixth-order contribution after multiplication by \(j^2b_jm_j^2n_j\) tends to zero. Substituting the displayed \(m_j,n_j,b_j,\alpha_j\) yields every constant in OW20. These are values on the explicit CW configuration, not expectations in the vacuum.

\subsection*{OW5. The exact quartic term when the linear face circulation vanishes}

Retain all four signed vectors and define \(B_p=\sum_{a<b}y_a\times y_b\). Direct expansion gives
\[
 \sum_{a<b<c}y_a\cdot(y_b\times y_c)=C_p\cdot B_p.       \tag{OW21}
\]
Terms with a repeated index vanish. For any distinct ordered triple \(a<b<c\), the remaining terms have coefficients \(+1,-1,+1\), so their sum is its one displayed triple product. This proves OW21 without a reordering of the matrix product. In particular the complete cubic term vanishes on the explicitly defined vector space \(C_p=0\).

On this vector space the leading fourth-order coefficient can also be calculated exactly. Let \(P_p(t)=c(t)I+2\sum_\alpha v^\alpha(t)T_\alpha\), with \(c(t)^2+|v(t)|^2=1\). Ordered quaternion multiplication of the four exponentials gives \(v(t)=tC_p/2+t^2B_p/4+O(t^3)\). This follows by choosing either one linear vector term or two linear vector terms; the vector part of the latter is their cross product in the original order. For \(C_p=0\), \(c(t)\to1\), and hence
\[
 2-2c(t)=\frac{2|v(t)|^2}{1+c(t)}
       =\frac{t^4}{16}|B_p|^2+O(t^5).
\]
This identifies the fourth derivative at zero; it does not replace a finite value by a truncated series. Taylor's exact fifth-order integral formula and OW6 give the full equality
\[
 \boxed{C_p=0:\quad D_p=\frac{|B_p|^2}{16}+R_{5,p},\qquad
 R_{5,p}=\frac1{24}\int_0^1(1-t)^4\Phi^{(5)}(t)\,dt,
 \quad |R_{5,p}|\le\frac{(\sum_a|y_a|)^5}{1920}.}        \tag{OW22}
\]
The denominator is \(120\cdot2^5/2=1920\), retaining the matrix dimension two and each original half-angle. For example the four-edge list \((t e_1,t e_2,-t e_1,-t e_2)\) has \(C_p=0\), \(B_p=2t^2e_3\). Direct quaternion multiplication gives its exact defect \(4\sin^4(t/2)\), whose quartic term is \(t^4/4\). Thus vanishing linear circulation does not remove the original magnetic defect. OW22 describes this exact space of configurations; it does not assume the actual vacuum is supported there.

\subsection*{OW6. The exact positive defect of the ordered Taylor product}

The omitted term in OW2 can itself be calculated without assuming unitarity. For the four original Hermitian matrices define \(Q_a=I+iH_a-H_a^2/2\), \(R=Q_1Q_2Q_3Q_4\), and \(Q_{>a}=Q_{a+1}\cdots Q_4\), with \(Q_{>4}=I\). Multiplication gives \(Q_a^*Q_a=I+H_a^4/4\). Telescoping in the original order therefore proves
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 R^*R-I&=\tfrac14\sum_{a=1}^4Q_{>a}^*H_a^4Q_{>a},\\
 \Delta(R):=\tfrac12\operatorname{Tr}(R^*R-I)
 &=\tfrac18\sum_{a=1}^4\|H_a^2Q_{>a}\|_{\rm HS}^2\ge0,\\
 \phi(P)-\phi(R)
 &=\tfrac12\big(\|I-P\|_{\rm HS}^2-\|I-R\|_{\rm HS}^2\big)
       +\Delta(R),\qquad P=e^{iH_1}e^{iH_2}e^{iH_3}e^{iH_4}.
 \end{aligned}                                               \tag{OW23}
\]
To see the telescoping identity, first insert \(Q_1^*Q_1-I\) inside the remaining three factors, then do the same with \(Q_2,Q_3,Q_4\); no two factors are interchanged. Taking traces identifies each summand as the displayed squared norm. If \(\|H_a\|_{\rm HS}\le\eta\) for every \(a\), then \(\|H_a^2\|_{\rm HS}^2\le\eta^4\) and \(\|Q_a\|_{\rm op}^2\le1+\eta^4/4\). Summing the resulting geometric series gives
\[
 0\le\Delta(R)\le\tfrac12\big[(1+\eta^4/4)^4-1\big].
\]
For \(\eta=0\) the same conclusion follows directly. Each \(Q_a\) is invertible because \(Q_a^*Q_a\ge I\). Hence every tail \(Q_{>a}\) is invertible, and the sum of squares in OW23 vanishes exactly when every \(H_a=0\). Thus the Taylor product used in the intended face is unitary only in that zero case. OW23 both quantifies and characterizes the defect in the source equality, while OW4 and OW8–OW9 repair the action comparison independently of it.

\subsection*{OW7. Receiving calculation and source scope}

OW14–OW17 supply the exact cubic and fourth-order quantities required for a weak-field comparison on the original programme objects. Their values under the actual vacuum remain part of the research calculation. The field words in \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/dbbcbaf7e8d50e41f7491dc9bbed1c0c6ed54b0c/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/CURVATURE_ROOTED_WORDS.md}{CW42–CW49} are an explicit test of the comparison and preserve the large original odd phase proved in CW47–CW49. They do not establish that the actual vacuum concentrates near those configurations or follows a Gaussian law.

OW21–OW22 go one step further: on the exact zero-linear-circulation space the ordered commutator sum supplies the quartic magnetic energy. Its next receiving calculation is the actual-vacuum distribution of these original full terms, not an assumption that the cubic or quartic contribution can be omitted.

The source's lattice model is a Euclidean \(U(r)\) Wilson Gibbs measure. Its free-energy theorem is not an identification of the programme's continuous-time \(SU(2)\) Hamiltonian vacuum. The exact common map used here is the restriction of its intended ordered matrix expression to the traceless Hermitian matrices in OW11, with the printed coordinate repairs recorded above, followed by the unchanged original face map OW12 and coefficient \(b\) in OW16. Every comparison actually used is proved above, rather than importing a change of state.

Reading coverage: original v6 author TeX lines 187–430 for identity, definitions and statements, and 1756–1888 for the complete ordered action comparison and its proofs. The free-energy proof as a whole has not been read or applied. The downloaded v3 archive was retained for provenance but was not used as the source of a mathematical claim. Both archives remain intact; source hashes, the three exact v6 proof defects and their propagation are recorded in the private reading and claim ledgers. No claim of novelty is made.

\subsection*{8. Propagated improvements from the complete independent calculation}

The complete derivation in \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/ORDERED_WILSON_INDEPENDENT_REVIEW.md}{UR1–UR15} proves two strengthenings while retaining every earlier valid bound. For the general Hermitian matrices of OW5, put \(q_{\rm op}=\sum_a\|H_a\|_{\rm op}\) and \(q_{\rm HS}=\sum_a\|H_a\|_{\rm HS}\). Then
\[
 |\Phi^{(k)}(t)|\le\min\{r q_{\rm op}^k,
                  q_{\rm HS}^2q_{\rm op}^{k-2}\},\quad k\ge2,
 \qquad
 |R_3|\le q_{\rm HS}^2q_{\rm op}/6,\quad
 |R_4|\le q_{\rm HS}^2q_{\rm op}^2/24.
 \tag{OW24}
\]
To prove it without reordering the product, index its k differentiations by ordered derivative labels. In each term choose the first two differentiation labels for Hilbert–Schmidt estimates; every other differentiated factor uses its operator norm. Move factors only cyclically inside the trace and apply the Hilbert–Schmidt inner-product inequality to those two factors, retaining all intervening unitary matrices. Summing over all derivative labels factors the bound into the exact displayed product of norm sums. The other member of the minimum is OW6. Integration of the full third and fourth derivatives proves both remainder bounds. In particular \(\|H_a\|_{\rm HS}\le\eta\) gives \(|R_3|\le32\eta^3/3\), independently of r. The original SU(2) specialization has \(q_{\rm HS}=\sqrt2q_{\rm op}\), so its constants in OW14–OW17 stay exactly as stated.

On the exact original-coordinate locus \(C=\sum_a y_a=0\), \(B=\sum_{a<b}y_a\times y_b=0\), there is the stronger sixth-order estimate
\[
 0\le 2-\operatorname{Tr}\prod_{a=1}^4e^{tX(y_a)}
 \le \frac{|t|^6(\sum_a|y_a|)^6}{2304},\qquad t\in\mathbb R.
 \tag{OW25}
\]
Indeed \(P'(0)=X(C)=0\) and, by expanding the entire ordered second derivative, \(P''(0)=\sum_{a<b}[X(y_a),X(y_b)]=X(B)=0\). The third derivative has operator norm at most \(q_{\rm op}^3\), with \(q_{\rm op}=\sum_a|y_a|/2\). Its exact integral remainder gives \(\|P(t)-I\|_{\rm op}\le|t|^3q_{\rm op}^3/6\). The original product is unitary, so \(2-\operatorname{Tr}P=\tfrac12\|I-P\|_{\rm HS}^2\le\|I-P\|_{\rm op}^2\) in dimension two. Squaring and keeping the factor \(2^6\) proves (OW25). This is a calculation on the displayed coordinate locus, with no claim that the actual vacuum concentrates on it.

In OW17 the selected representatives must be measurable with finite fourth moments for its stated expectations. The supplied principal representatives \(|x_e|<2\pi\), with any measurable choice on the Haar-null exceptional set, satisfy this requirement. An arbitrary unbounded or nonmeasurable selection is not covered. This qualification does not change the original potential, vacuum or principal-chart calculation.

The figure records OW24–OW25 and UR8/UR13. It is a schematic of exact bounds, with no numerical or vacuum-concentration data. The complete original human source comparison remains OW1–OW4 and UR1–UR6; draw\_ordered\_review.py reproduces the figure.

\section*{The signed phase density, its coupled covariance, and the actual endpoint}

11 October 2026. This calculation keeps the original phase, actual positive unit vacuum, original open-box Hamiltonian, all means, and the prescribed simultaneous path. Its new receiving result is a coupled enclosure for the actual pair \(Q_4-jK_1\) and \(\mathcal E_j\), using the covariance of their complete signed densities. The covariance retains the mixed mass–energy moments that a sum of separate mass and energy error bounds loses. It does not establish the sign of the actual shrinking-window endpoint.

The existing moment identities PK202, PRE23–PRE31, and SR32–SR40 are providers, not claimed advances. The finite-cutoff construction is the full unshifted construction PK207–PK227. The exact original words are retained from PK158–PK163, PS37–PS46, PG28–PG32, and PRE10–PRE16. All source versions and actual reading coverage are recorded separately. Existing disk indexes were queried before this derivation. No paper returned only as a routing record supplies a theorem here.

\subsection*{1. Original objects and the two signed quantities}

Retain \(m=2L\), \(n=m+1\), \(L\ge2\), the physical vertices \(o+a(i-L,k-L,l-L)\), all original positive edges and faces, and
\[
 N=3m(m+1)^2,\quad M=3m^2(m+1),\quad Q_L=SU(2)^{E_L},\quad
 T_\alpha=-i\sigma_\alpha/2,\quad
 X_{e,\alpha}f=\left.\frac d{dt}f(\ldots,e^{tT_\alpha}U_e,\ldots)\right|_{t=0}.
 \tag{AC1}
\]
Product Haar is probability measure. The fields are orthonormal for the radius-two metric. Put \(K=-\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}^2\), \(\Gamma(f,h)=\sum_{e,\alpha}(X_{e,\alpha}f)(X_{e,\alpha}h)\), and keep
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 W_{(z;i,j)}=\operatorname{tr}[U_i(z)U_j(z+e_i)U_i(z+e_j)^{-1}U_j(z)^{-1}],\\
 H=\kappa K+\kappa\xi\sum_p(2-W_p),\quad
 \kappa=2g^2/a,\quad \xi=1/(4g^4),\\
 H\psi=E_0\psi,\quad\psi>0,\quad\int\psi^2dU=1,\quad
 d\mu=\psi^2dU,\quad
 \mathcal L=K-2\Gamma(\log\psi,\cdot)
            =\kappa^{-1}\psi^{-1}(H-E_0)\psi .
 \end{gathered}\tag{AC2}
\]
The scalar \(2\kappa\xi M\), every boundary face, and the vacuum energy remain in these equations. The gauge action is imposed at every vertex, including the boundary.

The tree root is \((0,0,0)\), and its paths run in directions three, two, one. Its chords are
\[
 \mathcal C_2=\{(i,k,l;2):1\le i\le m,\ 0\le k<m,\ 0\le l\le m\},\qquad
 \mathcal C_3=\{(i,k,l;3):0\le i,k\le m,\ (i,k)\ne(0,0),\ 0\le l<m\}.
 \tag{AC3}
\]
Write \(Z_c=t_sU_ct_t^{-1}\), \(q^\alpha(U)=-2\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha U)\), and retain the complete rows
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 h=\pi/(2n),\quad v_0(l)=n^{-1/2},\quad
 v_1(l)=\sqrt{2/n}\cos[\pi(l+1/2)/n],\quad
 w_1(l)=-\sqrt{2/n}\sin[\pi(l+1)/n],\\
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}=-v_1(i)w_1(k)v_0(l)/\sqrt2,\quad a_{(i,k,l;3)}=0,\\
 b_{(i,k,l;2)}=v_0(i)w_1(k)v_1(l)/\sqrt2,\quad
 b_{(i,k,l;3)}=-v_0(i)v_1(k)w_1(l)/\sqrt2,\\
 u_1=\sum_ca_cq(Z_c),\quad u_3=\sum_cb_cq(Z_c),\quad
 S=u_1\cdot u_3,\quad T=S_{\mathcal R},\quad
 \sigma_*=\sqrt8\sin h,\quad\beta_0=\sigma_*/(2g^2),\\
 F=\beta_0(S-T),\qquad D=S+T .
 \end{gathered}\tag{AC4}
\]
The letter \(T\) in this note denotes the reflected full scalar \(S_{\mathcal R}\), not a Lie generator or a phase-amplitude bound. Reflection retains its reflected root, all reversed physical edges, and every complete reflected word. For example its direction-two target word in tree coordinates is \(A_{k,l}U_2(m-i,k,l)A_{k+1,l}^{-1}\), with
\[
 A_{k,l}=\left(\prod_{t=0}^{l-1}U_3(m,0,t)\right)
          \left(\prod_{b=0}^{k-1}U_2(m,b,l)\right),
 \tag{AC5}
\]
in increasing order. The original \(i=0\) tree edges and all empty products are retained. Thus the constants and affine remainders in PS44 and PG31 remain in \(F,D\).

Set, as full functions before taking expectations,
\[
 A=\tfrac12(S^2+T^2),\quad C=ST,\quad
 B=\tfrac12[\Gamma(S,S)+\Gamma(T,T)],\quad J=\Gamma(S,T).
 \tag{AC6}
\]
Each is reflection-even. Since reflection commutes with \(H\) and fixes its positive unit vacuum, \(\mu A=Q_4\), \(\mu B=K_1\), \(\mu C=Q_4^{\mathcal R}\), \(\mu J=K_{\mathcal R}\). For any \(s>0\), put \(\tau=\kappa/s\) and define the two signed densities
\[
 h_0=A-\tau B,\qquad
 h_D=D^2-\tau\Gamma(D,D)
       =2(A+C-\tau B-\tau J).
 \tag{AC7}
\]
The complete endpoint density is
\[
 h_F=F^2-\tau\Gamma(F,F)
     =2\beta_0^2(A-C-\tau B+\tau J)
     =\beta_0^2(4h_0-h_D).
 \tag{AC8}
\]
All identities here are pointwise. Consequently
\[
 \mu h_0=Q_4-\tau K_1,\qquad
 \mathcal E(s)=G-C_F/s=\mu h_F
   =\beta_0^2(4\mu h_0-\mu h_D),\quad
 G=\mu F^2,\quad C_F=\kappa\mu\Gamma(F,F).
 \tag{AC9}
\]
This is the existing SR33 identity in a form suitable for a joint calculation.

Means have not been discarded. Let \(m_S=\mu S=\mu T\), \(S_+=(S+T)/2-m_S\), and \(S_-=(S-T)/2\). Reflection makes the first function even and the second odd, both with zero actual mean. It also makes their mass and energy cross terms zero. Thus
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mu h_0&=m_S^2+\mu S_+^2+\mu S_-^2
                 -\tau[\mu\Gamma(S_+,S_+)+\mu\Gamma(S_-,S_-)],\\
 \mu h_D&=4m_S^2+4\mu S_+^2-4\tau\mu\Gamma(S_+,S_+),\\
 \mathcal E(s)&=4\beta_0^2[\mu S_-^2-\tau\mu\Gamma(S_-,S_-)] .
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC10}
\]
The first two lines exhibit the identical mean and even-sector contributions cancelling in their displayed combination. They do not assume \(m_S=0\) or \(D=0\).

\subsection*{2. The actual vacuum overlap supplied by the full Hamiltonian}

This section makes the input to the coupled estimate explicit. The existing physical electric gap is three: a nontrivial gauge-invariant spin support has no degree-one vertex, hence contains a cycle; the original cubic graph has no cycle shorter than four, and each nonzero edge spin costs at least \(3/4\). The fundamental face trace attains three.

One may retain the original global density-oscillation bound \(\Omega=32\pi\xi M\) of PK212, or its proved square-root-score improvement. The original bound already proves
\[
 \delta_{\rm v}=3\kappa e^{-\Omega},\qquad
 H-E_0\ge\delta_{\rm v}(I-|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|)
              \quad\hbox{on the physical space}.
 \tag{AC11}
\]
For clarity, the proof uses the full actual density. Changing edges one at a time along geodesics of length at most \(2\pi\), and using \(|\nabla_e\log\psi|\le2r_e\xi\), gives \(\operatorname{osc}\log\psi^2\le8\pi\xi\sum_er_e=32\pi\xi M\). If \(m_\rho=\min\rho\), \(M_\rho=\max\rho\), then
\(\operatorname{Var}_\mu f\le M_\rho\operatorname{Var}_{dU}f\le(M_\rho/3)\int\Gamma(f,f)dU\le e^\Omega\mu\Gamma(f,f)/3\).
The full ground-state identity from (AC2) proves (AC11). Every quantity is at the same original finite regulator.

Let \(P_\Lambda=\mathbf1_{[0,\Lambda]}(K)\) on the entire physical space, with both reflection parities retained, and let \(\phi_\Lambda\) be a unit lowest eigenvector of \(P_\Lambda H P_\Lambda\), with eigenvalue \(u_\Lambda\). Keep
\[
 \overline E=\ell=2\kappa\xi M,\quad
 \kappa\Lambda>\overline E,\quad
 w_\Lambda^{\rm bd}=\frac{\ell^2}{\kappa\Lambda-\overline E},\quad
 \eta_\Lambda=\frac{w_\Lambda^{\rm bd}}{\delta_{\rm v}}.
 \tag{AC12}
\]
These are the explicit original bounds underlying PK214; a smaller proved PK214 error can replace \(w_\Lambda^{\rm bd}\). Haar's constant trial vector gives \(E_0\le u_\Lambda\le\overline E\). On \(1-P_\Lambda\), positivity of the complete potential gives \(H\ge\kappa\Lambda\). Its off-diagonal block has norm at most \(\ell\), because it comes solely from \(-\kappa\xi\sum_pW_p\), whose norm is at most \(2\kappa\xi M\).

To derive the energy error directly, project the full equation \(H\psi=E_0\psi\) onto both blocks. The complementary inverse \((H_{QQ}-E_0)^{-1}\) exists and has norm at most \((\kappa\Lambda-E_0)^{-1}\). The component \(P_\Lambda\psi\) is nonzero, since otherwise \(E_0\ge\kappa\Lambda>\overline E\). Eliminating the complementary component gives
\[
 u_\Lambda-E_0
 \le \frac{\ell^2}{\kappa\Lambda-E_0}
 \le w_\Lambda^{\rm bd},\qquad
 1-|\langle\phi_\Lambda,\psi\rangle|^2\le\eta_\Lambda.
 \tag{AC13}
\]
The second inequality follows by applying (AC11) to \(\phi_\Lambda\). When \(\eta_\Lambda<1\), the lowest cutoff eigenspace is one-dimensional: a two-dimensional eigenspace would contain a unit vector orthogonal to \(\psi\), whose energy error is at least \(\delta_{\rm v}\), contrary to (AC13). Reflection preserves this eigenspace and its nonzero overlap with the even vacuum; hence \(\phi_\Lambda\) is even. Its phase can be chosen so \(\langle\phi_\Lambda,\psi\rangle\ge0\). These facts retain the finite approximation as an approximation; they do not identify it with \(\psi\).

For any chosen \(0<\eta_*<1/2\), the explicitly finite choice
\[
 \Lambda=1+\left\lceil\max\left\{3,\,
      \frac{\overline E+\ell^2/(\delta_{\rm v}\eta_*)}{\kappa}\right\}\right\rceil
 \tag{AC14}
\]
ensures \(\eta_\Lambda<\eta_*\). The size may be extremely large on the prescribed path. The unshifted scalar \(\overline E\) has not been removed from this cutoff.

\subsection*{3. A coupled expectation enclosure}

Write \(d\mu_\Lambda=|\phi_\Lambda|^2dU\). For the pair \(\mathbf h=(h_0,h_D)\), define the two finite-cutoff means and the complete covariance matrix
\[
 m_a^\Lambda=\mu_\Lambda h_a,\qquad
 \Sigma_{ab}^\Lambda
   =\mu_\Lambda(h_ah_b)-m_a^\Lambda m_b^\Lambda,\qquad
 a,b\in\{0,D\}.
 \tag{AC15}
\]
All means are explicit here. For any real vector \(c=(c_0,c_D)\), put
\[
 h_c=c_0h_0+c_Dh_D,\quad m_c^\Lambda=c^Tm^\Lambda,\quad
 \sigma_c^2=c^T\Sigma^\Lambda c,\quad
 l_c\le h_c(U)\le u_c\quad(U\in Q_L).
 \tag{AC16}
\]
The bounds \(l_c,u_c\) below are proved for the original functions. Suppose \(0\le\eta_\Lambda\le1/2\), as guaranteed by (AC14). Then the actual expectation vector obeys, simultaneously for every \(c\),
\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 c^T\mu\mathbf h
 &\ge m_c^\Lambda
       -2\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda(1-\eta_\Lambda)}\,\sigma_c
       -\eta_\Lambda(m_c^\Lambda-l_c),\\
 c^T\mu\mathbf h
 &\le m_c^\Lambda
       +2\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda(1-\eta_\Lambda)}\,\sigma_c
       +\eta_\Lambda(u_c-m_c^\Lambda).
 \end{aligned}}\tag{AC17}
\]
This is an intersection of directional enclosures for the same expectation vector, not a replacement by independent scalar intervals.

Here is the complete proof. Put \(q=1-|\langle\phi_\Lambda,\psi\rangle|^2\), \(0\le q\le\eta_\Lambda\), and decompose the actual unit vacuum as
\(\psi=\sqrt{1-q}\,\phi_\Lambda+\chi\), with \(\langle\phi_\Lambda,\chi\rangle=0\), \(\|\chi\|^2=q\). Expansion gives the exact identity
\[
 \mu h_c-m_c^\Lambda
 =2\sqrt{1-q}\,\operatorname{Re}
       \langle\chi,(h_c-m_c^\Lambda)\phi_\Lambda\rangle
       +\langle\chi,(h_c-m_c^\Lambda)\chi\rangle.
 \tag{AC18}
\]
The squared norm of the vector in the first inner product is exactly \(\sigma_c^2\). Cauchy–Schwarz bounds that term by \(2\sqrt{q(1-q)}\,\sigma_c\). Multiplication by \(h_c-m_c^\Lambda\) is between \(l_c-m_c^\Lambda\) and \(u_c-m_c^\Lambda\); the second term lies between \(q(l_c-m_c^\Lambda)\) and \(q(u_c-m_c^\Lambda)\). Both \(m_c^\Lambda-l_c\) and \(u_c-m_c^\Lambda\) are nonnegative. On \(0\le q\le1/2\), \(\sqrt{q(1-q)}\) and \(q\) are increasing, so replacing \(q\) by \(\eta_\Lambda\) in these two one-sided estimates proves (AC17). No moment of the actual vacuum has been replaced by the finite-cutoff mean.

Because \(\phi_\Lambda\) is even, the variance of the even \(h_0\) is no larger than that of the unsymmetrized \(S^2-\tau\Gamma(S,S)\). Indeed the latter is \(h_0+o_0\), with
\[
 o_0=\tfrac12\{S^2-T^2-\tau[\Gamma(S,S)-\Gamma(T,T)]\},
 \quad
 \mu_\Lambda o_0=\mu_\Lambda(h_0o_0)=0,\quad
 \operatorname{Var}_{\mu_\Lambda}(h_0+o_0)
       =\Sigma_{00}^\Lambda+\mu_\Lambda o_0^2.
 \tag{AC19}
\]
This even projection preserves the actual target mean as well, since \(\mu o_0=0\).

\subsection*{4. Every mixed term in the actual receiving direction}

Use \(c_F=\beta_0^2(4,-1)\) in (AC17). The finite-cutoff endpoint mean and its exact variance are
\[
 m_F^\Lambda=\mu_\Lambda h_F
       =\beta_0^2(4m_0^\Lambda-m_D^\Lambda),\qquad
 \sigma_F^2=\beta_0^4
      (16\Sigma_{00}^\Lambda+\Sigma_{DD}^\Lambda-8\Sigma_{0D}^\Lambda).
 \tag{AC20}
\]
The covariance coefficient \(-8\) is essential. For example, before subtracting its two means, its entry is exactly
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mu_\Lambda(h_0h_D)
 =\mu_\Lambda\{&
 A D^2-\tau A\,\Gamma(D,D)-\tau B D^2
                  +\tau^2B\,\Gamma(D,D)\}.
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC21}
\]
Each \(A,B,D\) here is the complete function (AC4)–(AC7), including every reflected transport and affine term. The covariance then subtracts
\((\mu_\Lambda A-\tau\mu_\Lambda B)
 (\mu_\Lambda D^2-\tau\mu_\Lambda\Gamma(D,D))\), with no zero-mean assumption.

Equivalently, the same full variance has the exact direct expression
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sigma_F^2={}&
 \mu_\Lambda F^4-2\tau\mu_\Lambda[F^2\Gamma(F,F)]
           +\tau^2\mu_\Lambda[\Gamma(F,F)^2]\\
 &-\{\mu_\Lambda F^2-\tau\mu_\Lambda\Gamma(F,F)\}^2 .
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC22}
\]
Thus the next coupled moment is a definite original-word expression of degree at most eight per physical link, rather than two unspecified approximation errors. No term in (AC22) is an actual-vacuum expectation unless it is explicitly written with \(\mu\).

In the invariant four functions of (AC6), expansion gives another complete check:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sigma_F^2/(4\beta_0^4)={}&
 \operatorname{Var}_\Lambda A+\operatorname{Var}_\Lambda C
                 -2\operatorname{Cov}_\Lambda(A,C)\\
 &+\tau^2\{\operatorname{Var}_\Lambda B+\operatorname{Var}_\Lambda J
                 -2\operatorname{Cov}_\Lambda(B,J)\}\\
 &-2\tau\{\operatorname{Cov}_\Lambda(A,B)
          -\operatorname{Cov}_\Lambda(A,J)
          -\operatorname{Cov}_\Lambda(C,B)
          +\operatorname{Cov}_\Lambda(C,J)\}.
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC23}
\]
These are ten covariance entries, each formed with its own retained means. Keeping only variances of mass and energy would discard the entire last line, which has a sign in the original endpoint variance.

\subsection*{5. Exact connection to the complete Poisson and spectral residuals}

For the actual vacuum, the full equation (AC2) and Haar integration by parts prove
\[
 \mu(f\mathcal Lg)=\mu\Gamma(f,g),\qquad
 \mu\mathcal Lg=0,\qquad
 \mathcal L(fg)=f\mathcal Lg+g\mathcal Lf-2\Gamma(f,g).
 \tag{AC24}
\]
All functions below are smooth on the compact product and hence lie in every operator domain used. Define the complete residual function \(R_F=F-\tau\mathcal LF\). The product rule gives the pointwise and vector identities
\[
 h_F=F R_F+\frac{\tau}{2}\mathcal L(F^2),\qquad
 \psi R_F=F\psi-\frac1s(H-E_0)(F\psi),\qquad
 \mathcal E(s)=\mu(FR_F).
 \tag{AC25}
\]
The divergence term is not pointwise zero. It has zero actual mean by (AC24). It need not have zero finite-cutoff mean, which is why (AC22) uses the polynomial \(h_F\) directly and does not insert the unknown actual score into a finite-cutoff calculation.

The exact relation of its actual variance to this full residual is
\[
 \operatorname{Var}_\mu h_F
 =\operatorname{Var}_\mu(FR_F)
   +\tau\,\mu\Gamma(FR_F,F^2)
   +\frac{\tau^2}{4}\mu[(\mathcal L(F^2))^2].
 \tag{AC26}
\]
Expand (AC25) after subtracting its mean, use \(\mu\mathcal L(F^2)=0\), and apply (AC24) to the cross term. This proves (AC26), including its sign and factor. It supplies the exact connecting map between the multiplication variance used in (AC22) and the complete Hamiltonian residual. The former is not the PK219 spectral second moment, and the displayed divergence and cross terms quantify their relation.

Here \(G>0\): choose the physical bulk chord \(e=(1,0,0;2)\), set it to \(\exp(\pi T_1)\), and set all other physical links to identity. The boundary products are identity, and (AC4) gives \(a_e b_e\ne0\), since \(m\ge4\). The original and reflected quadratic products have opposite coefficients, so \(F=8\beta_0a_eb_e\ne0\). At the identity configuration \(F=0\). Positivity of the continuous vacuum then gives \(G>0\), and connectedness gives \(C_F>0\). This reproduces PRE23's original-word witness rather than assuming either strict inequality.

More specifically, let \(\lambda=C_F/G\), \(v=F\psi\), and \(r=(H-E_0)v-\lambda v\). Then
\[
 \langle v,r\rangle=0,\qquad
 \|r\|^2=\|(H-E_0)v\|^2-C_F^2/G,\qquad
 \|\psi R_F\|^2=(1-\lambda/s)^2G+\|r\|^2/s^2.
 \tag{AC27}
\]
These follow by the orthogonal decomposition of the full operator action, retaining the original raw norm \(G\). They recover the rank-one case of PK223. In particular, a claim that the spectral residual alone determined (AC22) would omit the last two terms of (AC26) and the multiplication by \(F\).

PRE30–PRE31 already give the exact Poisson square using the full solution \(\mathcal Lu_F=F\), \(\mu u_F=0\). That square is not a new result here. The calculation above identifies the additional mixed multiplication covariance needed to estimate the signed density jointly; it neither assumes a small residual nor substitutes a conditional phase operator for the complete \(\mathcal L\).

\subsection*{6. What the all-physical-link degree-two property actually supplies}

The quaternion identity
\(q(U)\cdot q(V)=\operatorname{tr}(UV^{-1})-\operatorname{tr}(UV)\)
keeps \(S\) as the complete ordered trace sum with coefficients \(a_cb_d\). Vary a physical tree edge by an arbitrary \(V\in SU(2)\), not only one subgroup. If \(\epsilon_z\in\{0,1\}\) records whether its root path crosses the edge, every chord \(c=(s,t)\) transforms as \(Z_c\mapsto C^{\epsilon_s}Z_cC^{-\epsilon_t}\), with \(C=A_eVA_e^{-1}\); an inverse traversal uses \(V^{-1}\). For a second chord \(d=(u,w)\), cyclicity gives the complete formulas
\[
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{tr}(Z'_c(Z'_d)^{-1})
 &=\operatorname{tr}(Z_c C^{\epsilon_w-\epsilon_t}Z_d^{-1}
                         C^{\epsilon_s-\epsilon_u}),\\
\operatorname{tr}(Z'_cZ'_d)
 &=\operatorname{tr}(Z_c C^{\epsilon_u-\epsilon_t}Z_d
                         C^{\epsilon_s-\epsilon_w}).
\end{aligned}
\]
Every displayed exponent is in \(\{-1,0,1\}\). These two factors are linear in the four quaternion coordinates of \(V\). Chord edges occur at most twice in the pair. The same argument uses the full reflected tree for \(T\), with its own unchanged prefix and edge orientation.

It follows that \(S,T,F,D\), as functions of any one original physical group factor with all other factors fixed, lie in \(\mathcal H_0\oplus\mathcal H_1\oplus\mathcal H_2\). This strengthens the subgroup wording to the complete quaternion-polynomial statement, with the exact word transformation providing the comparison. A physical derivative preserves this degree in every factor: its vector field is linear in that factor's quaternion coordinates and leaves the polynomial degree unchanged; differentiation in a different factor differentiates coefficients only. Therefore
\[
 \deg_e F\le2,\quad
 \deg_e\Gamma(F,F)\le4,\quad
 \deg_e h_0,\deg_e h_D,\deg_e h_F\le4,\quad
 \deg_e(h_ah_b)\le8
       \quad(a,b\in\{0,D,F\})
 \tag{AC28}
\]
for every original physical edge. The corresponding maximum spins are \(1,2,2,4\). Products with the finite spin polynomial \(\phi_\Lambda\) are finite original-coordinate polynomials. Thus all means and mixed moments in (AC15)–(AC23) reduce to finite Haar contractions of precisely the kind in PK218, with no new unknown vacuum required for their finite-cutoff evaluation.

There is also an explicit exact test of what the degree information leaves uncancelled. Let \(P_ef\) be Haar integration in edge \(e\) only and let \(Z_ef(U)=f(\ldots,-U_e,\ldots)\). The radius-two Casimir eigenvalues on \(\mathcal H_0,\mathcal H_1,\mathcal H_2\) are \(0,3/4,2\). Since \(Z_e\) acts by \(1,-1,1\), comparison of these three eigenspaces proves
\[
 K_ef=\frac{11}{8}f+\frac58Z_ef-2P_ef
     =2(f-P_ef)+\frac58(Z_ef-f)
          \quad(f=S,T,F,D).
 \tag{AC29}
\]
Here \(K_e=-\sum_\alpha X_{e,\alpha}^2\); the complete three-colour sum is already included. Applying the actual integration-by-parts identity gives
\[
 \mu\Gamma(F,F)
 =\sum_e\left\{
   \frac{11}{8}G+\frac58\mu(FZ_eF)
       -2\mu(FP_eF)-\mu[F\Gamma_e(\log\rho,F)]\right\}.
 \tag{AC30}
\]
The last term is the entire actual density drift, since \(\log\rho=2\log\psi\). This exact formula retains the signed cross expectations whose control would be needed to replace the separate \(N\)-edge estimate. Neither the actual density nor the original phase is invariant under an individual central link flip in general. Therefore the terms \(\mu(FZ_eF)\), \(\mu(FP_eF)\), and the drift cannot be replaced by their Haar counterparts. Degree two supplies the exact finite operator (AC29), but does not itself establish a smaller actual quotient.

\subsection*{7. Explicit original ranges and an attempted path estimate}

Use \(c_h=\cos h\), \(C_h=\cot h\). The exact original absolute row and repeated-occurrence sums are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 S_1=\sqrt{2/n}\,C_h(C_h-c_h),\\
 S_3=\sqrt{2/n}[(2-1/n)C_h^2-(c_h/n)C_h],\\
 A_1=m\sqrt{2/n}\,C_h(5C_h-4c_h),\\
 A_3=(m/n)\sqrt{2/n}[(9m+5)C_h^2-2c_hC_h],\\
 U_0=16S_1^2S_3^2,\qquad
 C_{\rm occ}=12[S_3\sqrt{S_1A_1}+S_1\sqrt{S_3A_3}]^2 .
 \end{gathered}\tag{AC31}
\]
These are IP2–IP5's complete original-word sums. They retain both occurrences of a shared root edge. Since \(|q|\le2\), \(|S|,|T|\le4S_1S_3\). The occurrence derivative calculation bounds \(\Gamma(S,S),\Gamma(T,T)\le C_{\rm occ}\). Triangle inequality for the complete derivative vectors then gives \(\Gamma(F,F)\le4\beta_0^2C_{\rm occ}\) and \(\Gamma(D,D)\le4C_{\rm occ}\). Consequently the following original-function ranges are available in (AC17):
\[
 \begin{array}{c|cc}
 \text{density}&l&u\\ \hline
 h_0&-\tau C_{\rm occ}&U_0\\
 h_D&-4\tau C_{\rm occ}&4U_0\\
 h_F&-4\tau\beta_0^2C_{\rm occ}&4\beta_0^2U_0 .
 \end{array}\tag{AC32}
\]
The last row follows directly from the squares in (AC8). Combining independent ranges for \(4h_0-h_D\) would give the larger interval
\([-4\beta_0^2(\tau C_{\rm occ}+U_0),4\beta_0^2(U_0+\tau C_{\rm occ})]\);
thus retaining the complete odd phase also improves the tail term of the enclosure.

The fully coupled endpoint enclosure now reads
\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal E(s)&\ge m_F^\Lambda
 -2\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda(1-\eta_\Lambda)}\,\sigma_F
 -\eta_\Lambda(m_F^\Lambda+4\tau\beta_0^2C_{\rm occ}),\\
 \mathcal E(s)&\le m_F^\Lambda
 +2\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda(1-\eta_\Lambda)}\,\sigma_F
 +\eta_\Lambda(4\beta_0^2U_0-m_F^\Lambda).
 \end{aligned}}\tag{AC33}
\]
For \(Q_4-\tau K_1\), use \(m_0^\Lambda,\sqrt{\Sigma_{00}^\Lambda}\) and the first row of (AC32) in (AC17). Both statements hold for the same original vacuum and the same finite cutoff; (AC20) keeps their joint covariance rather than making independent choices of their extrema.

For comparison with a bound that has not yet evaluated (AC22), any real \(h\in[l,u]\) satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Var}_{\mu_\Lambda}h
 \le(u-\mu_\Lambda h)(\mu_\Lambda h-l)\le(u-l)^2/4.
 \tag{AC34}
\]
Indeed \((h-l)(u-h)\ge0\); take its mean and expand the resulting quadratic inequality. Substitution in (AC17), together with the sharper centered projection estimate below, gives the explicit coarse errors
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 |\mu h-\mu_\Lambda h|
 &\le[\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda(1-\eta_\Lambda)}+\eta_\Lambda](u-l),\\
 |\mu h-\mu_\Lambda h|&\le\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda}(u-l).
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{AC35}
\]
For the second line, the difference of the two unit-vector projections has eigenvalues \(+\sqrt q,-\sqrt q\) on their two-dimensional span and zero elsewhere. Its trace is zero, so subtracting the retained scalar \((l+u)/2\) from the multiplication operator does not change the expectation difference. The centered operator has norm at most \((u-l)/2\); the trace norm of the projection difference is \(2\sqrt q\). This proves the second line, including all factors. It is also an immediate strengthening of PK215 when an observable's complete range is known.

For \(h=h_F\), the width is
\(R_{F,j}=4\beta_{0,j}^2(U_{0,j}+jC_{{\rm occ},j})\).
Applying PK215 separately without centering gives \(2\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda}R_{F,j}\); centering each nonnegative observable already gives the sharper second line of (AC35). Thus the potential further gain in (AC33) is the actual finite-cutoff covariance in (AC22), not an unearned universal factor improvement. Intersect every directional interval (AC17) with \([m_c^\Lambda-\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda}(u_c-l_c),m_c^\Lambda+\sqrt{\eta_\Lambda}(u_c-l_c)]\). The resulting simultaneous enclosure is never weaker than that centered range estimate. No small variance is assumed.

Now keep the prescribed path exactly:
\[
 L_j=j^4,\quad a_j=(100j)^{-1},\quad
 g_j^2=\kappa_*/(200j),\quad
 \kappa_j=\kappa_*,\quad\xi_j=10000j^2/\kappa_*^2,\quad
 s_j=\kappa_*/j,\quad m_j=2j^4,\quad n_j=2j^4+1 .
 \tag{AC36}
\]
It has \(\tau_j=j\) and \(j^6\beta_{0,j}^2\to5000\pi^2/\kappa_*^2\). The trigonometric formulas in (AC31) give, with all finite boundary terms still present there,
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \frac{U_{0,j}}{j^{24}}\longrightarrow\frac{2^{22}}{\pi^8},\\
 \frac{C_{{\rm occ},j}}{j^{28}}
 \longrightarrow\frac{3\,2^{21}}{\pi^8}
                  \left(\sqrt5+\frac3{\sqrt2}\right)^2,\\
 \frac{R_{F,j}}{j^{23}}
 \longrightarrow
 \frac{60000\,2^{21}}{\kappa_*^2\pi^6}
                  \left(\sqrt5+\frac3{\sqrt2}\right)^2 .
 \end{gathered}\tag{AC37}
\]
To verify these constants, \(S_1\sim4\sqrt2\,n^{3/2}/\pi^2\), \(S_3\sim8\sqrt2\,n^{3/2}/\pi^2\), \(A_1/S_1\sim5m\), \(A_3/S_3\sim(9/2)m\), and \(n,m\sim2j^4\). Insert these factors into each unchanged expression (AC31); the second line contributes the leading \(j^{29}\) part of \(U_0+jC_{\rm occ}\), and the original \(4\beta_0^2\) supplies the last line.

Thus merely using the available absolute ranges in the new covariance certificate still requires vacuum-overlap error substantially smaller than \(j^{-46}\) to obtain a vanishing absolute endpoint error. For example, selecting
\[
 \eta_{*,j}=\min\left\{\frac14,\,
       \frac{1}{16j^2(1+R_{F,j})^2}\right\}
 \tag{AC38}
\]
in (AC14) proves \(|\mathcal E_j-m_{F,j}^\Lambda|\le1/(2j)\): use \(\sqrt{\eta}+\eta\le2\sqrt{\eta}\) and
\(2\sqrt{\eta_{*,j}}R_{F,j}\le1/(2j)\).
This is a finite certified error, not a computed value of \(m_{F,j}^\Lambda\). It does not assert practical feasibility of the resulting electric matrix. It also does not give a sign because the finite-cutoff mean itself has not been evaluated here.

\subsection*{8. What the attempt establishes and the next exact coupling}

The old score-only estimate SR40 gives a lower endpoint coefficient that eventually becomes negative. Equations (AC29)–(AC30) identify exactly which signed original-word and density-drift terms that estimate fails to control. The calculation then proceeds to the coupled signed density (AC8), its exact covariance (AC20)–(AC23), and the actual-vacuum enclosure (AC33). Those formulas are fully proved and retain the actual state.

The next finite computation is now specific: for the same \(\phi_\Lambda\), evaluate the complete original-word contractions
\[
 \mu_\Lambda F^2,\quad
 \mu_\Lambda\Gamma(F,F),\quad
 \mu_\Lambda F^4,\quad
 \mu_\Lambda[F^2\Gamma(F,F)],\quad
 \mu_\Lambda[\Gamma(F,F)^2],
 \tag{AC39}
\]
and, for the joint \(Q_4-jK_1\) and even-correction enclosure, the entries of (AC15) with the expansion (AC21). Their finite evaluation uses the proved per-link spin bounds (AC28), the original cutoff eigenvector, and exact Haar contractions. The negative mixed term in (AC22) is retained before estimation. It is not assigned a sign or a magnitude beyond its exact identity.

The endpoint is positive whenever the already-proved numerical lower expression in (AC33) evaluates positive; this is a description of how to read the enclosure, not an assumed theorem premise. No such numerical positivity is claimed in this note. Nor is the known fixed-box oscillator limit exchanged with the growing-box path.

For all \(s>0\), oddness of \(F\psi\) still gives the actual spectral consequence
\[
 \langle F\psi,\mathbf1_{(0,s]}(H-E_0)F\psi\rangle
 \ge\max\{0,\mathcal E(s)\}.
 \tag{AC40}
\]
This follows by integrating \(\mathbf1_{(0,s]}(\lambda)\ge1-\lambda/s\) over its positive spectral measure, whose vacuum atom vanishes. It retains the raw vector and completes the exact receiving map for the coupled certificate.

\subsection*{9. Calculating the mixed original density through the full vacuum equation}

The list (AC39) determines finite inputs, but does not evaluate its central mixed density. We now calculate that coupling exactly. Write
\[
 I_F=\mu[F^2\Gamma(F,F)],\quad
 V=\kappa\xi\sum_p(2-W_p),\quad
 w=\log\psi,\quad
 {\cal T}(U)=\frac{E_0-V(U)}{\kappa}-\Gamma(w,w).
 \tag{AC41}
\]
The scalar \(2\kappa\xi M\) remains inside \(V\), and the complete score norm in \({\cal T}\) contains every original physical edge and colour. The chain and product rules for the nonnegative operator \(K\) give
\(K(F^4)=4F^3KF-12F^2\Gamma(F,F)\) and
\(K(\psi^2)=2\psi K\psi-2\Gamma(\psi,\psi)=2{\cal T}\psi^2\).
Integration by parts in product Haar, followed by the full equation \(K\psi=(E_0-V)\psi/\kappa\), therefore gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 I_F
 &=\frac13\mu(F^3{\cal L}F)
  =\frac13\mu(F^3KF)-\frac23\mu[F^3\Gamma(w,F)]\\
 &=\frac13\mu(F^3KF)-\frac16\mu({\cal T}F^4)\\
 &=\frac{11N}{24}\mu F^4
     +\frac5{24}\sum_e\mu(F^3Z_eF)
     -\frac23\sum_e\mu(F^3P_eF)\\
 &\hspace{7mm}
     -\frac{E_0}{6\kappa}\mu F^4
     +\frac{\xi}{6}\sum_p\mu[(2-W_p)F^4]
     +\frac16\mu[\Gamma(w,w)F^4].
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC42}
\]
For the first line, \(\Gamma(F,F^3)=3F^2\Gamma(F,F)\) and (AC24) suffice. For the second, the identity
\(\mu\Gamma(w,F^4)=\mu({\cal T}F^4)\)
follows by integrating \(\Gamma(\psi^2,F^4)=2\psi^2\Gamma(w,F^4)\), using \(K(\psi^2)=2{\cal T}\psi^2\). Since \(\Gamma(w,F^4)=4F^3\Gamma(w,F)\), the factor \(1/6\) follows. The final two lines insert the complete (AC29) into \(KF\). Every term is an actual expectation. In particular the last two terms have a positive sign; dropping the score square or the \(2\) in a face term would change this identity.

There is a second finite Casimir calculation of the same mixed density, keeping the full physical link and avoiding score differentiation. On the space of quaternion polynomials of degree at most four, the five eigenvalues of \(K_e\) are
\[
 (\lambda_0,\lambda_1,\lambda_2,\lambda_3,\lambda_4)
       =(0,3/4,2,15/4,6),\qquad
 {\sf P}_{e,l}=
   \prod_{\substack{0\le r\le4\\r\ne l}}
       \frac{K_e-\lambda_r I}{\lambda_l-\lambda_r},
 \quad 0\le l\le4 .
 \tag{AC43}
\]
These are exact spectral projections on this finite polynomial space: on the degree-\(r\) harmonic each product evaluates to its Kronecker delta. They are not conditional projections for the actual density. Their sum is the identity, and \(K_e=\sum_l\lambda_l{\sf P}_{e,l}\) on that space. Since \(F^2\) has degree at most four, its full components \(f_{e,l}={\sf P}_{e,l}(F^2)\) are defined without a truncation error. The product rule and (AC29) give the pointwise identity
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \Gamma_e(F,F)
 &=\frac{11}{8}F^2+\frac58FZ_eF-2FP_eF\\
 &\quad-\frac38f_{e,1}-f_{e,2}
             -\frac{15}{8}f_{e,3}-3f_{e,4}.
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC44}
\]
Thus every term of the mixed original density, prior to any expectation, is explicitly specified by degree at most eight polynomials per link:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 I_F={}&\frac{11N}{8}\mu F^4
       +\frac58\sum_e\mu(F^3Z_eF)-2\sum_e\mu(F^3P_eF)\\
 &-\frac14\sum_e\sum_{l,r=0}^4
          (\lambda_l+\lambda_r)\,\mu(f_{e,l}f_{e,r}).
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC45}
\]
Indeed \(F^2=\sum_rf_{e,r}\), and symmetrizing
\(\frac12\sum_{e,l,r}\lambda_l\mu(f_{e,l}f_{e,r})\)
gives the last line. Its scalar harmonic \(f_{e,0}\), all cross harmonics, and their nonzero means are retained. If
\(M^e_{lr}=\operatorname{Cov}_\mu(f_{e,l},f_{e,r})
              +(\mu f_{e,l})(\mu f_{e,r})\),
the last line is exactly \(-\frac14\sum_{e,l,r}(\lambda_l+\lambda_r)M^e_{lr}\).
Actual weighting does not make these different Haar harmonics orthogonal; setting the off-diagonal entries to zero is therefore unjustified. Equations (AC42) and (AC45) describe the exact drift and weighted-harmonic terms which such a step would lose. The pointwise construction (AC44), and consequently (AC45), remain valid when their expectations are taken with \(\mu_\Lambda\). The use of the true vacuum equation in (AC42) is reserved for \(\mu\).

The full Hamiltonian gives a useful additional coupling with its sign already fixed. The ground-state identity applied to the entire original square \(F^2\), not to a changed phase, proves
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 4\kappa I_F
 &=\langle F^2\psi,(H-E_0)F^2\psi\rangle\\
 &=\langle (F^2-G)\psi,(H-E_0)(F^2-G)\psi\rangle\\
 &\ge\delta_{\rm v}(\mu F^4-G^2),\qquad G=\mu F^2 .
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC46}
\]
The second equality expands \(F^2\psi=G\psi+(F^2-G)\psi\); the cross terms and \(G^2E_0\) contribution vanish only after using the complete equation \((H-E_0)\psi=0\). Its raw norm is \(\mu F^4-G^2\), with the mean retained. The first equality follows because
\(\Gamma(F^2,F^2)=4F^2\Gamma(F,F)\). This is an even-composite excitation coupled to the original odd phase.

Consequently, if \(E_F=\mu\Gamma(F,F)\) and
\({\cal A}_{F^2}=\langle F^2\psi,(H-E_0)F^2\psi\rangle\), then
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \operatorname{Var}_\mu h_F
 &=\mu F^4-\frac{{\cal A}_{F^2}}{2s}
       +\frac{\kappa^2}{s^2}\mu[\Gamma(F,F)^2]
       -(G-\kappa E_F/s)^2\\
 &\le\left(1-\frac{\delta_{\rm v}}{2s}\right)
             \operatorname{Var}_\mu(F^2)
       +\frac{\kappa^2}{s^2}\operatorname{Var}_\mu(\Gamma(F,F))
       +\frac{2\kappa}{s}G E_F .
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC47}
\]
The mixed energy appears with a negative coefficient in the variance, so its lower bound in (AC46) has the correct direction for this upper bound. This is a coupled use of the full equation, not a separate lower bound on \(G\) or \(C_F\). The last line follows by expanding both variances and using \(I_F\ge\delta_{\rm v}\operatorname{Var}_\mu(F^2)/(4\kappa)\); every remaining mean is shown.

The cutoff version requires a further term. Let
\[
 e_\Lambda=(H-u_\Lambda)\phi_\Lambda,\quad
 P_\Lambda e_\Lambda=0,\quad
 G_\Lambda=\mu_\Lambda F^2,\quad
 z_\Lambda=(F^2-G_\Lambda)\phi_\Lambda .
 \tag{AC48}
\]
The residual is finite and computable: because \(P_\Lambda\) commutes with \(K\),
\(e_\Lambda=-\kappa\xi(1-P_\Lambda)(\sum_pW_p)\phi_\Lambda\).
The scalar \(2\kappa\xi M-u_\Lambda\) lies entirely in the cutoff space and cancels in this displayed residual only after the full eigenvalue equation has been used. For any real smooth \(f\), direct expansion of the kinetic quadratic form yields
\[
 \langle f\phi_\Lambda,(H-u_\Lambda)f\phi_\Lambda\rangle
 =\kappa\mu_\Lambda\Gamma(f,f)
       +\operatorname{Re}\langle f^2\phi_\Lambda,e_\Lambda\rangle .
\]
The two derivative cross terms in \(|\nabla(f\phi_\Lambda)|^2\) are precisely the cross terms produced by integrating
\(\operatorname{Re}\langle f^2\phi_\Lambda,K\phi_\Lambda\rangle\);
the potential and eigenvalue terms multiply \(f^2|\phi_\Lambda|^2\) identically. Thus no positivity or eigenvector identity for a changed state has been inserted. With \(f=F^2\), this proves
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 4\kappa\mu_\Lambda[F^2\Gamma(F,F)]
 &=\langle F^2\phi_\Lambda,(H-u_\Lambda)F^2\phi_\Lambda\rangle
       -\operatorname{Re}\langle F^4\phi_\Lambda,e_\Lambda\rangle\\
 &=\langle z_\Lambda,(H-u_\Lambda)z_\Lambda\rangle
       -\operatorname{Re}
          \langle(F^2-G_\Lambda)^2\phi_\Lambda,e_\Lambda\rangle .
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC49}
\]
The second line follows by expanding both complete squares: the \(2G_\Lambda\) cross terms cancel, and the \(G_\Lambda^2\) term vanishes because \(\langle\phi_\Lambda,e_\Lambda\rangle=0\). This is an exact reduction of the mixed moment to the unshifted finite Hamiltonian, its explicitly specified boundary residual, and multiplication by the complete original phase. All three vectors are finite original spin polynomials; (AC28) bounds their added per-link degrees. No unknown actual score enters their computation.

In particular (AC22) has the following complete Hamiltonian form, which can be inserted directly in the already-proved actual-state enclosure (AC33):
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sigma_F^2={}&\mu_\Lambda F^4
 -\frac1{2s}
     \langle F^2\phi_\Lambda,(H-u_\Lambda)F^2\phi_\Lambda\rangle\\
 &+\frac1{2s}\operatorname{Re}
                  \langle F^4\phi_\Lambda,e_\Lambda\rangle
 +\frac{\kappa^2}{s^2}\mu_\Lambda[\Gamma(F,F)^2]
 -(G_\Lambda-\kappa\mu_\Lambda\Gamma(F,F)/s)^2 .
 \end{aligned}\tag{AC50}
\]
The positive and negative signs of the two new full-operator terms are fixed by (AC49). Omitting the boundary residual would generally change the finite-cutoff variance.

The remaining gradient-square term also has a complete finite polynomial expression. Define, in the indicated order,
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 Y_e=(F^2,\ FZ_eF,\ FP_eF,\ f_{e,1},\ f_{e,2},\ f_{e,3},\ f_{e,4})^T,\qquad
 c=(11/8,\ 5/8,\ -2,\ -3/8,\ -1,\ -15/8,\ -3)^T,\\
 \mu_\Lambda\Gamma(F,F)=
       \sum_e\sum_{a=1}^7c_a\mu_\Lambda Y_{e,a},\\
 \mu_\Lambda[\Gamma(F,F)^2]=
       \sum_{e,f}\sum_{a,b=1}^7
                   c_ac_b\,\mu_\Lambda(Y_{e,a}Y_{f,b}).
 \end{gathered}
\]
This follows by squaring the pointwise formula (AC44), with every ordered edge pair and every one of its 49 component pairs included. The moments are raw moments; replacing each by a covariance also requires adding
\((\mu_\Lambda Y_{e,a})(\mu_\Lambda Y_{f,b})\).
Each product has per-link degree at most eight. Together with (AC49), this specifies all finite contractions in (AC50), including the cross-edge contributions rather than just the \(e=f\) terms.

Finally test the new actual coupling on the unchanged path. With the retained original bound,
\(\delta_{{\rm v},j}/(2s_j)=(3j/2)\exp[-32\pi\xi_jM_j]\), while
\[
 \frac{32\pi\xi_jM_j}{j^{14}}
       \longrightarrow\frac{7\,680\,000\pi}{\kappa_*^2}.
\]
Hence this coefficient tends to zero. The proved square-root score bound would improve that exponent's order, but still does not make this coefficient a positive uniform fraction. Thus (AC46) supplies a correctly signed exact coupling and (AC47) a valid bound, while this available gap input does not force the mixed moment to cancel the gradient-square variance on the original path. The remaining calculated quantity is the weighted quartic polynomial in (AC44), squared and summed with all edge cross terms, together with the finite residual in (AC50). Those are explicit finite contractions, not an assumed cancellation. The actual endpoint and the sign of its cutoff mean have still not been numerically evaluated.


\begin{figure}[htbp]\centering\includegraphics[width=\linewidth,height=.72\textheight,keepaspectratio]{COUPLED_ENDPOINT_MAP.png}\caption{Exact signed-density and covariance maps, with the same finite-cutoff state throughout; AC8, AC15–AC23 and AC33. These are proved relations, not evaluated vacuum data.}\end{figure}

Provider proofs: \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/PERIOD_COUPLING_AND_PHYSICAL_KERNELS.md}{PK158–PK227}, \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/ade565f79be8b0eea8dc8e8a95a4034dde91644f/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/PHASE_RESPONSE_AND_ENDPOINT.md}{PRE1–PRE55}, and \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/ade565f79be8b0eea8dc8e8a95a4034dde91644f/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/SQUARE_ROOT_SCORE_RECEIVING_MAPS.md}{SR1–SR72}. Original source versions and bounded reading coverage are retained in COUPLED\_SOURCE\_USE\_LEDGER.json.
\clearpage
\section*{The actual finite-vacuum semigroup limit and its spectral escape space}

11 October 2026. This is a construction from the prescribed finite-volume Yang–Mills Hamiltonians, their actual positive unit physical vacua, and the unchanged path. One common nonprincipal ultrafilter is used for every object. No uniform spectral tightness, zero-time continuity, raw-phase normalization, mass gap, or interacting four-dimensional identification is assumed. The complete spectral escape space is retained. The original Wilson algebra has a stronger result: its operators reduce the continuous subspace, so its compression is a representation.

The finite-vacuum providers are \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/af1715c22ac52ba5fcc1d3768f2c6f0895de79ac/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/GLOBAL_PHYSICAL_RESPONSE.md}{GPR1–GPR8 and GPR53}, and the raw coefficient/word provider is \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/af1715c22ac52ba5fcc1d3768f2c6f0895de79ac/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_SCORE_AND_ORIGINAL_ENDPOINT.md}{PS36–PS39}. The actual curvature budget used in Section 9 is \href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/dbbcbaf7e8d50e41f7491dc9bbed1c0c6ed54b0c/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/CURVATURE_ROOTED_WORDS.md}{CW14–CW15}, whose complete coefficient sums CW33 are also retained below. All facts from them used below are stated and proved again. Earlier free scalar reconstructions use different oscillator spaces and explicit mode isometries; none is substituted for the present physical spaces. For the reconstruction comparison, the original author TeX of Ashtekar, Marolf, Mourão and Thiemann, \href{https://arxiv.org/abs/quant-ph/9904094}{quant-ph/9904094}, Definition 1 and Theorem 1, Lemma 6, distinguishes reflection positivity from continuity and vacuum uniqueness. Here continuity is proved on an explicitly constructed subspace, with its complement kept. Source-reading coverage and exact hashes are in ACTUAL\_LIMIT\_SOURCE\_USE\_LEDGER.json. The elementary spectral theorem for self-adjoint operators and continuous functional calculus are the functional-analytic foundations used below.

\subsection*{1. Original finite spaces, path and energy identity}

Fix \(\kappa_*>0\) and integer \(j\ge2\). Retain
\[
 L_j=j^4,\quad m_j=2j^4,\quad n_j=2j^4+1,\quad
 a_j=(100j)^{-1},\quad g_j^2=\kappa_*/(200j),\quad
 \xi_j=10000j^2/\kappa_*^2,\quad \kappa_j=\kappa_*,\quad
 h_j=\pi/(2n_j),\quad s_j=\kappa_*/j.
 \tag{ASL1}
\]
The vertices are \(V_j=\{-L_j,\ldots,L_j\}^3\), the positive edges are \(E_j=\{(x,i):x,x+e_i\in V_j\}\), and the positively ordered faces are \(\mathcal P_j=\{(x;i,k):i<k,\ x,x+e_i,x+e_k,x+e_i+e_k\in V_j\}\). Thus
\[
 N_j=|E_j|=3m_jn_j^2,\quad M_j=|\mathcal P_j|=3m_j^2n_j,\quad
 Q_j=SU(2)^{E_j},\quad dU_j=\bigotimes_{e\in E_j}dU_e.
 \tag{ASL2}
\]
Every Haar measure is probability measure. Every vertex, including each boundary vertex, acts by \(U_{st}\mapsto q_sU_{st}q_t^{-1}\). The physical space \(\mathcal H_j\) is the invariant subspace of \(L^2(Q_j,dU_j)\). All scalar products are antilinear in the first entry. With \(T_\alpha=-i\sigma_\alpha/2\), the original left fields \(X_{e,\alpha}\) are orthonormal; the edge metric is the round metric of radius two. Keep
\[
\begin{aligned}
 W_{(x;i,k)}&=\operatorname{Tr}\bigl[U_i(x)U_k(x+e_i)U_i(x+e_k)^{-1}U_k(x)^{-1}\bigr],\\
 K_j&=-\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}^2,\qquad
 H_j=\kappa_*K_j+\kappa_*\xi_j\sum_{p\in\mathcal P_j}(2-W_p),\\
 H_j\psi_j&=E_j^{\rm vac}\psi_j,\quad \psi_j>0,\quad
 \int\psi_j^2dU_j=1,\quad A_j=H_j-E_j^{\rm vac}I,\quad
 d\mu_j=\psi_j^2dU_j.
\end{aligned}\tag{ASL3}
\]
The notation \(E_j^{\rm vac}\) avoids collision with the original edge set. The scalar \(2\kappa_*\xi_jM_j\) and the full vacuum energy remain in (ASL3).

For completeness, on the compact connected product the Casimir has discrete product-spin spectrum tending to infinity with finite multiplicities. Its resolvent is compact. The real smooth bounded potential preserves self-adjointness on the second Sobolev space and the first Sobolev form domain. A lowest Rayleigh minimizer exists, its modulus does not increase the kinetic form, elliptic regularity makes it smooth, and the strong maximum principle makes it positive. The product rule and integration by parts give, for \(v=\psi_j f\) in the form domain,
\[
 q_j(v):=\|A_j^{1/2}v\|^2
 =\kappa_*\int\psi_j^2\sum_{e,\alpha}|X_{e,\alpha}f|^2dU_j.
 \tag{ASL4}
\]
First prove the identity for smooth \(f\): expand \(K_j(\psi_jf)\), substitute the complete equation (ASL3), and integrate the mixed term. Both scalar terms cancel by that equation, rather than being removed from the Hamiltonian. Density extends the identity, since division by a fixed positive smooth \(\psi_j\) preserves the finite-volume Sobolev form domain. A second ground vector divided by \(\psi_j\) has zero derivative and is constant. Thus the ground eigenspace is one-dimensional. Gauge transformations fix its positive unit vector, so the full and physical ground energies agree and \(A_j\ge0\) on \(\mathcal H_j\). This also proves the stated properties of the actual vacua.

\subsection*{2. One Hilbert ultraproduct and the zero-time projection}

Choose once and for all a nonprincipal ultrafilter \(\mathcal U\) on \(\{2,3,\ldots\}\). Every bounded scalar sequence has a unique \(\mathcal U\)-limit: compactness supplies an accumulation point selected by the ultrafilter, and disjoint neighbourhoods prove uniqueness. Sums, products, conjugates and inequalities commute with this limit by their continuity. Define
\[
 \ell^\infty(\mathcal H_j)=\{(v_j):\sup_j\|v_j\|<\infty\},\qquad
 \mathcal N_{\mathcal U}=\{(v_j):\lim_{\mathcal U}\|v_j\|=0\},\qquad
 \mathcal H_{\mathcal U}=\ell^\infty(\mathcal H_j)/\mathcal N_{\mathcal U},
 \quad \langle[v_j],[w_j]\rangle=\lim_{\mathcal U}\langle v_j,w_j\rangle.
 \tag{ASL5}
\]
The scalar product is well defined by Cauchy–Schwarz and positive definite on the quotient. Its norm equals the Banach quotient norm. Indeed it is at most every representative's supremum norm. In the other direction, if \(a=\lim_{\mathcal U}\|v_j\|\), then \(S_\epsilon=\{j:\|v_j\|\le a+\epsilon\}\) belongs to \(\mathcal U\). Replacing \(v_j\) by zero off \(S_\epsilon\) changes it by an element of \(\mathcal N_{\mathcal U}\) and bounds its supremum by \(a+\epsilon\). The space \(\ell^\infty(\mathcal H_j)\) is complete by componentwise limits of uniformly Cauchy sequences, and \(\mathcal N_{\mathcal U}\) is closed. To see explicitly that its quotient is complete, select a subsequence of any quotient Cauchy sequence whose successive quotient distances are at most \(2^{-2r}\), lift each difference to norm at most \(2^{-r}\), and sum the absolutely convergent series in \(\ell^\infty\). Its quotient is the limit of that subsequence and hence of the Cauchy sequence. Thus (ASL5) is a Hilbert space.

A uniformly bounded sequence \(B_j\in\mathcal B(\mathcal H_j)\) induces \(B[v_j]=[B_jv_j]\). Products and adjoints are preserved and
\[
 \|B\|\le\lim_{\mathcal U}\|B_j\|,\qquad
 \Omega=[\psi_j],\quad\|\Omega\|=1,\qquad
 T(t)=[e^{-tA_j}]\quad(t>0).
 \tag{ASL6}
\]
These assertions follow directly from (ASL5); in particular the vacuum has its original norm. Each \(T(t)\) is a positive self-adjoint contraction, \(T(t)T(s)=T(t+s)\), and \(T(t)\Omega=\Omega\). For \(t>0,h\ge0\), spectral calculus gives
\[
 \|T(t+h)-T(t)\|
 \le\sup_{\lambda\ge0}e^{-t\lambda}(1-e^{-h\lambda})
 \le {h\over et}.
 \tag{ASL7}
\]
Here \(1-e^{-h\lambda}\le h\lambda\) and \(\sup\lambda e^{-t\lambda}=1/(et)\). The same estimate with the smaller positive time proves norm continuity throughout \((0,\infty)\), without a zero-time assumption.

As \(t\downarrow0\), \(T(t)\) increases in the operator order and is bounded by \(I\). Its strong limit exists: for \(0<s<t\), the positive contraction \(D=T(s)-T(t)\) satisfies \(\|Dv\|^2\le\langle v,Dv\rangle\); the latter is Cauchy because \(\langle v,T(t)v\rangle\) is bounded monotone. Denote the strong limit by \(P\). Strong convergence of products of uniformly bounded operators and the semigroup law yield
\[
 P^*=P,\quad P^2=\lim_{t\downarrow0}T(t)^2
 =\lim_{t\downarrow0}T(2t)=P,\qquad
 PT(t)=T(t)P=T(t),\quad P\Omega=\Omega.
 \tag{ASL8}
\]
For the middle identity take \(s\downarrow0\) in \(T(s)T(t)=T(s+t)\), using (ASL7). Consequently
\[
 \mathcal H_{\mathcal U}=\mathcal H_c\oplus\mathcal H_{\rm esc},\quad
 \mathcal H_c=P\mathcal H_{\mathcal U},\quad
 \mathcal H_{\rm esc}=Q\mathcal H_{\mathcal U},\quad Q=I-P,
 \qquad T(t)=T_c(t)\oplus0\quad(t>0).
 \tag{ASL9}
\]
Precisely the vectors of \(\mathcal H_c\) satisfy \(T(t)v\to v\) as \(t\downarrow0\). For arbitrary \(v\), the exact missing squared norm is \(\|Qv\|^2\); moreover \(\lim_{t\downarrow0}\|T(t)v-v\|=\|Qv\|\). The full semigroup with its original value \([e^0]=I\) may be discontinuous at zero. We retain that fact and its complete defect space.

\subsection*{3. Generator, original spectral cutoffs and full escape identity}

Set \(R=[(I+A_j)^{-1}]\). The integral
\[
 R=\int_0^\infty e^{-t}T(t)\,dt
 \tag{ASL10}
\]
holds in operator norm. On any compact interval away from zero, (ASL7) gives uniform Riemann-sum convergence, also for the finite-volume semigroups. The omitted interval \((0,\epsilon)\) has norm at most \(\epsilon\), and the tail after \(b\) has norm at most \(e^{-b}\). Finite-volume spectral calculus gives (ASL10), then these bounds pass it to the ultraproduct. No interchange involving zero-time continuity is used.

The operator \(R\) is positive, \(0\le R\le I\), and \(\ker R=\mathcal H_{\rm esc}\). One inclusion follows from (ASL9). Conversely, \(Rv=0\) makes the nonnegative continuous integrand \(\langle v,T(t)v\rangle\) vanish for every \(t>0\); its limit is \(\|Pv\|^2\). On \(\mathcal H_c\), \(R_c\) is therefore injective and has dense range. Its bounded spectral measure defines
\[
 A=R_c^{-1}-I,\quad
 D(A)=\operatorname{ran}R_c
 =\left\{v:\int_{(0,1]}(r^{-1}-1)^2\,d\langle v,E_{R_c}(r)v\rangle<\infty\right\}.
 \tag{ASL11}
\]
The equality of the domain with \(\operatorname{ran}R_c\) follows because integrability of \((r^{-1}-1)^2\), together with \(\|v\|^2<\infty\), is equivalent to integrability of \(r^{-2}\). The spectral multiplication operator is self-adjoint and nonnegative. For \(t>0\), let \(g_t(0)=0\) and \(g_t(r)=\exp[-t(r^{-1}-1)]\) for \(0<r\le1\). This is continuous on \([0,1]\). Uniform polynomial approximation and preservation of products show \([g_t((I+A_j)^{-1})]=g_t(R)\). Hence
\[
 T(t)=e^{-tA}P,\qquad T_c(0)=I_{\mathcal H_c},\qquad
 A\Omega=0.
 \tag{ASL12}
\]
This reconstructs the generator itself. In particular, it is not an assumed limit of unbounded \(A_j\) on all of \(\mathcal H_{\mathcal U}\).

For every finite \(b\ge0\), keep the original spectral cutoff
\[
 E_b=[\mathbf1_{[0,b]}(A_j)].
 \tag{ASL13}
\]
It is an orthogonal projection, increases with \(b\), and satisfies
\[
 \|(I-T(t))E_b\|\le1-e^{-tb},\quad E_b\le P,\qquad
 \|(I-E_b)T(t)\|\le e^{-tb},\qquad
 \mathop{\rm s-lim}_{b\to\infty}E_b=P.
 \tag{ASL14}
\]
The first inequality proves \(E_b\mathcal H_{\mathcal U}\subset\mathcal H_c\). The second inequality shows that \(T(t)\mathcal H_{\mathcal U}\) belongs to the closure of the union of the cutoff ranges; their closed union therefore contains the strong limit \(P\mathcal H_{\mathcal U}\). This proves the last equality, not merely one inclusion. For every bounded original vector sequence,
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \|Q[v_j]\|^2=\lim_{b\to\infty}\lim_{\mathcal U}
       \|\mathbf1_{(b,\infty)}(A_j)v_j\|^2,
 \qquad
 \|P[v_j]\|^2=\lim_{b\to\infty}\lim_{\mathcal U}
       \|\mathbf1_{[0,b]}(A_j)v_j\|^2 .\quad}
 \tag{ASL15}
\]
The order of the two limits is part of the assertion. At each finite \(j\), all tails vanish as \(b\to\infty\); that reversed order would suppress the escape space and is not used.

The original cutoff need not be the reconstructed spectral projection at its endpoint. The exact comparison, on \(\mathcal H_c\), is
\[
 \mathbf1_{[0,b)}(A)\le E_b|_{\mathcal H_c}
 \le\mathbf1_{[0,b]}(A).
 \tag{ASL16}
\]
Indeed \(E_b\) commutes with \(R\), \(R\ge(1+b)^{-1}I\) on its range, and \(R\le(1+b)^{-1}I\) on its orthogonal complement. Functional calculus for \(R_c\) proves (ASL16). The entire possible discrepancy is a projection on the eigenspace \(\ker(A-b)\); no endpoint mass is dropped. For example the scalar sequences \(A_j=b+1/j\) and \(A_j=b-1/j\ge0\) both reconstruct the scalar \(b\), whereas their cutoffs at \(b\) are respectively zero and identity. At \(b=0\), the first example suffices.

If \(f\) is continuous on \([0,\infty)\) and vanishes at infinity, transform \(r=(1+\lambda)^{-1}\) to obtain
\[
 [f(A_j)]=f(A)P.
 \tag{ASL17}
\]
For bounded continuous \(f\) without a limit at infinity, the original bounded sequence still induces an operator. Its restriction to \(\mathcal H_c\) is \(f(A)\), by uniform approximation on each finite spectral interval and (ASL14); it preserves both subspaces because it commutes with every \(E_b\) and hence \(P\). Thus, in particular,
\[
 [e^{itA_j}]|_{\mathcal H_c}=e^{itA}\quad(t\in\mathbb R).
 \tag{ASL18}
\]
For precision in the approximation argument, multiply \(f\) by a continuous cutoff equal to one on \([0,b]\) and zero above \(b+1\); on \(E_b\) it agrees exactly with \(f\), while the error on \((P-E_b)v\) is at most \(2\|f\|_\infty\|(P-E_b)v\|\). This also proves (ASL18) without an assumption about the escaping unitary group.

Uniform form control is a sufficient property proved for specific vectors below: if \(\sup_jq_j(v_j)\le C\) and \(v_j\) is bounded, then
\[
 \|(I-E_b)[v_j]\|^2\le C/b\quad(b>0),\qquad
 [v_j]\in\mathcal H_c,\qquad
 \|A^{1/2}[v_j]\|^2\le\lim_{\mathcal U}q_j(v_j).
 \tag{ASL19}
\]
The tail inequality is \(\mathbf1_{(b,\infty)}(\lambda)\le\lambda/b\). For the form inequality use \((1-e^{-t\lambda})/t\le\lambda\), (ASL12), and monotone convergence as \(t\downarrow0\). A finite first-moment defect can remain even when the norm escape is zero; (ASL19) does not assert equality of energy moments.

\subsection*{4. Exact product defect for the full bounded original observable algebra}

Let \(\mathfrak M\) be the unital commutative star algebra of all sequences of essentially bounded gauge-invariant functions \(b_j\) on \(Q_j\) with \(\sup_j\|b_j\|_\infty<\infty\), modulo equality as induced ultraproduct multiplication operators. Write \(B=[M_{b_j}]\). Its operator-norm closure remains a commutative unital C*-algebra on \(\mathcal H_{\mathcal U}\). Compression gives
\[
 \Phi(B)=PBP|_{\mathcal H_c},\qquad
 \Phi(I)=I_{\mathcal H_c},\qquad
 \boxed{\Phi(BC)-\Phi(B)\Phi(C)=PBQCP|_{\mathcal H_c}.}
 \tag{ASL20}
\]
Insert \(I=P+Q\) between \(B\) and \(C\) to prove the identity. Compression is completely positive: for any finite positive operator matrix \([B_{ab}]\) and vectors \(v_a\in\mathcal H_c\), \(\sum_{a,b}\langle v_a,\Phi(B_{ab})v_b\rangle=\sum_{a,b}\langle v_a,B_{ab}v_b\rangle\ge0\). Its exact Schwarz defect is
\[
 \Phi(B^*B)-\Phi(B)^*\Phi(B)
       =(QBP)^*(QBP)\ge0.
 \tag{ASL21}
\]
Both Schwarz defects for \(B\) and \(B^*\) vanish exactly when \(QBP=QB^*P=0\), equivalently \(BP=PB\). On the algebra of such reducing operators, (ASL20) is multiplicative. For the original commuting \(B,C\), its compressed commutator is exactly
\[
 [\Phi(B),\Phi(C)]=P(CQB-BQC)P|_{\mathcal H_c}.
 \tag{ASL22}
\]
Thus every lost multiplication term has an identified path through the escape space.

\subsection*{5. The actual Wilson algebra reduces the continuous subspace}

Suppose a sequence of smooth gauge-invariant functions has uniform bounds \(\|b_j\|_\infty\le M\) and \(\|\nabla b_j\|_\infty\le D\), with the original full edge gradient. For complex \(f\), the complete product formula is
\[
\begin{aligned}
 q_j(b_j\psi_jf)=\kappa_*\int d\mu_j\bigl[
 |b_j|^2|\nabla f|^2+|f|^2|\nabla b_j|^2
 +2\operatorname{Re}\sum_{e,\alpha}
    \overline{b_jX_{e,\alpha}f}\,fX_{e,\alpha}b_j\bigr].
\end{aligned}\tag{ASL23}
\]
Every edge, coordinate and cross term is present. The elementary inequality \(|u+v|^2\le2|u|^2+2|v|^2\) then proves, without replacing the equality,
\[
 q_j(b_jv_j)\le2M^2q_j(v_j)+2\kappa_*D^2\|v_j\|^2.
 \tag{ASL24}
\]
The conjugate function has the same two bounds. For a bounded representative \(u_j\), the sequence \(v_j=\mathbf1_{[0,b]}(A_j)u_j\) has \(q_j(v_j)\le b\|u_j\|^2\). Equations (ASL19) and (ASL24) therefore show \(BE_b\mathcal H_{\mathcal U}\subset\mathcal H_c\). By (ASL14) and boundedness, \(B\mathcal H_c\subset\mathcal H_c\). Apply the same proof to \(B^*\) and take adjoints to obtain
\[
 BP=PB,\qquad \Phi(BC)=\Phi(B)\Phi(C)
 \quad\hbox{whenever either reducing factor supplies the required zero in (ASL20).}
 \tag{ASL25}
\]
In particular both the left and right multiplication identities hold for a reducing \(B\) and arbitrary \(C\). The form estimate constructs invariance; it is not a postulated algebraic property.

For an original face, \(|W_p|\le2\) and \(|\nabla_eW_p|\le1\) on each of its four distinct edges, and its other derivatives vanish. To prove the derivative constant, move the surrounding unitary matrices through the trace and, for inverse occurrences, differentiate \(U^{-1}\) with its minus sign. The resulting generator triple differs by an orthogonal adjoint rotation and possibly a common minus sign from \(\operatorname{Tr}(T_\alpha G)\). Write \(G=cI+2\sum x_\alpha T_\alpha\); then \(\operatorname{Tr}(T_\alpha G)=-x_\alpha\) and \(\sum x_\alpha^2=1-c^2\le1\). Thus, for any actual face sequence \(p_j\),
\[
 \|W_{p_j}\|_\infty\le2,\quad
